## Canadian Environmental Protection Act, 1999 {#canadian-environmental-protection-act-1999 .Title-of-Act}

[S.C.]{.abbr title="Statutes of Canada"} 1999, c. 33
:::

Assented to 1999-09-14

An Act respecting pollution prevention and the protection of the
environment and human health in order to contribute to sustainable
development
::::

::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::::: section
::: {.section .order}
###### Declaration {#preamble .MarginalNote}

It is hereby declared that the protection of the environment is
essential to the well-being of Canadians and that the primary purpose of
this Act is to contribute to sustainable development through pollution
prevention.

Preamble

Whereas the Government of Canada seeks to achieve sustainable
development that is based on an ecologically efficient use of natural,
social and economic resources and acknowledges the need to integrate
environmental, economic and social factors in the making of all
decisions by government and private entities;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes that every individual in
Canada has a right to a healthy environment as provided under this Act;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to implementing pollution
prevention as a national goal and as the priority approach to
environmental protection;

Whereas the Government of Canada acknowledges the need to control and
manage pollutants and wastes if their release into the environment
cannot be prevented;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of an
ecosystem approach;

Whereas the Government of Canada will continue to demonstrate national
leadership in establishing environmental standards, ecosystem objectives
and environmental quality guidelines and codes of practice;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to implementing the
precautionary principle that, where there are threats of serious or
irreversible damage, lack of full scientific certainty shall not be used
as a reason for postponing cost-effective measures to prevent
environmental degradation;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes that all governments in
Canada have authority that enables them to protect the environment and
recognizes that all governments face environmental problems that can
benefit from cooperative resolution;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of
endeavouring, in cooperation with provinces, territories and aboriginal
peoples, to achieve the highest level of environmental quality for all
Canadians and ultimately contribute to sustainable development;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to implementing the United
Nations Declaration on the Rights of Indigenous Peoples, including free,
prior and informed consent;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes that the risk of toxic
substances in the environment is a matter of national concern and that
toxic substances, once introduced into the environment, cannot always be
contained within geographic boundaries;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of
considering vulnerable populations in assessing whether substances are
toxic or capable of becoming toxic;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of minimizing
the risks posed by exposure to toxic substances and the cumulative
effects of toxic substances;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the integral role of
science, as well as the role of traditional aboriginal knowledge, in the
process of making decisions relating to the protection of the
environment and human health and that environmental or health risks and
social, economic and technical matters are to be considered in that
process;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the role of science and
Indigenous knowledge in the process of making decisions related to the
protection of the environment and human health, as well as the
importance of promoting the development and timely incorporation of
scientifically justified alternative methods and strategies in the
testing and assessment of substances to replace, reduce or refine the
use of vertebrate animals;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the responsibility of users
and producers in relation to toxic substances and pollutants and wastes,
and has adopted the "polluter pays" principle;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to ensuring that its
operations and activities on federal and aboriginal lands are carried
out in a manner that is consistent with the principles of pollution
prevention and the protection of the environment and human health;

Whereas the Government of Canada will endeavour to remove threats to
biological diversity through pollution prevention as well as the control
and management of the risk of any adverse effects of the use and release
of toxic substances, pollutants and wastes;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of
encouraging the progressive substitution of substances, processes and
technologies with alternatives that are safer for the environment or
human health, when they are economically and technically viable;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to openness, transparency
and accountability in respect of the protection of the environment and
human health;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the importance of Canadians
having information, including by means of the packaging and labelling of
products, regarding the risks posed by toxic substances to the
environment or to human health;

Whereas the Government of Canada is committed to implementing a
risk-based approach to the assessment and management of chemical
substances;

Whereas the Government of Canada recognizes the need to protect the
environment, including its biological diversity, and human health, by
ensuring the safe and effective use of biotechnology;

And whereas the Government of Canada must be able to fulfil its
international obligations in respect of the environment;
:::

:::: Enacts
Now, therefore, Her Majesty, by and with the advice and consent of the
Senate and House of Commons of Canada, enacts as follows:

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, Preamble
- 2023, c. 12, s. 2
:::
::::

## [Short Title]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63244 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Short title

**[[1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-1 .sectionLabel}** This Act may be cited as
the [Canadian Environmental Protection Act, 1999](/eng/acts/C-15.31).

## [Administrative Duties]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63247 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duties of the Government of Canada

- **[[2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In the
  administration of this Act, the Government of Canada shall, having
  regard to the Constitution and laws of Canada and subject to
  subsection (1.1),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} exercise its powers in a manner that

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} protects the environment and human health,
      including the health of vulnerable populations,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} applies the precautionary principle, which
      provides that the lack of full scientific certainty shall not be
      used as a reason for postponing cost-effective measures to prevent
      environmental degradation if there are threats of serious or
      irreversible damage, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} promotes and reinforces enforceable pollution
      prevention approaches;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} take preventive and remedial measures to protect,
    enhance and restore the environment;

  - [(a.2)]{.lawlabel} protect the right of every individual in Canada
    to a healthy environment as provided under this Act, subject to any
    reasonable limits;

  - [(a.3)]{.lawlabel} in relation to paragraph (a.2), uphold principles
    such as principles of environmental justice --- including the
    avoidance of adverse effects that disproportionately affect
    vulnerable populations --- the principle of non-regression and the
    principle of intergenerational equity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take the necessity of protecting the environment
    into account in making social and economic decisions;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} implement an ecosystem approach that considers the
    unique and fundamental characteristics of ecosystems;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} endeavour to act in cooperation with governments to
    protect the environment;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} encourage the participation of the people of Canada
    in the making of decisions that affect the environment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} facilitate the protection of the environment by the
    people of Canada;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} establish nationally consistent standards of
    environmental quality;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} provide information to the people of Canada on the
    state of the Canadian environment;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} apply knowledge, including traditional aboriginal
    knowledge, science and technology, to identify and resolve
    environmental problems;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} protect the environment, including its biological
    diversity, and human health, from the risk of any adverse effects of
    the use and release of toxic substances, pollutants and wastes;

  - [(j.1)]{.lawlabel} protect the environment, including its biological
    diversity, and human health, by ensuring the safe and effective use
    of biotechnology;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} endeavour to act expeditiously and diligently to
    assess whether existing substances or those new to Canada are toxic
    or capable of becoming toxic and assess the risk that such
    substances pose to the environment and human life and health;

  - [(k.1)]{.lawlabel} encourage the development and timely
    incorporation of scientifically justified alternative methods and
    strategies in the testing and assessment of substances to replace,
    reduce or refine the use of vertebrate animals;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} endeavour to act with regard to the intent of
    intergovernmental agreements and arrangements entered into for the
    purpose of achieving the highest level of environmental quality
    throughout Canada;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} ensure, to the extent that is reasonably possible,
    that all areas of federal regulation for the protection of the
    environment and human health are addressed in a complementary manner
    in order to avoid duplication and to provide effective and
    comprehensive protection;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} endeavour to exercise its powers to require the
    provision of information in a coordinated manner; and

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} apply and enforce this Act in a fair, predictable
    and consistent manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Considerations

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Government of Canada shall consider the
  following before taking any measure under paragraph (1)(a.1):

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the short- and long-term human and ecological
    benefits arising from the environmental protection measure;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the positive economic impacts arising from the
    measure, including those cost-savings arising from health,
    environmental and technological advances and innovation, among
    others; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any other benefits accruing from the measure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action not limited

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this section shall be construed so as to
  prevent the taking of any action to protect the environment or human
  health for the purposes of this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 2

## [Interpretation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63275 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[3]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-3 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  definitions in this subsection apply in this Act.

  [[aboriginal government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aboriginal government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a governing
      body that is established by or under or operating under an
      agreement between Her Majesty in right of Canada and aboriginal
      people and that is empowered to enact laws respecting

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the protection of the environment; or

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of Division 5 of Part 7, the
        registration of vehicles or engines. ([gouvernement
        autochtone]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[aboriginal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aboriginal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} reserves, surrendered lands and any other lands
        that are set apart for the use and benefit of a band and that
        are subject to the [Indian Act](/eng/acts/I-5);

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} land, including any water, that is subject to a
        comprehensive or specific claim agreement, or a self-government
        agreement, between the Government of Canada and aboriginal
        people where title remains with Her Majesty in right of Canada;
        and

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} air and all layers of the atmosphere above and
        the subsurface below land mentioned in paragraph (a) or
        (b). ([terres autochtones]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[air pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[air pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a condition of the
      air, arising wholly or partly from the presence in the air of any
      substance, that directly or indirectly

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} endangers the health, safety or welfare of
        humans;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} interferes with the normal enjoyment of life or
        property;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} endangers the health of animal life;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} causes damage to plant life or to property; or

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} degrades or alters, or forms part of a process
        of degradation or alteration of, an ecosystem to an extent that
        is detrimental to its use by humans, animals or
        plants. ([pollution atmosphérique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[analyst]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[analyst]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person or a member of a
      class of persons designated as an analyst under subsection
      217(1). ([analyste]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[biological diversity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[biological diversity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the variability
      among living organisms from all sources, including, without
      limiting the generality of the foregoing, terrestrial and marine
      and other aquatic ecosystems and the ecological complexes of which
      they form a part and includes the diversity within and between
      species and of ecosystems. ([diversité
      biologique]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[biotechnology]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[biotechnology]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the application of
      science and engineering in the direct or indirect use of living
      organisms or parts or products of living organisms in their
      natural or modified forms. ([biotechnologie]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[class of substances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[class of substances]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any two or more
      substances that

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} contain the same portion of chemical structure;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} have similar physico-chemical or toxicological
        properties; or

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of sections 68, 70 and 71,
        have similar types of use. ([catégorie de
        substances]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Committee]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the National Advisory
      Committee established under section 6. ([comité]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[ecosystem]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ecosystem]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a dynamic complex of
      plant, animal and micro-organism communities and their non-living
      environment interacting as a functional
      unit. ([écosystème]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[enforcement officer]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person or a
      member of a class of persons designated as an enforcement officer
      under section 217. ([agent de l'autorité]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the components of the
      Earth and includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} air, land and water;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} all layers of the atmosphere;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} all organic and inorganic matter and living
        organisms; and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the interacting natural systems that include
        components referred to in paragraphs (a) to
        (c). ([environnement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[environmental emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[environmental emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning
      given that expression in Part 8. ([urgence
      environnementale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[environmental quality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[environmental quality]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes the health
      of ecosystems. ([qualité de l'environnement]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Environmental Registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Environmental Registry]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the registry
      established under section 12. ([Registre]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[federal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[federal land]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} land, including any water, that belongs to Her
        Majesty in right of Canada, or that Her Majesty in right of
        Canada has the right to dispose of, and the air and all layers
        of the atmosphere above and the subsurface below that land; and

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the following land and areas, namely,

        - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the internal waters of Canada as determined
          under the [Oceans Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4), including the seabed
          and subsoil below and the airspace above those waters, and

        - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the territorial sea of Canada as determined
          under the [Oceans Act](/eng/acts/O-2.4), including the seabed
          and subsoil below and the air and all layers of the atmosphere
          above that sea. ([territoire domanial]{.DefinedTermLink
          lang="fr"})

  [[federal source]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[federal source]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a department of the Government of Canada;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an agency of the Government of Canada or other
        body established by or under an Act of Parliament that is
        ultimately accountable through a minister of the Crown in right
        of Canada to Parliament for the conduct of its affairs;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a Crown corporation as defined in subsection
        83(1) of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11); or

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a federal work or undertaking. ([source
        d'origine fédérale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[federal work or undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[federal work or undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any work
      or undertaking that is within the legislative authority of
      Parliament, including

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking operated for or in
        connection with navigation and shipping, whether inland or
        maritime, including the operation of ships and transportation by
        ship;

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a railway, canal, telegraph or other work or
        undertaking connecting one province with another, or extending
        beyond the limits of a province;

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a line of ships connecting a province with any
        other province, or extending beyond the limits of a province;

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} a ferry between any province and any other
        province or between any province and any country other than
        Canada;

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} airports, aircraft and commercial air services;

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a broadcast undertaking;

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} a bank or an authorized foreign bank within the
        meaning of section 2 of the [Bank Act](/eng/acts/B-1.01);

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking that, although wholly
        situated within a province, is before or after its completion
        declared by Parliament to be for the general advantage of Canada
        or for the advantage of two or more provinces; and

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a work or undertaking outside the exclusive
        legislative authority of the legislatures of the
        provinces. ([entreprises fédérales]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[fuel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[fuel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any form of matter that is
      combusted or oxidized for the generation of
      energy. ([combustible]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[government]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the government of a
      province or of a territory or an aboriginal
      government. ([gouvernement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[healthy environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[healthy environment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an environment
      that is clean, healthy and sustainable. ([environnement
      sain]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Minister of the
      Environment. ([ministre]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[movement within Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[movement within Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} or [[transport
      within Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means movement or transport
      between provinces. ([mouvement au Canada]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"} ou [transport au Canada]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[pollution prevention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[pollution prevention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the use of
      processes, practices, materials, products, substances or energy
      that avoid or minimize the creation of pollutants and waste and
      reduce the overall risk to the environment or human
      health. ([prévention de la pollution]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[precautionary principle]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[precautionary principle]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Principle 15
      of the 1992 Rio Declaration on Environment and Development, which
      provides that the lack of full scientific certainty shall not be
      used as a reason for postponing cost-effective measures to prevent
      environmental degradation if there are threats of serious or
      irreversible damage. ([principe de précaution]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[prescribed]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means prescribed by regulations
      made under this Act. ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[province]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a
      territory. ([province]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[release]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes discharge, spray, inject,
      inoculate, abandon, deposit, spill, leak, seep, pour, emit, empty,
      throw, dump, place and exhaust. ([rejet]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sell]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes to offer for sale or lease,
      have in possession for sale or lease or deliver for sale or
      lease. ([vente]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any distinguishable kind
      of organic or inorganic matter, whether animate or inanimate, and
      includes

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any matter that is capable of being dispersed
        in the environment or of being transformed in the environment
        into matter that is capable of being so dispersed or that is
        capable of causing such transformations in the environment,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any element or free radical,

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any combination of elements of a particular
        molecular identity that occurs in nature or as a result of a
        chemical reaction, and

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} complex combinations of different molecules
        that originate in nature or are the result of chemical reactions
        but that could not practicably be formed by simply combining
        individual constituents,

      and, except for the purposes of sections 66 to 66.2, 80 to 89 and
      104 to 115, includes

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any mixture that is a combination of substances
        and does not itself produce a substance that is different from
        the substances that were combined,

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any manufactured item that is formed into a
        specific physical shape or design during manufacture and has,
        for its final use, a function or functions dependent in whole or
        in part on its shape or design, and

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any animate matter that is, or any complex
        mixtures of different molecules that are, contained in
        effluents, emissions or wastes that result from any work,
        undertaking or activity. ([substance]{.DefinedTermLink
        lang="fr"})

  [[sustainable development]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[sustainable development]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means development
      that meets the needs of the present without compromising the
      ability of future generations to meet their own
      needs. ([développement durable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[transient reaction intermediate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[transient reaction intermediate]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
      substance that is formed and consumed in the course of a chemical
      reaction. ([intermédiaire de réaction]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[transit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[transit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, except for the purposes of
      sections 139 and 155, the portion of an international
      transboundary movement of waste or material referred to in
      subsection 185(1) through the territory of a country that is
      neither the country of origin nor the country of destination of
      the movement. ([transit]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[undertaking]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a business. ([Version
      anglaise seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[vulnerable population]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[vulnerable population]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a group of
      individuals within the Canadian population who, due to greater
      susceptibility or greater exposure, may be at an increased risk of
      experiencing adverse health effects from exposure to
      substances. ([population vulnérable]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Meaning of
  [[Ministers]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} and [[either
  Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the word [[Ministers]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} is
  used in this Act, it refers to both the Minister and the Minister of
  Health, and where the expression [[either
  Minister]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} is used in this Act, it refers to
  either the Minister or the Minister of Health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class of substances

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act, other than subsection
  (1), [[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a class of substances.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 28, s. 151, c. 33, s. 3

## [Her Majesty]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63360 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Binding on Her Majesty

**[[5]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5 .sectionLabel}** This Act is binding on Her
Majesty in right of Canada or a province.

## [PART 1]{.HLabel1}[Administration]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63363 .Part}

### [Implementation of Right to a Healthy Environment]{.HTitleText2} {#h-1404081 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Implementation framework

- **[[5.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-5.1 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purposes of paragraph 2(1)(a.2), the Ministers shall, within two
  years after the day on which this section comes into force, develop an
  implementation framework to set out how the right to a healthy
  environment will be considered in the administration of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right to a healthy environment

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} Without limiting the generality of subsection (1),
  the implementation framework shall set out

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the process under subsection 76.1(1) in respect of
    the protection of the right to a healthy environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The implementation framework, in a manner consistent
  with the purposes of this Act, shall, among other things, elaborate on

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the principles to be considered in the
    administration of this Act, such as principles of environmental
    justice --- including the avoidance of adverse effects that
    disproportionately affect vulnerable populations --- the principle
    of non-regression and the principle of intergenerational equity,
    according to which it is important to meet the needs of the present
    generation without compromising the ability of future generations to
    meet their own needs;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} research, studies or monitoring activities to
    support the protection of the right to a healthy environment
    referred to in paragraph 2(1)(a.2);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the relevant factors to be taken into account in
    interpreting and applying that right and in determining the
    reasonable limits to which it is subject, including social, health,
    scientific and economic factors; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} mechanisms to support the protection of that right.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In developing the implementation framework, the
  Ministers shall consult any interested persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish the implementation
  framework in the manner that the Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall include in the annual report
  required by section 342 a report on the implementation of the
  framework.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 5](#1402871-1404101){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402871-1404101.html"
  role="button"}
:::

### [Advisory Committees]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63364 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National Advisory Committee

- **[[6]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-6 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the
  purpose of enabling national action to be carried out and taking
  cooperative action in matters affecting the environment and for the
  purpose of avoiding duplication in regulatory activity among
  governments, the Minister shall establish a National Advisory
  Committee

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to advise the Ministers on regulations proposed to
    be made under subsection 93(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to advise the Minister on a cooperative,
    coordinated intergovernmental approach for the management of toxic
    substances; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to advise the Minister on other environmental
    matters that are of mutual interest to the Government of Canada and
    other governments and to which this Act relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Precautionary principle

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} In giving its advice and recommendations, the
  Committee shall use the precautionary principle.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Composition of Committee

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Committee shall consist of the following members:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} one representative for each of the Ministers;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} one representative of the government of each of the
    provinces; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (3), not more than six
    representatives of aboriginal governments, to be selected on the
    following regional basis,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all aboriginal
      governments, except Inuit, in Newfoundland and Labrador, Prince
      Edward Island, Nova Scotia and New Brunswick,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all aboriginal
      governments, except Inuit, in Quebec,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all aboriginal
      governments, except Inuit, in Ontario,

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all aboriginal
      governments, except Inuit, in Manitoba, Saskatchewan, Alberta, the
      Northwest Territories and Nunavut,

    - [(v)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all aboriginal
      governments, except Inuit, in British Columbia and Yukon, and

    - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} one representative for all Inuit aboriginal
      governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Provincial representatives

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} The representative of a provincial government shall
  be selected by that government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Aboriginal representatives

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the representative of
  aboriginal governments shall be selected by the aboriginal governments
  he or she represents.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inuit aboriginal representatives

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), the representative of
  Inuit aboriginal governments shall be selected by those governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Absence of aboriginal government

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where there is no Inuit aboriginal government or
  aboriginal government for a region referred to in any of subparagraphs
  (2)(c)(i) to (v), the representative of the Inuit or of aboriginal
  people for the region, as the case may be, may be selected in
  accordance with regulations made under subsection (4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may make regulations respecting the
  manner of selecting a representative under subsection (3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 6
- 2002, c. 7, s. 124
- 2015, c. 3, s. 172
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial advisory committees

- **[[7]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-7 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the
  purpose of carrying out their duties under this Act, the Ministers or
  either Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish advisory committees to report to the
    Ministers or either Minister; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specify the functions that the committees are to
    perform and the manner in which those functions are to be performed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The report of a committee established under
  subsection (1), including its recommendations and reasons, shall be
  made public.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report of various committees

**[[8]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-8 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall include
in the annual report required by section 342 a report of the activities
of the Committee and of any committees established under paragraph
7(1)(a).

### [Agreements Respecting Administration]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63405 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negotiation of agreement

- **[[9]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-9 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may negotiate an agreement with a government or with an
  aboriginal people with respect to the administration of this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of negotiated agreements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish any agreement negotiated
  under subsection (1) before it is entered into, or give notice of its
  availability, in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and
  in any other manner that the Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments or objections

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of an agreement
  or notice of its availability under subsection (2), any person may
  file with the Minister comments or a notice of objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication by Minister of results

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} After the end of the period of 60 days referred to in
  subsection (3), the Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a report or a notice of the
  availability of a report that summarizes how any comments or notices
  of objection were dealt with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Entering into agreements

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, after publishing a report or notice
  under subsection (4),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with the approval of the Governor in Council, enter
    into an agreement with a government or an aboriginal people with
    respect to the administration of this Act; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to any terms and conditions that the
    Governor in Council may specify in the approval, agree to amendments
    of the agreement.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of final agreements

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish any agreement under
  subsection (5), or give notice of its availability, in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made under subsection (5) terminates
  five years after the date on which it comes into force or may be
  terminated earlier by either party giving the other at least three
  months notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Annual report

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall include in the annual report
  required by section 342 a report on the administration of this Act
  under agreements made under subsection (5).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action not limited by Agreement

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} No agreement made under this section shall limit or
  restrict the carrying out of any action the Minister deems necessary
  for the administration and enforcement of this Act, including the
  conduct of inspections or investigations.

### [Agreements Respecting Equivalent Provisions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63427 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of regulations

- **[[10]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-10 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Except
  with respect to Her Majesty in right of Canada, the provisions of a
  regulation made under subsection 93(1), 200(1) or 209(1) or (2) do not
  apply within the jurisdiction of a government for which there is in
  force an order, made under subsection (3), declaring that the
  provisions do not apply within that jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except with respect to a federal source, the
  provisions of a regulation made under section 167 or 177 do not apply
  within the jurisdiction of a government for which there is in force an
  order, made under subsection (3), declaring that the provisions do not
  apply within that jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of equivalent provisions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (4), (5) and (6), where the
  Minister and a government agree in writing that there are in force by
  or under the laws applicable to the jurisdiction of the government

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} provisions that are equivalent to a regulation made
    under a provision referred to in subsection (1) or (2), and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} provisions that are similar to sections 17 to 20
    for the investigation of alleged offences under environmental
    legislation of that jurisdiction,

  the Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister,
  make an order declaring that the provisions of the regulation do not
  apply in an area under the jurisdiction of the government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of agreements

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish any agreement referred to
  in subsection (3) before it is entered into, or give notice of its
  availability, in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and
  in any other manner that the Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments or objections

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of an agreement
  or notice of its availability under subsection (4), any person may
  file with the Minister comments or a notice of objection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication by Minister of results

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} After the end of the period of 60 days referred to in
  subsection (5), the Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a report or a notice of the
  availability of a report that summarizes how any comments or notices
  of objection were dealt with.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of final agreements

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish any agreement referred to
  in subsection (3) after it is entered into, or give notice of its
  availability, in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and
  in any other manner that the Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} An agreement made under subsection (3) terminates
  five years after the date on which it comes into force or may be
  terminated earlier by either party giving the other at least three
  months notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of order

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister, revoke an order made under subsection (3) if the
  agreement referred to in that subsection terminates or is terminated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall include in the annual report
  required by section 342 a report on the administration of this
  section.

## [PART 2]{.HLabel1}[Public Participation]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63452 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63453 .Subheading}

Definition of [[environmental protection action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[11]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-11 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[environmental protection action]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an action
under section 22.

### [Environmental Registry]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63456 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Establishment of Environmental Registry

**[[12]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-12 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
establish a registry, to be called the Environmental Registry, for the
purpose of facilitating access to documents relating to matters under
this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of Environmental Registry

- **[[13]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-13 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Environmental Registry shall contain notices and other documents
  published or made publicly available by the Ministers or either
  Minister under this Act, and shall also include, subject to the
  [Access to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1) and the [Privacy
  Act](/eng/acts/P-21),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notices of objection and of any approval granted
    under this Act;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a copy of every policy and of every proposed
    regulation or order made under this Act; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} copies of documents submitted to a court by the
    Minister relating to any environmental protection action.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form and manner of Environmental
  Registry

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may determine the form of the
  Environmental Registry and how it is to be kept, so long as the
  registry is publicly accessible and searchable and is in electronic
  form.

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection from civil proceeding or
prosecution

- **[[14]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-14
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament,
  no civil or criminal proceedings may be brought against any person
  mentioned in subsection (2) for the full or partial disclosure in good
  faith of any notice or other document through the Environmental
  Registry or any consequences of its disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Persons protected

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The persons against whom the proceedings may not be
  brought are Her Majesty in right of Canada, the Minister and any
  person acting on behalf of or under the direction of the Minister.

### [Rights under Other Parts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63472 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional rights

**[[15]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-15 .sectionLabel}** The rights conferred by
this Part are in addition to the right to request, under section 76,
that a substance be assessed, the right to file a notice of objection
under Parts 1, 7 and 11 and the right to request under Parts 7 and 11
that a board of review be established under section 333.
