### [Voluntary Reports]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63475 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary reports

- **[[16]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-16 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where
  a person has knowledge of the commission or reasonable likelihood of
  the commission of an offence under this Act, but is not required to
  report the matter under this Act, the person may report any
  information relating to the offence or likely offence to an
  enforcement officer or any person to whom a report may be made under
  this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person making the report may request that their
  identity, and any information that could reasonably be expected to
  reveal their identity, not be disclosed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for confidentiality

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall disclose or cause to be disclosed the
  identity of a person who makes a request under subsection (2) or any
  information that could reasonably be expected to reveal their identity
  unless the person authorizes the disclosure in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee protection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament, no employer
  shall dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or otherwise
  disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of employment,
  by reason that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee has made a report under subsection
    (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has refused or stated an intention of refusing
    to do anything that is an offence under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has done or stated an intention of doing
    anything that is required to be done by or under this Act.

### [Investigation of Offences]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63488 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for investigation by Minister

- **[[17]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-17 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who is resident in Canada and at least 18 years of age may
  apply to the Minister for an investigation of any offence under this
  Act that the individual alleges has occurred.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Statement to accompany application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application shall include a solemn affirmation or
  declaration

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} stating the name and address of the applicant;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} stating that the applicant is at least 18 years old
    and a resident of Canada;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} stating the nature of the alleged offence and the
    name of each person alleged to have contravened, or to have done
    something in contravention of, this Act or the regulations; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} containing a concise statement of the evidence
    supporting the allegations of the applicant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may prescribe the form in which an
  application under this section is required to be made.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Investigation by Minister

**[[18]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-18 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
acknowledge receipt of the application within 20 days of the receipt and
shall investigate all matters that the Minister considers necessary to
determine the facts relating to the alleged offence.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progress reports

**[[19]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-19 .sectionLabel}** After acknowledging
receipt of the application, the Minister shall report to the applicant
every 90 days on the progress of the investigation and the action, if
any, that the Minister has taken or proposes to take, and the Minister
shall include in the report an estimate of the time required to complete
the investigation or to implement the action, but a report is not
required if the investigation is discontinued before the end of the 90
days.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may send evidence to Attorney
General of Canada

**[[20]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-20 .sectionLabel}** At any stage of an
investigation, the Minister may send any documents or other evidence to
the Attorney General of Canada for consideration of whether an offence
has been or is about to be committed under this Act and for any action
that the Attorney General may wish to take.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Discontinuation of investigation

- **[[21]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-21 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may discontinue the investigation if the Minister is of the
  opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the alleged offence does not require further
    investigation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the investigation does not substantiate the alleged
    offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the investigation is discontinued, the Minister
  shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prepare a report in writing describing the
    information obtained during the investigation and stating the
    reasons for its discontinuation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} send a copy of the report to the applicant and to
    any person whose conduct was investigated.

  A copy of the report sent to a person whose conduct was investigated
  must not disclose the name or address of the applicant or any other
  personal information about them.

### [Environmental Protection Action]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63516 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Circumstances when an individual may
bring an action

- **[[22]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-22 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  individual who has applied for an investigation may bring an
  environmental protection action if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister failed to conduct an investigation and
    report within a reasonable time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister's response to the investigation was
    unreasonable.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nature of the action

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The action may be brought in any court of competent
  jurisdiction against a person who committed an offence under this Act
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was alleged in the application for the
    investigation; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} caused significant harm to the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Relief that may be claimed

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In the action, the individual may claim any or all of
  the following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a declaratory order;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an order, including an interlocutory order,
    requiring the defendant to refrain from doing anything that, in the
    opinion of the court, may constitute an offence under this Act;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} an order, including an interlocutory order,
    requiring the defendant to do anything that, in the opinion of the
    court, may prevent the continuation of an offence under this Act;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} an order to the parties to negotiate a plan to
    correct or mitigate the harm to the environment or to human, animal
    or plant life or health, and to report to the court on the
    negotiations within a time set by the court; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} any other appropriate relief, including the costs
    of the action, but not including damages.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period of two years

- **[[23]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-23 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} An
  environmental protection action may be brought only within a
  limitation period of two years beginning when the plaintiff becomes
  aware of the conduct on which the action is based, or should have
  become aware of it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time during investigation not included

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The limitation period does not include any time
  following the plaintiff's application for an investigation, but before
  the plaintiff receives a report under subsection 21(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}No action for remedial conduct

**[[24]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-24 .sectionLabel}** An environmental
protection action may not be brought if the alleged conduct

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} was taken

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to correct or mitigate harm or the risk of harm to
    the environment or to human, animal or plant life or health, or

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to protect national security, support humanitarian
    relief efforts, participate in multilateral military or
    peace-keeping activities under the auspices of international
    organizations or defend a member state of the North Atlantic Treaty
    Organization; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} was reasonable and consistent with public safety.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

**[[25]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-25 .sectionLabel}** An environmental
protection action may not be brought against a person if the person was
convicted of an offence under this Act, or environmental protection
alternative measures within the meaning of Part 10 were used to deal
with the person, in respect of the alleged conduct on which the action
is based.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of the action

- **[[26]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-26 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  plaintiff in an environmental protection action shall give notice of
  the action to the Minister no later than 10 days after the document
  originating the action is first served on a defendant, and the
  Minister shall give notice of the action in the Environmental Registry
  as soon as possible after receipt of the plaintiff's notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of other matters

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In an environmental protection action,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the court may order any party to the action to give
    notice to the Minister of any matter relating to the action, within
    the time specified by the court; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister shall give notice of that matter in
    the Environmental Registry as soon as possible after receipt of the
    party's notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attorney General to be served

- **[[27]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-27 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  plaintiff shall serve the Attorney General of Canada with a copy of
  the document originating an environmental protection action within 20
  days after first serving the document on a defendant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Attorney General may participate

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Attorney General of Canada is entitled to
  participate in the action, either as a party or otherwise. Notice of
  his or her decision to participate shall be given to the plaintiff and
  be included in the Environmental Registry within 45 days after the
  copy of the originating document is served on the Attorney General.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Right of appeal

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Attorney General of Canada is entitled to appeal
  from a judgment in the action and to make submissions and present
  evidence in an appeal.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other participants

- **[[28]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-28 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court may allow any person to participate in an environmental
  protection action in order to provide fair and adequate representation
  of the private and public interests involved.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner and terms of participation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court may determine the manner and terms of the
  person's participation, including the payment of costs.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Burden of proof

**[[29]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-29 .sectionLabel}** The offence alleged in an
environmental protection action and the resulting significant harm are
to be proved on a balance of probabilities.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Defences

- **[[30]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-30 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  following defences are available in an environmental protection
  action:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the defence of due diligence in complying with this
    Act and the regulations;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the defence that the alleged conduct is authorized
    by or under an Act of Parliament;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} except with respect to Her Majesty in right of
    Canada or a federal source, the defence that the alleged conduct is
    authorized by or under a law of a government that is the subject of
    an order made under subsection 10(3); and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the defence of officially induced mistake of law.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other defences not excluded

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit the availability of any
  other defences.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Undertakings to pay damages

**[[31]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-31 .sectionLabel}** In deciding whether to
dispense with an undertaking to pay damages caused by an interlocutory
order in an environmental protection action, the court may consider any
special circumstances, including whether the action is a test case or
raises a novel point of law.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stay or dismissal

- **[[32]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-32 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court may stay or dismiss an environmental protection action if it is
  in the public interest to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors to be considered

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In deciding whether to stay or dismiss the action,
  the court may consider

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} environmental, health, safety, economic and social
    concerns;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} whether the issues raised in the action would be
    better resolved in some other way;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} whether the Minister has an adequate plan to
    correct or mitigate the harm to the environment or human, animal or
    plant life or health or otherwise to address the issues raised in
    the action; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other relevant matter.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedies

**[[33]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-33 .sectionLabel}** If a court finds that the
plaintiff is entitled to judgment in an environmental protection action,
it may grant any relief mentioned in subsection 22(3).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Orders to negotiate plans

- **[[34]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-34 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  court order to negotiate a plan to correct or mitigate the harm to the
  environment or human, animal or plant life or health may, to the
  extent that it is reasonable, practicable and ecologically sound,
  require the plan to provide for

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the prevention, reduction or elimination of the
    harm;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the restoration of the environment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the restoration of all uses, including enjoyment,
    of the environment affected by the offence;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the payment of money by the defendant as the court
    may direct to achieve the plan's purposes; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} monitoring the implementation of the plan and the
    progress made in achieving its purposes.

  Before making the order, the court must take into account any efforts
  that the defendant has already made to deal with the harm.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other orders

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The court may also make interlocutory or ancillary
  orders to ensure that the negotiation of the plan runs smoothly,
  including orders

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the payment of the costs of negotiation;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} requiring the plaintiff or the defendant to prepare
    a draft of the plan; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} setting a time limit for the negotiations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Appointment of other person to prepare
  plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The court may appoint a person who is not a party to
  prepare a draft plan if the parties cannot agree on the plan or the
  court is not satisfied with the plan that they negotiate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Order to prepare another plan

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The court may order the parties to prepare another
  plan if it is not satisfied with the plan that they negotiate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval and effective date

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The court may approve a plan that the parties
  negotiate or a plan prepared by a person appointed under
  subsection (3) and the approved plan comes into effect on a day
  determined by the court.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction on orders to negotiate plans

**[[35]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-35 .sectionLabel}** A court may not order the
negotiation of a plan to correct or mitigate the harm to the environment
or human, animal or plant life or health if it determines that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the harm has already been corrected or mitigated; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} adequate measures to correct or mitigate the harm
  have already been ordered under this Act or any other law in force in
  Canada.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Settlement or discontinuance

**[[36]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-36 .sectionLabel}** An environmental
protection action may be settled or discontinued only with the approval
of the court and on terms that it considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Settlements and orders

**[[37]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-37 .sectionLabel}** If an environmental
protection action results in an order of a court or a settlement
approved by a court,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the resolution of any question of fact by the order
  or settlement is binding on a court in any other environmental
  protection action in which that question arises; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} no other environmental protection action may be
  brought with respect to the offence or alleged offence dealt with by
  the order or settlement.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs

**[[38]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-38 .sectionLabel}** In deciding whether to
award costs in an environmental protection action, the court may
consider any special circumstances, including whether the action is a
test case or raises a novel point of law.

### [Action to Prevent or Compensate Loss]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63621 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Injunction

**[[39]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-39 .sectionLabel}** Any person who suffers,
or is about to suffer, loss or damage as a result of conduct that
contravenes any provision of this Act or the regulations may seek an
injunction from a court of competent jurisdiction ordering the person
engaging in the conduct

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} to refrain from doing anything that it appears to the
  court causes or will cause the loss or damage; or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} to do anything that it appears to the court prevents
  or will prevent the loss or damage.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil cause of action

**[[40]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-40 .sectionLabel}** Any person who has
suffered loss or damage as a result of conduct that contravenes any
provision of this Act or the regulations may, in any court of competent
jurisdiction, bring an action to recover from the person who engaged in
the conduct

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} an amount equal to the loss or damage proved to have
  been suffered by the person; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} an amount to compensate for the costs that the person
  incurs in connection with the matter and proceedings under this
  section.

### [Other Matters]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63630 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence of offence

- **[[41]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-41 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In an
  action under this Part, the record of proceedings in any court in
  which a defendant was convicted of an offence under this Act is
  evidence that the defendant committed the offence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certificate evidence of conviction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In the action, evidence that a defendant was
  convicted of an offence under this Act may be given by a certificate
  stating with reasonable particularity the conviction and sentence of
  the defendant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Signature of certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The certificate shall be signed by

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person who made the conviction; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the clerk of the court in which the conviction was
    made.

  Once it is proved that the defendant is the offender mentioned in the
  certificate, it is evidence without proof of the signature or the
  official character of the person appearing to have signed it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Civil remedies not affected

- **[[42]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-42 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  civil remedy for any conduct is suspended or affected by reason only
  that the conduct is an offence under this Act.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedies not repealed, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Act shall be interpreted so as to
  repeal, remove or reduce any remedy available to any person under any
  law in force in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Damages caused by a ship

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No claim for damage caused by a ship may be made
  under this Act to the extent that a claim for that damage may be made
  under the [Marine Liability Act](/eng/acts/M-0.7) or the [Arctic
  Waters Pollution Prevention Act](/eng/acts/A-12).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 42
- 2001, c. 6, s. 112
:::

## [PART 3]{.HLabel1}[Information Gathering, Objectives, Guidelines and Codes of Practice]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63650 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63651 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[43]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-43 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[fish]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[fish]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the meaning assigned by section 2
    of the [Fisheries Act](/eng/acts/F-14). ([poissons]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[hormone disrupting substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[hormone disrupting substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a
    substance having the ability to disrupt the synthesis, secretion,
    transport, binding, action or elimination of natural hormones in an
    organism, or its progeny, that are responsible for the maintenance
    of homeostasis, reproduction, development or behaviour of the
    organism. ([substance hormonoperturbante]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

### [Environmental Data and Research]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63656 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring, research and publication

- **[[44]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-44 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establish, operate and maintain a system for
    monitoring environmental quality;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct research and studies relating to pollution
    prevention, the nature, transportation, dispersion, effects, control
    and abatement of pollution and the effects of pollution on
    environmental quality, and provide advisory and technical services
    and information related to that research and those studies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} conduct research and studies relating to

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} environmental contamination arising from
      disturbances of ecosystems by human activity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} changes in the normal geochemical cycling of
      toxic substances that are naturally present in the environment,
      and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} detection and damage to ecosystems;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} collect, process, correlate, interpret, create an
    inventory of and publish on a periodic basis data on environmental
    quality in Canada from monitoring systems, research, studies and any
    other sources;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} formulate plans for pollution prevention and the
    control and abatement of pollution, including plans respecting the
    prevention of, preparedness for and response to an environmental
    emergency and for restoring any part of the environment damaged by
    or during an emergency, and establish, operate and publicize
    demonstration projects and make them available for demonstration;
    and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} publish, arrange for the publication of or
    distribute through an information clearing-house

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} information respecting pollution prevention,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} pertinent information in respect of all aspects
      of environmental quality, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a periodic report on the state of the Canadian
      environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation and agreements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in establishing a system referred to in paragraph
    (1)(a), cooperate with governments, foreign governments and
    aboriginal people and with any person who has established or
    proposes to establish any such system; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the approval of the Governor in Council, enter
    into agreements for the operation or maintenance of a system
    referred to in paragraph (1)(a) by the Minister on behalf of any
    government, aboriginal people or any person or for the operation or
    maintenance of any such system by the government or any person on
    behalf of the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation with other bodies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in exercising the powers conferred
  by paragraphs (1)(b) to (e), act in cooperation with any government,
  foreign government, government department or agency, institution,
  aboriginal people or any person and may sponsor or assist in any of
  their research, studies, planning or activities in relation to
  environmental quality, pollution prevention, environmental emergencies
  or the control or abatement of pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Protection of right to healthy
  environment

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall conduct research, studies or
  monitoring activities to support the Government of Canada in
  protecting the right to a healthy environment referred to in paragraph
  2(1)(a.2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Hormone disrupting substances

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall conduct research or studies
  relating to hormone disrupting substances, methods related to their
  detection, methods to determine their actual or likely short-term or
  long-term effect on the environment and human health, and preventive,
  control and abatement measures to deal with those substances to
  protect the environment and human health.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 44
- 2017, c. 26, s. 22(E)
- [2023, c. 12, s. 7](#1402880-1404115){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402880-1404115.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-44-20171212.html "Link to previous version of section 44"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Role of Minister of Health

- **[[45]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-45 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister of Health shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct research and studies, including
    biomonitoring surveys, relating to the role of substances in
    illnesses or in health problems;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} collect, process, correlate and publish on a
    periodic basis data from any research or studies done under
    paragraph (a); and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} distribute available information to inform the
    public about the effects of substances on human health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vulnerable populations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, the research and studies
  referred to in paragraph (1)(a) may relate to vulnerable populations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 45
- [2023, c. 12, s. 8](#1402882-1404127){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402882-1404127.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-45-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 45"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Information Gathering]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63687 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice requiring information

- **[[46]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-46 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, for the purpose of conducting research, creating an
  inventory of data, formulating objectives and codes of practice,
  issuing guidelines or assessing or reporting on the state of the
  environment, publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person described
  in the notice to provide the Minister with any information that may be
  in the possession of that person or to which the person may reasonably
  be expected to have access, including information regarding the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} substances specified in the plan developed under
    section 73;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} substances that have not been determined to be
    toxic under Part 5 because of the current extent of the
    environment's exposure to them, but whose presence in the
    environment must be monitored if the Minister considers that to be
    appropriate;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} substances, including nutrients, that can be
    released into water or are present in products like water
    conditioners and cleaning products;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} substances released, or disposed of, at or into the
    sea;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} substances that are toxic under section 64 or that
    may become toxic;

  - [(e.1)]{.lawlabel} products that contain a substance that is toxic
    under section 64 or that may become toxic, or products that may
    release such a substance into the environment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} substances that may cause or contribute to
    international or interprovincial pollution of fresh water, salt
    water or the atmosphere;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} substances or fuels that may contribute
    significantly to air pollution;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} substances that, if released into Canadian waters,
    cause or may cause damage to fish or to their habitat;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} substances that, if released into areas of Canada
    where there are migratory birds, endangered species or other
    wildlife regulated under any other Act of Parliament, are harmful or
    capable of causing harm to those birds, species or wildlife;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} substances that are on the list established under
    regulations made under subsection 200(1);

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the release of substances into the environment at
    any stage of their life-cycle;

  - [(k.1)]{.lawlabel} activities that may contribute to pollution;

  - [(k.2)]{.lawlabel} hydraulic fracturing;

  - [(k.3)]{.lawlabel} tailings ponds;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} pollution prevention; and

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} use of federal land and of aboriginal land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other recipient

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in accordance with an agreement
  signed with a government, require that a person to whom a notice is
  directed submit the information to the Minister or to that government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions respecting access to
  information

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An agreement referred to in subsection (2) shall set
  out conditions respecting access by the Minister or other government
  to all or part of the information that the person is required to
  submit and may set out any other conditions respecting the
  information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period of notice and date for
  compliance

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (1) must indicate
  the period during which it is in force, which may not exceed three
  years, and the date or dates within which the person to whom the
  notice is directed shall comply with the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Every person to whom a notice is directed shall
  comply with the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on request in writing from any
  person to whom a notice is directed, extend the date or dates within
  which the person shall comply with the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The notice must indicate the manner in which the
  information is to be provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Preservation of information

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} The notice may indicate the period during which, and
  the location where, the person to whom the notice is directed shall
  keep copies of the required information, together with any
  calculations, measurements and other data on which the information is
  based. The period may not exceed three years from the date the
  information is required to be submitted to the Minister.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 46
- [2023, c. 12, s. 9](#1402887-1404137){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402887-1404137.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-46-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 46"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines

- **[[47]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-47 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall issue guidelines respecting the use of the powers
  provided for by subsection 46(1) and, in issuing those guidelines, the
  Minister shall take into account any factor that the Minister
  considers relevant, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the costs and benefits to the Minister and the
    person to whom the notice under subsection 46(1) is directed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the co-ordination of requests for information with
    other governments, to the extent practicable; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the information collected under
    subsection 46(1) is to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In carrying out the duties under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall offer to consult with the government of a province and
  the members of the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal
  governments and may consult with a government department or agency,
  aboriginal people, representatives of industry and labour and
  municipal authorities or with persons interested in the quality of the
  environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (2), the Minister may act under subsection (1) if the offer to consult
  is not accepted by the government of a province or members of the
  Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 47
- 2017, c. 26, s. 63(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-47-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 47"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National inventory

**[[48]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-48 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
establish a national inventory of releases of pollutants using the
information collected under section 46 and any other information to
which the Minister has access, and may use any information to which the
Minister has access to establish any other inventory of information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication in whole or in part

**[[49]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-49 .sectionLabel}** The notice published
under subsection 46(1) must indicate whether or not the Minister intends
to publish the information and, if so, whether in whole or in part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of inventory

**[[50]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-50 .sectionLabel}** Subject to subsection
53(4), the Minister shall publish the national inventory of releases of
pollutants in any manner that the Minister considers appropriate and may
publish or give notice of the availability of any other inventory of
information established under section 48, in any manner that the
Minister considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

**[[51]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-51 .sectionLabel}** A person who provides
information to the Minister under subsection 46(1) may, if the
Minister's intention to publish the information has been indicated under
section 49, submit with the information a written request, setting out a
reason referred to in section 52, that the information be treated as
confidential.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasons

**[[52]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-52 .sectionLabel}** Despite Part 11, a
request under section 51 may only be based on any of the following
reasons:

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the information constitutes a trade secret;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of the information would likely cause
  material financial loss to, or prejudice to the competitive position
  of, the person providing the information or on whose behalf it is
  provided; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure of the information would likely
  interfere with contractual or other negotiations being conducted by
  the person providing the information or on whose behalf it is
  provided.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional justification

- **[[53]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-53 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, after studying the reasons provided under section 52,
  require the person in question to provide, within 20 days and in
  writing, additional justification for the request for confidentiality.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may extend the period mentioned in
  subsection (1) by up to 10 days if the extension is necessary to
  permit adequate preparation of the additional justification.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's decision

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In determining whether to accept or reject the
  request, the Minister shall consider whether the reasons are
  well-founded and, if they are, the Minister may nevertheless reject
  the request if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the disclosure is in the interest of the protection
    of the environment, public health or public safety; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the public interest in the disclosure outweighs in
    importance

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any material financial loss or prejudice to the
      competitive position of the person who provided the information or
      on whose behalf it was provided, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any damage to the privacy, reputation or human
      dignity of any individual that may result from the disclosure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Acceptance of request

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister accepts the request, the information
  shall not be published.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister rejects the request,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has the right to ask the Federal Court
    to review the matter within 30 days after the person is notified
    that the request has been rejected or within any further time that
    the Court may, before the expiry of those 30 days, fix or allow; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Minister shall advise the person in question of
    the Minister's intention to publish the information and of the
    person's right to ask the Federal Court to review the matter.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Applicable provisions

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Where a person asks the Federal Court to review the
  matter under paragraph (5)(a), sections 45, 46 and 47 of the [Access
  to Information Act](/eng/acts/A-1) apply, with any modifications that
  the circumstances require, in respect of a request for a review under
  that paragraph as if it were an application made under section 44 of
  that Act.

### [Objectives, Guidelines and Codes of Practice]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63762 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formulation by the Minister

- **[[54]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-54 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of carrying out the Minister's mandate related to
  preserving the quality of the environment, the Minister shall issue

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} environmental quality objectives specifying goals
    or purposes for pollution prevention or environmental control,
    including goals or purposes stated in quantitative or qualitative
    terms;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} environmental quality guidelines specifying
    recommendations in quantitative or qualitative terms to support and
    maintain particular uses of the environment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} release guidelines recommending limits, including
    limits expressed as concentrations or quantities, for the release of
    substances into the environment from works, undertakings or
    activities; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} codes of practice respecting pollution prevention
    or specifying procedures, practices or release limits for
    environmental control relating to works, undertakings and activities
    during any phase of their development and operation, including the
    location, design, construction, start-up, closure, dismantling and
    clean-up phases and any subsequent monitoring activities.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of objectives, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The objectives, guidelines and codes of practice
  referred to in subsection (1) shall relate to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the environment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pollution prevention or the recycling, reusing,
    treating, storing or disposing of substances or reducing the release
    of substances into the environment;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} works, undertakings or activities that affect or
    may affect the environment; or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conservation of natural resources and
    sustainable development.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} In carrying out the duties under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall offer to consult with the government of a province and
  the members of the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal
  governments and may consult with a government department or agency,
  aboriginal people, representatives of industry and labour and
  municipal authorities or with persons interested in the quality of the
  environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (3), the Minister may act under subsection (1) if the offer to consult
  is not accepted by the government of a province or members of the
  Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish any objectives, guidelines
  or codes of practice issued under this section, or give notice of
  them, in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any
  other manner that the Minister considers appropriate.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formulation by the Minister of Health

- **[[55]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-55 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of carrying out the mandate of the Minister of Health
  related to preserving and improving public health under this Act, the
  Minister of Health shall issue objectives, guidelines and codes of
  practice with respect to the elements of the environment that may
  affect the life and health of the people of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In carrying out the duties under subsection (1), the
  Minister of Health may consult with a government, a government
  department or agency, aboriginal people, representatives of industry
  and labour and municipal authorities or with persons interested in the
  preservation and improvement of public health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister of Health shall publish any objectives,
  guidelines or codes of practice issued under this section, or give
  notice of them, in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and
  in any other manner that the Minister of Health considers appropriate.

## [PART 4]{.HLabel1}[Pollution Prevention]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63789 .Part}

### [Pollution Prevention Plans]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63790 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement for pollution prevention
plans

- **[[56]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-56 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, at any time, publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person or class
  of persons described in the notice to prepare and implement a
  pollution prevention plan in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a substance or group of substances specified on the
    list of toxic substances in Schedule 1;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a substance or group of substances with respect to
    which subsection 166(1) or 176(1) applies; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a product that contains a substance specified on
    the list of toxic substances in Schedule 1 or that may release such
    a substance into the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Alternatives

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The notice may include a requirement that the plan
  prioritize the identification, development or use of safer or more
  sustainable alternatives to the substance, group of substances or
  product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice may specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance, group of substances or product in
    relation to which the plan is to be prepared;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the commercial, manufacturing, processing or other
    activity in relation to which the plan is to be prepared;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the factors to be considered in preparing the plan;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the period within which the plan is to be prepared;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the period within which the plan is to be
    implemented; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any administrative matter necessary for the
    purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister is of the opinion that further
  time is necessary to prepare or implement the plan, the Minister may
  extend the period for a person who submits a written request before
  the expiry of the period referred to in the notice or of any extended
  period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of notice of extension

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish in the Environmental
  Registry and in any other manner that the Minister considers
  appropriate a notice stating the name of any person for whom an
  extension is granted, whether the extension is for the preparation or
  the implementation of the plan, and the duration of the period of the
  extension.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application for waiver

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On a written request submitted by any person to whom
  a notice under subsection (1) is directed, the Minister may waive the
  requirement for that person to consider a factor specified under
  paragraph (2)(c) where the Minister is of the opinion that it is not
  reasonable or practicable to consider the factor on the basis of
  reasons provided in the request.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progress reports

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A notice under subsection (1) may include a
  requirement that the person to whom the notice is directed file with
  the Minister, within the periods specified in the notice, written
  reports on their progress in implementing the plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 56
- [2023, c. 12, s. 10](#1402894-1404151){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402894-1404151.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-56-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 56"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan prepared or implemented for another
purpose

- **[[57]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-57
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), where a
  person who is required to prepare or implement a pollution prevention
  plan under a notice published under section 56 has prepared or
  implemented a plan in respect of pollution prevention on a voluntary
  basis or for another government or under another Act of Parliament
  that meets all or some of the requirements of the notice, the person
  may use that plan for the purposes of meeting the requirements of this
  Part and, in that case, the plan shall be considered to be a pollution
  prevention plan that has been prepared or implemented under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where partial requirements met

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where a person uses a plan under subsection (1) that
  does not meet all of the requirements of the notice, the person shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} amend the plan so that it meets all of those
    requirements; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prepare an additional pollution prevention plan
    that meets the remainder of those requirements.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of preparation

- **[[58]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-58 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Every
  person who is required to prepare a pollution prevention plan under
  section 56 or 291 or under an agreement in respect of environmental
  protection alternative measures shall file, within 30 days after the
  end of the period for the preparation of the plan specified in the
  notice referred to in subsection 56(1) or extended under subsection
  56(3), or specified by the court under section 291 or in the
  agreement, as the case may be, a written declaration to the Minister
  that the plan has been prepared and is being implemented.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is required to implement a pollution
  prevention plan under section 56 or 291 or under an agreement in
  respect of environmental protection alternative measures shall file,
  within 30 days after the completion of the implementation of the plan,
  a written declaration to the Minister that the implementation of the
  plan has been completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Filing of amended declaration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a person has filed a declaration under
  subsection (1) or (2) and the declaration contains information that,
  at any time after the filing, has become false or misleading, that
  person shall file an amended declaration to the Minister within 30
  days after that time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Form of declaration

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A declaration referred to in subsection (1), (2)
  or (3) shall be filed in the form and manner and contain the
  information specified by the Minister.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to keep plan

**[[59]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-59 .sectionLabel}** Every person who is
required to prepare a pollution prevention plan under section 56 or 291
or under an agreement in respect of environmental protection alternative
measures shall keep a copy of the plan at the place in Canada in
relation to which the plan is prepared.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement to submit certain plans

- **[[60]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-60 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person or class
  of persons described in the notice who are required to prepare and
  implement a pollution prevention plan under section 56 to submit,
  within the period specified by the Minister, the plan or any part of
  the plan for the purpose of determining and assessing preventive or
  control actions in respect of a substance, group of substances or
  product.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of plans required by court
  or agreement

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person or class
  of persons described in the notice who are required to prepare and
  implement a pollution prevention plan under section 291 or under an
  agreement in respect of environmental protection alternative measures
  to submit, within the period specified by the Minister, the plan or
  any part of the plan.

### [Model Plans and Guidelines]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63831 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Model plans

**[[61]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-61 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
providing guidance in the preparation of a pollution prevention plan,
the Minister may publish in the [Canada
Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) or in any other manner that the
Minister considers appropriate a model pollution prevention plan or a
notice stating where a copy of the plan may be obtained.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines

- **[[62]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-62 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall, with particular consideration of paragraph 2(1)(m),
  develop guidelines respecting the circumstances in which and the
  conditions under which pollution prevention planning is appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In carrying out the duties under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall offer to consult with the government of a province and
  the members of the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal
  governments and may consult with a government department or agency,
  aboriginal people, representatives of industry and labour and
  municipal authorities or with persons interested in the quality of the
  environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (2), the Minister may act under subsection (1) if the offer to consult
  is not accepted by the government of a province or members of the
  Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

### [Other Initiatives]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63841 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Information clearing-house

- **[[63]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-63 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, for the purposes of encouraging and facilitating
  pollution prevention, establish and maintain a national pollution
  prevention information clearing-house in order to collect, exchange
  and distribute information relating to pollution prevention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recognition program

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may establish a program to publicly
  recognize significant achievements in the area of pollution
  prevention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cooperation with other bodies

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, in exercising the powers conferred
  by subsections (1) and (2), act alone or in cooperation with any
  government in Canada or government of a foreign state or any of its
  institutions or any person.

## [PART 5]{.HLabel1}[Controlling Toxic Substances]{.HTitleText1} {#h-63849 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63850 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Toxic substances

**[[64]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-64 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of this
Part and Part 6, except where the expression "inherently toxic" appears,
a substance is toxic if it is entering or may enter the environment in a
quantity or concentration or under conditions that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} have or may have an immediate or long-term harmful
  effect on the environment or its biological diversity;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger to the
  environment on which life depends; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger in Canada to
  human life or health.

**[[65]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-65 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2023, c. 12,
s. 12\]]{.Repealed}

### [General]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63867 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Domestic Substances List

- **[[66]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall, for the purpose of section 81, maintain a list to be
  known as the Domestic Substances List and, subject to subsection
  66.2(1), the List shall specify all substances that the Minister is
  satisfied were, between January 1, 1984 and December 31, 1986,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} manufactured in or imported into Canada by any
    person in a quantity of not less than 100 kg in any one calendar
    year; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in Canadian commerce or used for commercial
    manufacturing purposes in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-domestic Substances List

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall, for the purpose of section 81,
  maintain a list to be known as the Non-domestic Substances List, and
  the List shall specify substances, other than

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substances referred to in subsection (1),
    unless the substance has been deleted from the Domestic Substances
    List under subsection 66.2(1); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} living organisms within the meaning of Part 6.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of Lists

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a substance was not included on the Domestic
  Substances List and the Minister subsequently learns that, between
  January 1, 1984 and December 31, 1986, the requirements set out in
  paragraph (1)(a) or (b) were met in respect of the substance, the
  Minister shall add the substance to the List and, where necessary,
  delete it from the Non-domestic Substances List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of Lists

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister includes a substance on the
  Domestic Substances List and subsequently learns that, between January
  1, 1984 and December 31, 1986, the requirements set out in paragraph
  (1)(a) or (b) were not met in respect of the substance, the Minister
  shall delete the substance from the List and may add it to the
  Non-domestic Substances List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of Lists

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate the Domestic Substances List, the
  Non-domestic Substances List and any amendments to those Lists.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions set out in this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 66

  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Domestic Substances List --- [Food and
Drugs Act](/eng/acts/F-27)

- **[[66.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, for the purpose of
  section 81, add a substance to the Domestic Substances List if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance was included on the version of the
    Revised In Commerce List that was prepared by the Minister of Health
    after the end, on November 3, 2019, of acceptance of substance
    nominations to that List and that is referred to in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), Part I, Volume 152, Number 44,
    as the static list;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the substance is not referred to in Annex I to the
    notice entitled "Removal of substances with no commercial activity
    from the Revised In Commerce List" published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), Part I, Volume 156, Number 8;
    and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} no conditions specified under paragraph 84(1)(a) in
    respect of the substance are in effect.

  If the substance appears on the Non-domestic Substances List, the
  Minister shall delete it from that List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the powers set out in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 14](#1402913-1404173){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402913-1404173.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of Lists

- **[[66.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-66.2 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  the Minister is satisfied that a substance referred to in subsection
  66(1) that is specified on the Domestic Substances List --- or a
  substance added to the List under subsection 66.1(1) or 87(1) or (5)
  --- is not being manufactured in Canada, imported into Canada, in
  Canadian commerce or used for commercial manufacturing purposes in
  Canada, the Minister may delete the substance from the List and may
  add it to the Non-domestic Substances List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication --- notice of intent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before deleting a substance from the Domestic
  Substances List under subsection (1), the Minister shall publish in
  the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other
  manner that the Minister considers appropriate a notice indicating the
  Minister's intention to delete it from the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of the notice,
  any person may file written comments with the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions set out in this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 14](#1402913-1404184){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402913-1404184.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulation of criteria

- **[[67]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-67 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Ministers, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting a property or characteristic of a
    substance, including persistence, bioaccumulation, carcinogenicity,
    mutagenicity and reproductive toxicity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the substances or groups of substances
    in respect of which the property or characteristic may be
    applicable;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the conditions under which and the
    circumstances in which the property or characteristic may be
    applicable;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed for analysing, testing or
    measuring the property or characteristic, including conditions, test
    procedures and laboratory practices to be followed for replacing,
    reducing or refining the use of vertebrate animals; and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting, for the purpose of subsection 77(3),
    the classification of a substance as a substance that poses the
    highest risk.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Condition

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No regulation that is applicable to a mineral or
  metal may be made under subsection (1) unless, in the opinion of the
  Ministers, the natural occurrence, properties and characteristics of
  that mineral or metal in the environment have been taken into
  consideration.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Research, investigation and evaluation

**[[68]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68 .sectionLabel}** For the purpose of
assessing whether a substance is toxic or is capable of becoming toxic
--- or for the purpose of assessing whether to control, or the manner in
which to control, a substance, a product that contains a substance or a
product that may release a substance into the environment --- including
a substance specified on the list of toxic substances in Schedule 1,
either Minister may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} collect or generate data and conduct investigations
  respecting any matter in relation to the substance or product
  including

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} whether short-term exposure to the substance causes
    significant effects,

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} the potential of organisms in the environment to
    be widely exposed to the substance,

  - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} whether organisms are exposed to the substance
    via multiple pathways,

  - [(iii.1)]{.lawlabel} whether exposure to the substance in
    combination with exposure to other substances has the potential to
    cause cumulative effects,

  - [(iii.2)]{.lawlabel} whether there is a vulnerable population or
    environment in relation to the substance,

  - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the substance to cause a reduction
    in metabolic functions of an organism,

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the substance to cause delayed or
    latent effects over the lifetime of an organism, including
    carcinogenic, mutagenic or neurotoxic effects,

  - [(vi)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the substance to cause survival
    impairment of an organism,

  - [(vi.1)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the substance to disrupt the
    reproductive system or endocrine system of an organism,

  - [(vii)]{.lawlabel} whether exposure to the substance has the
    potential to contribute to population failure of a species,

  - [(viii)]{.lawlabel} the ability of the substance to cause
    transgenerational effects,

  - [(ix)]{.lawlabel} quantities, uses and disposal of the substance,

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the substance is released into
    the environment,

  - [(xi)]{.lawlabel} the extent to which the substance can be dispersed
    and will persist in the environment,

  - [(xii)]{.lawlabel} the existence, development and use of safer or
    more sustainable alternatives to the substance or product,

  - [(xiii)]{.lawlabel} methods of controlling the presence of the
    substance in the environment,

  - [(xiv)]{.lawlabel} methods of reducing the quantity of the substance
    used or produced or the quantities or concentration of the substance
    released into the environment, and

  - [(xv)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the public may be provided
    with information regarding the substance or product, including, in
    the case of a product, by labelling it;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} correlate and evaluate any data collected or
  generated under paragraph (a) and publish results of any
  investigations carried out under that paragraph; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide information and make recommendations
  respecting any matter in relation to the substance or product,
  including measures to control the presence of the substance or product
  in the environment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 68

  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402920-1404204.html"
  role="button"}
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restriction --- vertebrate animals

- **[[68.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-68.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall, to the extent
  practicable, use scientifically justified alternative methods and
  strategies to replace, reduce or refine the use of vertebrate animals
  in the generation of data and the conduct of investigations under
  paragraph 68(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Methods and strategies to refine use

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of subsection (1), methods and
  strategies to refine the use of vertebrate animals include minimizing
  pain and distress caused to vertebrate animals used in the generation
  of data and the conduct of investigations under paragraph 68(a).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 16.1](#1402933-1404218){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1402933-1404218.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Formulation of guidelines by the
Ministers

- **[[69]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-69 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Either
  Minister or both Ministers, as the case may be, may issue guidelines
  for the purposes of the interpretation and application of the
  provisions of this Part for which they have responsibility.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In exercising the powers under subsection (1), either
  Minister or both Ministers shall offer to consult with the government
  of a province and the members of the Committee who are representatives
  of aboriginal governments and may consult with a government department
  or agency, aboriginal people, representatives of industry and labour
  and municipal authorities or with persons interested in assessing and
  controlling toxic substances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Saving

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in subsection (2) shall prevent either
  Minister or both Ministers from exercising the powers under
  subsection (1) at any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which an offer is made under subsection (2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines public

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Guidelines issued under this section shall be made
  available to the public, and the Minister who issued the guidelines
  shall give notice of them in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that that
  Minister considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote

- [2023, c. 12, s. 17](#1402935-1404211){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::

### [Information Gathering]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63924 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to the Minister

**[[70]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-70 .sectionLabel}** Where a person

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} imports, manufactures, transports, processes or
  distributes a substance for commercial purposes, or

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} uses a substance in a commercial manufacturing or
  processing activity,

and obtains information that reasonably supports the conclusion that the
substance is toxic or is capable of becoming toxic, the person shall
without delay provide the information to the Minister unless the person
has actual knowledge that either Minister already has the information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice requiring information, samples or
testing

- **[[71]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-71 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, for the purpose of assessing whether a substance is
  toxic or is capable of becoming toxic --- or for the purpose of
  assessing whether to control, or the manner in which to control, a
  substance, a product that contains a substance or a product that may
  release a substance into the environment --- including a substance
  specified on the list of toxic substances in Schedule 1,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} publish in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
    Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person who is
    described in the notice and who is or was within the period
    specified in the notice engaged in any activity involving the
    substance or product, as the case may be, to notify the Minister
    that the person is or was during that period engaged in that
    activity;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} publish in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
    Minister considers appropriate a notice requiring any person who is
    described in the notice to provide the Minister with any information
    and samples referred to in subsection (2) that may be in the
    person's possession or to which the person may reasonably be
    expected to have access; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to section 72, send a written notice to any
    person who is described in the notice and who is or was within the
    period specified in the notice engaged in any activity involving the
    importation or manufacturing of the substance or product, as the
    case may be, requiring the person to conduct toxicological or other
    tests that the Minister may specify in the notice and submit the
    results of the tests to the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice under paragraph
  (1)(b)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice published under paragraph (1)(b) may require
  any information and samples, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a substance, available toxicological
    information, available monitoring information, samples of the
    substance and information on the quantities, composition, uses and
    distribution of the substance;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a product that contains a substance
    or that may release a substance into the environment, information on
    the quantities, composition, manufacturing, processing, packaging,
    labelling, uses and distribution of the product;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a work, undertaking or activity,
    plans, specifications, studies and information on procedures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} information on the method used to quantify any
    information that is provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method and manner --- notice under
  paragraph (1)(b)

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} A notice published under paragraph (1)(b) may
  specify the method to be used to quantify any information that is to
  be provided and the manner in which that information and any sample is
  to be provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice under paragraph
  (1)(c)

  [(2.2)]{.lawlabel} A notice sent under paragraph (1)(c) may, among
  other things, require the person to provide any information and
  samples, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in respect of a substance or of a product that
    contains a substance or that may release a substance into the
    environment, toxicological information, monitoring information, test
    samples and information on the quantities, composition, uses and
    distribution of the substance or product; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} information on the methods, test procedures and
    laboratory practices followed for performing any required test and
    on the conditions under which those tests are conducted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Method and manner --- notice under
  paragraph (1)(c)

  [(2.3)]{.lawlabel} The notice sent under paragraph (1)(c) may, among
  other things, specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, methods, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed for conducting sampling,
    analyses, measurements, quantification or monitoring as part of any
    required test;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, methods, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed to replace, reduce or refine the
    use of vertebrate animals;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the test results are to be
    submitted;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the method to be used to quantify any information
    that is to be provided; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which that information and any sample
    is to be provided.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with notice

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every person to whom a notice referred to in any of
  paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) is directed or sent shall comply with the
  notice within the time specified in the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite subsection (3), the Minister may, on request
  in writing from any person to whom a notice referred to in any of
  paragraphs (1)(a) to (c) is directed or sent, extend the time or times
  within which the person shall comply with the notice.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercise of power under paragraph
71(1)(c)

**[[72]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-72 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may not
exercise the power under paragraph 71(1)(c) in relation to a substance,
a product that contains a substance or a product that may release a
substance into the environment unless the Ministers have reason to
suspect that the substance is toxic or capable of becoming toxic or it
has been determined under this Act that the substance is toxic or
capable of becoming toxic.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 72
- [2023, c. 12, s. 19](#1402957-1404248){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-72-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 72"){.PITLink}

### [Substances and Assessments of Substances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-63948 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan --- priorities

- **[[73]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-73 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Ministers shall, within two years after the day on which this section
  receives royal assent, develop and publish a plan with timelines

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that specifies the substances to which the
    Ministers are satisfied priority should be given in assessing
    whether they are toxic or capable of becoming toxic;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that specifies the activities or initiatives in
    relation to assessing, controlling or otherwise managing the risks
    to the environment or to human health posed by substances that are
    or will be undertaken under an Act of Parliament for whose
    administration either Minister is responsible and which the
    Ministers are of the opinion should be prioritized; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} that specifies activities or initiatives to promote
    the development and timely incorporation of scientifically justified
    alternative methods and strategies in the testing and assessment of
    substances to replace, reduce or refine the use of vertebrate
    animals.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation and considerations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In developing a proposed plan, and in implementing
  the plan, the Ministers

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} may consult with the Committee, a government
    department or agency, aboriginal people, representatives of industry
    and labour and municipal authorities or with persons interested in
    the quality of the environment or the preservation and improvement
    of public health;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall consider whether assessing substances by
    class is more advantageous than assessing them individually, with a
    view toward avoiding substitutions within the class that may be
    harmful; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} shall take into account the matters referred to in
    paragraph 68(a), including the manner in which the public may be
    provided with information regarding substances or products
    including, in the case of products, by labelling them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of proposed plan

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish the proposed plan in the
  Environmental Registry and shall give notice in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate of the proposed plan's availability.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of the notice,
  any person may file with the Minister written comments on the proposed
  plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consideration of comments

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall take into account the comments
  and may amend the proposed plan in any manner they consider
  appropriate based on those comments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of plan

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish the plan in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review of plan

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Ministers shall review the plan within eight
  years after it is published and every eight years after that.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} If after reviewing the plan the Ministers propose to
  amend it, subsections (2) to (7) apply to the proposed amended plan.

  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

**[[74]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-74 .sectionLabel}** The Ministers shall
include in the annual report required by section 342 a report on the
progress made in assessing the substances specified in the plan
developed under section 73 and in respect of any activities or
initiatives specified in the plan.
