### [Export of Substances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-64384 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export Control List

**[[100]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-100 .sectionLabel}** The Ministers may, by
order,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} add to Part 1 of the Export Control List in Schedule
  3 any substance the use of which is prohibited in Canada by or under
  an Act of Parliament, and delete any substance from that Part;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} add to Part 2 of the Export Control List in Schedule
  3 any substance that is subject to an international agreement that
  requires notification or requires the consent of the country of
  destination before the substance is exported from Canada, and delete
  any substance from that Part; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} add to Part 3 of the Export Control List in Schedule
  3 any substance the use of which is restricted in Canada by or under
  an Act of Parliament, and delete any substance from that Part.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to Minister

- **[[101]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-101
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), no person
  shall export a substance specified in the Export Control List in
  Schedule 3 unless the person provides prior notice of the proposed
  export to the Minister in accordance with the regulations made under
  subsection 102(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions on export

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), no person shall export a
  substance specified in Part 1 of the Export Control List in Schedule 3
  unless the export of the substance

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is for the purpose of destroying the substance or
    complying with a direction under subparagraph 99(b)(iii); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is done in accordance with any regulations made
    under subsection 102(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Restrictions on export

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), no person shall export a
  substance specified in Part 2 or 3 of the Export Control List in
  Schedule 3 unless the export of the substance is done in accordance
  with any regulations made under subsection 102(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Total prohibition on export

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall export a substance specified in the
  Export Control List in Schedule 3 if the export of the substance is
  prohibited by a regulation made under subsection 102(2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations respecting exports

- **[[102]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-102 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Ministers, make
  regulations in relation to substances specified in the Export Control
  List in Schedule 3

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information that must be given to
    the Minister regarding an export of such a substance, the time when
    or period within which the information must be given, and the manner
    in which it must be given;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information that must accompany an
    export of such a substance and the manner in which it must accompany
    the substance;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting conditions under which a person may
    export such a substance;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information that must be kept by a
    person who exports such a substance and the manner in which, the
    period for which and the place where the information must be kept;
    and

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes of section
    101.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations prohibiting exports

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Ministers, make regulations prohibiting the export of a substance
  specified in the Export Control List in Schedule 3.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of exports

**[[103]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-103 .sectionLabel}** If a person exports a
substance specified in the Export Control List in Schedule 3, the
Minister shall publish in the Environmental Registry the name or
specifications of the substance, the name of the exporter and the name
of the country of destination.

## [PART 6]{.HLabel1}[Animate Products of Biotechnology]{.HTitleText1} {#h-64413 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[104]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-104 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[living organism]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[living organism]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance that is an
    animate product of biotechnology. ([organisme
    vivant]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[significant new activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[significant new activity]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes, in
    respect of a living organism, any activity that results or may
    result in

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the entry or release of the living organism into
      the environment in a quantity or concentration that, in the
      Ministers' opinion, is significantly greater than the quantity or
      concentration of the living organism that previously entered or
      was released into the environment; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the entry or release of the living organism into
      the environment or the exposure or potential exposure of the
      environment to the living organism in a manner and circumstances
      that, in the Ministers' opinion, are significantly different from
      the manner and circumstances in which the living organism
      previously entered or was released into the environment or of any
      previous exposure or potential exposure of the environment to the
      living organism. ([nouvelle activité]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Adding living organisms to Domestic
Substances List

- **[[105]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall, for the purpose of section 106, add to the Domestic
  Substances List maintained under section 66 any living organism if the
  Minister is satisfied that, between January 1, 1984 and December 31,
  1986, the living organism

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was manufactured in or imported into Canada by any
    person; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entered or was released into the environment
    without being subject to conditions under this or any other Act of
    Parliament or of the legislature of a province.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of List

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister includes a living organism on the
  Domestic Substances List and subsequently learns that, between January
  1, 1984 and December 31, 1986, the requirements set out in paragraphs
  (1)(a) and (b) were not met, the Minister shall delete the substance
  from the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of List

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) the Domestic Substances List and
  any amendment to the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions set out in this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 105
- [2023, c. 12, s. 38](#1403020-1404510){.wb-lbx

  role="button"}
:::

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Domestic Substances List --- [Food and
Drugs Act](/eng/acts/F-27)

- **[[105.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, for the purpose of
  section 106, add a living organism to the Domestic Substances List if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the living organism was included on the version of
    the Revised In Commerce List that was prepared by the Minister of
    Health after the end, on November 3, 2019, of acceptance of
    substance nominations to that List and that is referred to in the
    [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), Part I, Volume 152,
    Number 44, as the static list; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} no conditions specified under paragraph 109(1)(a)
    in respect of the living organism are in effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the power set out in subsection (1).

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 39](#1403022-1404519){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403022-1404519.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of List

- **[[105.2]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-105.2
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is satisfied that a
  living organism added to the Domestic Substances List under subsection
  105(1), 105.1(1) or 112(1) is not being manufactured in Canada or
  imported into Canada the Minister may delete the living organism from
  the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication --- notice of intent

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before deleting a living organism from the Domestic
  Substances List under subsection (1), the Minister shall publish in
  the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any other
  manner that the Minister considers appropriate a notice indicating the
  Minister's intention to delete it from the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of the notice,
  any person may file written comments with the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by order, designate any person or
  class of persons to exercise the powers and perform the duties and
  functions set out in this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 39](#1403022-1404530){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403022-1404530.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manufacture or import of living organisms

- **[[106]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-106
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a living organism is not
  specified on the Domestic Substances List, no person shall manufacture
  or import the living organism unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the prescribed information with respect to the
    living organism, accompanied by the prescribed fee, has been
    provided by that person to the Minister on or before the prescribed
    date; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for assessing the information under
    section 108 has expired.

- [(2)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 26, s. 26\]]{.Repealed}

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of significant new
  activity in respect of living organism on List

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where a living organism is specified on the Domestic
  Substances List with an indication that this subsection applies with
  respect to the living organism, no person shall use, manufacture or
  import the living organism for a significant new activity that is
  indicated on the List with respect to the living organism unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has provided the Minister with the
    prescribed information, on or before the date that is specified by
    the Minister or prescribed, accompanied by the prescribed fee; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for assessing the information specified
    by the Minister or provided under section 108 has expired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of significant new
  activity in respect of living organism not on List

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where a living organism is not specified on the
  Domestic Substances List and the Minister publishes a notice in the
  [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) indicating that this
  subsection applies with respect to the living organism, no person
  shall use the living organism for a significant new activity that is
  indicated in the notice unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person has provided the Minister with the
    prescribed information, on or before the date that is specified by
    the Minister or prescribed, accompanied by the prescribed fee; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for assessing the information specified
    by the Minister or provided under section 108 has expired.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transfer of rights in respect of
  substance

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where prescribed information with respect to a
  substance has been provided under subsection (1), (3) or (4) by a
  person who subsequently transfers the right or privilege in relation
  to the substance for which the information was provided, the
  information is, subject to any conditions that may be prescribed,
  deemed to have been provided by the transferee of that right or
  privilege.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Subsections (1), (3) and (4) do not apply to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a living organism that is manufactured or imported
    for a use that is regulated under any other Act of Parliament that
    provides for notice to be given before the manufacture, import or
    sale of the living organism and for an assessment of whether it is
    toxic or capable of becoming toxic;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a living organism that is manufactured, used or
    imported under the conditions and in the circumstances prescribed as
    exempt from this section; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} impurities and contaminants related to the
    preparation of a living organism.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Governor in Council may amend Schedule
  4

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of the administration of this
  section, the Governor in Council has the exclusive responsibility for
  determining whether or not the requirements referred to in paragraph
  (6)(a) are met by or under an Act of Parliament referred to in that
  paragraph, or regulations made under that Act, and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the Governor in Council determines that the
    requirements referred to in paragraph (6)(a) are met by or under an
    Act of Parliament referred to in that paragraph, or regulations made
    under that Act, the Governor in Council may by order add to Schedule
    4 the name of that Act or those regulations, as the case may be, and
    the fact that an Act or regulations are listed in Schedule 4 is
    conclusive proof that the requirements referred to in paragraph
    (6)(a) are met; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the Governor in Council determines that the
    requirements referred to in paragraph (6)(a) are no longer met by or
    under an Act of Parliament, or regulations, listed in Schedule 4,
    the Governor in Council may by order delete from Schedule 4 the name
    of that Act or those regulations, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of information requirements

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} On the request of any person to whom subsection
  (1), (3) or (4) applies, the Minister may waive any of the
  requirements to provide information under that subsection if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the opinion of the Ministers, the information is
    not needed in order to determine whether the living organism is
    toxic or capable of becoming toxic;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a living organism is to be used for a prescribed
    purpose or manufactured at a location where, in the opinion of the
    Ministers, the person requesting the waiver is able to contain the
    living organism so as to satisfactorily protect the environment and
    human health; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is not, in the opinion of the Ministers,
    practicable or feasible to obtain the test data necessary to
    generate the information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of notice of waiver

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall, as soon as possible in the
  circumstances, publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) a notice stating the name of any
  person to whom a waiver is granted and the type of information to
  which it relates.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance with waiver

  [(10)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister waives any of the requirements
  for information under paragraph (8)(b), the person to whom the waiver
  is granted shall not use, manufacture or import the living organism
  unless it is for the purpose prescribed by regulations made under
  paragraph 114(1)(f) or at the location specified in the request for
  the waiver, as the case may be.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Correction of information

  [(11)]{.lawlabel} A person who has provided information under this
  section, including for the purposes of a request for a waiver under
  subsection (8), or under section 107 or 109 shall notify the Minister
  of any corrections to the information as soon as possible after
  learning of them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for information previously
  waived

  [(12)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister is notified of any corrections to
  information that was provided for the purposes of a request for a
  waiver under subsection (8), the Minister may, after consideration by
  the Ministers of those corrections, require the person to whom the
  waiver was granted to provide the Minister with the information to
  which the waiver related within the time specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of section 109

  [(13)]{.lawlabel} Where the Ministers suspect, after considering

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any corrections received under subsection (11), or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the information provided under subsection (12),

  that a living organism is toxic or capable of becoming toxic, the
  Minister may exercise any of the powers referred to in paragraphs
  109(1)(a) to (c).

:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition of activity

- **[[107]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-107
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister has reasonable
  grounds to believe that a person has used, manufactured or imported a
  living organism in contravention of subsection 106(1), (3) or (4), the
  Minister may, in writing, require the person to provide the
  information referred to in that subsection and prohibit any activity
  involving the living organism until the expiry of the period for
  assessing the information under section 108.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiver of information requirements

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} On the request of any person required under
  subsection (1) to provide information, the Minister may waive any of
  the requirements for prescribed information if one of the conditions
  specified in paragraphs 106(8)(a) to (c) is met and, in that case,
  subsections 106(9) to (13) apply with respect to the waiver.

- [(3)]{.lawlabel} [\[Repealed, 2017, c. 26, s. 27\]]{.Repealed}

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 107
- 2017, c. 26, s. 27
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-107-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 107"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of information

- **[[108]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  Ministers shall, within the prescribed assessment period, assess
  information provided under subsection 106(1), (3) or (4) or paragraph
  109(1)(c) or otherwise available to them in respect of a living
  organism in order to determine whether it is toxic or capable of
  becoming toxic.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Assessment of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsections (3) and (4), the Ministers
  shall assess information provided under subsection 107(1) or otherwise
  available to them in respect of a living organism in order to
  determine whether it is toxic or capable of becoming toxic.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for assessment

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An assessment of information under subsection (2)
  shall be made following the date on which the information is provided
  within a period that does not exceed the number of days in the
  prescribed assessment period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of assessment period

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the Ministers are of the opinion that further
  time is necessary to assess any information, the Minister may, before
  the expiry of the assessment period referred to in subsection (1) or
  (3), extend the period for assessing the information, but the
  extension shall not exceed the number of days in the prescribed
  assessment period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of extension

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister extends the period for assessing
  information, the Minister shall, before the expiry of the assessment
  period referred to in subsection (1) or (3), notify the person who
  provided the information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Termination of period

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, before the expiry of the assessment
  period referred to in subsection (1) or (3), terminate the period for
  assessing information and, immediately before doing so, shall notify
  the person who provided the information.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

- **[[108.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-108.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If the information that the
  Ministers assess under subsection 108(1) or (2) is in respect of a
  vertebrate animal or a prescribed living organism or group of living
  organisms, the Ministers shall consult any interested persons before
  the expiry of the period for assessing that information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before undertaking consultations, the Minister shall
  publish a notice of consultation in any manner that the Minister
  considers appropriate.

::: HistoricalNote
- [2023, c. 12, s. 39.1](#1403026-1404540){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403026-1404540.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Action to be taken after assessment

- **[[109]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-109
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Ministers have assessed
  any information under section 108 and they suspect that a living
  organism is toxic or capable of becoming toxic, the Minister may,
  before the expiry of the period for assessing the information,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} permit any person to manufacture or import the
    living organism, subject to any conditions that the Ministers may
    specify;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prohibit any person from manufacturing or importing
    the living organism; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} request any person to provide any additional
    information or submit the results of any testing that the Ministers
    consider necessary for the purpose of assessing whether the living
    organism is toxic or capable of becoming toxic.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Additional information or testing

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister requests additional information or
  test results under paragraph (1)(c), the person to whom the request is
  directed shall not manufacture or import the living organism unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person provides the additional information or
    submits the test results; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period for assessing information under section
    108 has expired or a period of 120 days after the additional
    information or test results were provided has expired, whichever is
    later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Variation of conditions and
  prohibitions

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may vary or rescind a condition or
  prohibition specified or imposed under paragraph (1)(a) or (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Expiry of prohibition

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Any prohibition on the manufacture or import of a
  living organism imposed under paragraph (1)(b) expires two years after
  the day on which it is imposed unless, before the expiry of the two
  years, there is published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a regulation respecting preventive or control
    actions, in relation to the living organism, that is proposed to be
    made under this Act; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a statement, identifying a regulation respecting
    preventive or control actions in relation to the living organism
    that is proposed to be made under any other Act of Parliament,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} made jointly by the Minister and the Minister
      responsible for the administration of the Act of Parliament under
      which the regulation is to be made, if the Act is not an Act
      referred to in subparagraph (ii), or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} made by the Minister of Health, if the Act of
      Parliament is one for whose administration that Minister is
      responsible.

  If such a proposed regulation --- or such a statement identifying a
  proposed regulation --- is so published, the prohibition expires on
  the day on which the regulation comes into force.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of conditions and
  prohibitions

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister specifies, imposes, varies or
  rescinds any condition for or prohibition on the manufacture or import
  of a living organism, the Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) a notice setting out the condition
  or prohibition and the living organism in respect of which it applies.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 109
- [2023, c. 12, s. 40](#1403028-1404550){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403028-1404550.html"
  role="button"}
:::

Version](section-109-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 109"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Significant new activity

- **[[110]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-110
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where the Ministers have assessed
  any information under section 108 in respect of a living organism that
  is not on the Domestic Substances List and they suspect that a
  significant new activity in relation to that living organism may
  result in the living organism becoming toxic, the Minister may, within
  90 days after the expiry of the period for assessing the information,
  publish in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) and in any
  other manner that the Minister considers appropriate a notice
  indicating that subsection 106(4) applies with respect to the living
  organism.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A notice referred to in subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall indicate, by inclusion or exclusion, the
    significant new activities in relation to the living organism with
    respect to which subsection 106(4) is to apply and, if regulations
    in respect of those significant new activities are not made under
    paragraphs 114(1)(c), (d) and (g), specify the information to be
    provided to the Minister under that subsection, the date on or
    before which it is to be provided and the period within which it is
    to be assessed under section 108; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may specify for the purpose of subsection 111(2)
    classes of persons who are not required to be notified under
    subsection 111(1) with respect to the living organism.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Classes of persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by notice published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), specify for the purpose of
  subsection 111(2) classes of persons who are not required to be
  notified under subsection 111(1) with respect to a living organism for
  which a notice was published under subsection (1), if no such class
  was specified in that notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, by notice published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/),

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vary the significant new activities in relation to
    a living organism for which a notice has been published under
    subsection (1) or vary the information to be provided to the
    Minister under subsection 106(4), the date on or before which that
    information is to be provided or the period within which it is to be
    assessed under section 108;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} indicate that subsection 106(4) no longer applies
    with respect to that living organism;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} vary the classes of persons, if any, that are
    specified for the purpose of subsection 111(2) in a notice published
    under subsection (1) or (3); or

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} indicate that a class of persons is no longer
    specified for the purpose of subsection 111(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 110
- [2023, c. 12, s. 41](#1403030-1404565){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403030-1404565.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-110-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 110"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of persons required to
comply

- **[[111]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-111
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where a notice is published in the
  [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) under subsection 110(1) in
  respect of a living organism, every person who transfers the physical
  possession or control of the living organism shall notify all persons
  to whom the possession or control is transferred of the obligation to
  comply with subsection 106(4).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person who is within a class of persons that is
  specified in a notice published under subsection 110(1) or (3) --- or,
  if the class is varied by a notice published under subsection 110(4),
  within the class as varied --- is not required to be notified under
  subsection (1) with respect to the living organism to which the notice
  relates.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 111
- [2023, c. 12, s. 42](#1403032-1404573){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403032-1404573.html"
  role="button"}

Version](section-111-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 111"){.PITLink}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of List

- **[[112]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall add a living organism to the Domestic Substances List
  within 120 days after the following conditions are met:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister has been provided with information in
    respect of the living organism under section 106 or 107 and any
    additional information or test results required under subsection
    109(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Ministers are satisfied that the living
    organism has been manufactured in or imported into Canada by the
    person who provided the information prescribed for the purpose of
    this paragraph;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period for assessing the information under
    section 108 has expired; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} no conditions specified under paragraph 109(1)(a)
    in respect of the living organism remain in effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendment of List

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister adds a living organism to the
  Domestic Substances List and subsequently learns that the living
  organism was not manufactured or imported as described in subsection
  (1), the Minister shall delete the living organism from the List.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Significant new activity

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If a living organism is on the Domestic Substances
  List or is to be added to the List under subsection (1) or 105.1(1),
  the Minister may amend the List to indicate that subsection 106(3)
  applies with respect to the living organism.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of amendment

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An amendment referred to in subsection (3)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall indicate, by inclusion or exclusion, the
    significant new activities in relation to the living organism with
    respect to which subsection 106(3) is to apply and, if regulations
    in respect of those significant new activities are not made under
    paragraphs 114(1)(c), (d) and (g), specify the information to be
    provided to the Minister under that subsection, the date on or
    before which it is to be provided and the period within which it is
    to be assessed under section 108; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may specify for the purpose of subsection 112.1(2)
    classes of persons who are not required to be notified under
    subsection 112.1(1) with respect to the living organism.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Subsequent amendment

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may amend the Domestic Substances List
  in respect of a living organism with respect to which subsection
  106(3) applies

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} to vary the significant new activities in relation
    to the living organism or vary the information to be provided to the
    Minister under subsection 106(3), the date on or before which that
    information is to be provided or the period within which it is to be
    assessed under section 108;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} to indicate that subsection 106(3) no longer
    applies;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} to specify classes of persons for the purpose of
    subsection 112.1(2), if no such classes are specified with respect
    to the living organism;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} to vary the classes of persons, if any, that are
    specified for the purpose of subsection 112.1(2); or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} to delete any class of persons that is specified
    for the purpose of subsection 112.1(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 112

:::

Version](section-112-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 112"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notification of persons required to
comply

- **[[112.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-112.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a living organism is specified on
  the Domestic Substances List with an indication that subsection 106(3)
  applies with respect to the living organism, every person who
  transfers the physical possession or control of the living organism
  shall notify all persons to whom the possession or control is
  transferred of the obligation to comply with that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person is not required to be notified under
  subsection (1) with respect to a living organism if they are within a
  class of persons that is specified on the Domestic Substances List in
  respect of the living organism for the purpose of this subsection.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of masked name

**[[113]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-113 .sectionLabel}** Where the publication
under this Part of the explicit biological name of a living organism
would result in the release of confidential business information in
contravention of section 314, the living organism shall be identified by
a name determined in the prescribed manner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[114]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-114 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Ministers, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting living organisms or establishing groups
    of living organisms for the purposes of the provision of information
    under section 106 or 107, including those that are exotic or
    indigenous, research and development living organisms and living
    organisms manufactured only for export, and designating ecozones or
    groups of ecozones;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing conditions and circumstances for the
    purpose of paragraph 106(6)(b);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information that shall be provided
    to the Minister under subsection 106(1), (3) or (4) or section 107
    and the form and manner in which it is to be provided;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribing dates on or before which information
    shall be provided under subsection 106(1), (3) or (4);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the maintenance of books and records for
    the administration of any regulation made under this section;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the purpose for which a living organism
    must be used so as to permit the waiver of information requirements
    under subsection 106(8);

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} prescribing periods within which the Ministers
    shall assess information under subsection 108(1);

  - [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} prescribing a living organism or group of living
    organisms for the purpose of subsection 108.1(1);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed in developing test data on a
    living organism in order to comply with the information requirements
    of section 106 or 107 or requests for information under paragraph
    109(1)(c);

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing information for the purpose of
    paragraph 112(1)(b);

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the manner of determining a name for a
    living organism for the purpose of section 113; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} generally for carrying out the purposes and
    provisions of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescribed assessment period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of sections 106 and 108, where no
  assessment period is prescribed or specified with respect to a living
  organism, the prescribed assessment period is 120 days after the
  Minister is provided with the prescribed information.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescription of conditions and
  circumstances

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under paragraph (1)(b) may prescribe
  conditions and circumstances in respect of a living organism in terms
  of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether or not the living organism is a member of a
    group of living organisms established by regulations made under
    paragraph (1)(a); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the purposes for which the living organism is
    manufactured or imported.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prescription of information and
  assessment periods

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under paragraph (1)(c), (d) or (g)
  may prescribe information, dates or periods in respect of a living
  organism in terms of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} whether or not the living organism is a member of a
    group of living organisms established by regulations made under
    paragraph (1)(a);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the purposes for which the living organism is
    manufactured or imported; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the conditions under which and the circumstances in
    which the living organism is manufactured or imported.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 114

- [2023, c. 12, s. 44.1](#1403038-1404598){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403038-1404598.html"
  role="button"}

::: PITLink

Version](section-114-20171212.html "Link to previous version of section 114"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other regulations

- **[[115]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-115
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), the
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Ministers, make
  regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for the purposes of implementing an international
    agreement,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} respecting living organisms, whether or not they
      are on the Domestic Substances List, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} respecting the safety of the environment or
      human health, including the safe transfer, handling and uses of
      any living organism that is moved across a boundary; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the effective and safe use of living
    organisms in pollution prevention.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Living organisms regulated under other
  Acts of Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not make a regulation
  under subsection (1) in respect of any living organism if the
  regulation regulates an aspect of the living organism that is
  regulated by or under any other Act of Parliament in a manner that
  provides, in the opinion of the Governor in Council, sufficient
  protection to the environment and human health.

## [PART 7]{.HLabel1}[Controlling Pollution and Managing Wastes]{.HTitleText1} {#h-64575 .Part}

### [DIVISION 1]{.HLabel2}[Nutrients]{.HTitleText2} {#h-64576 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[116]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-116 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Division and in Part 10.

[[cleaning product]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[cleaning product]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a phosphate compound
    or other substance that is intended to be used for cleaning
    purposes, and includes laundry detergents, dish-washing compounds,
    metal cleaners, de-greasing compounds and household, commercial and
    industrial cleaners. ([produit de nettoyage]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[nutrient]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[nutrient]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance or combination of
    substances that, if released in any waters, provides nourishment
    that promotes the growth of aquatic vegetation. ([substance
    nutritive]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[water conditioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[water conditioner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a substance that is
    intended to be used to treat water, and includes water-softening
    chemicals, anti-scale chemicals and corrosion
    inhibiters. ([conditionneur d'eau]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[117]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-117 .sectionLabel}** No person shall
manufacture for use or sale in Canada or import a cleaning product or
water conditioner that contains a prescribed nutrient in a concentration
greater than the permissible concentration prescribed for that product
or conditioner.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 117
- 2016, c. 9, s. 30(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-117-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 117"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[118]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-118 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for the purpose of preventing or reducing the growth of
  aquatic vegetation that is caused by the release of nutrients in
  waters and that can interfere with the functioning of an ecosystem or
  degrade or alter, or form part of a process of degrading or altering,
  an ecosystem to an extent that is detrimental to its use by humans,
  animals or plants, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing nutrients;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the permissible concentration of a
    prescribed nutrient in a cleaning product or water conditioner;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed for analysing, testing,
    measuring or monitoring a nutrient, cleaning product or water
    conditioner; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} requiring persons who manufacture for use or sale
    in Canada or import a cleaning product or water conditioner

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to maintain books and records for the proper
      administration of this Division and the regulations,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to submit samples of the cleaning product or
      water conditioner to the Minister, and

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} to submit to either Minister information
      regarding cleaning products, water conditioners and their
      ingredients.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation
  of the Minister, make regulations exempting a cleaning product or
  water conditioner from the application of section 117.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Class of cleaning product or water
  conditioner

  [(1.2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under subsection (1) or (1.1) may
  distinguish among classes of cleaning products or water conditioners
  that they may establish on the basis of any factor, including

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the physical or chemical properties of the cleaning
    products or water conditioners; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the conditions under which, the purposes for which
    or the manner or place in which the cleaning products or water
    conditioners are intended to be used.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nutrients regulated under other Acts of
  Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not make a regulation
  under subsection (1) in respect of a nutrient to the extent that the
  nutrient, or a product in which the nutrient is contained, is, in the
  opinion of the Governor in Council, regulated by or under any other
  Act of Parliament in a manner that provides, in the opinion of the
  Governor in Council, sufficient protection to the environment.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 118
- 2016, c. 9, s. 31
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-118-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 118"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedial measures

- **[[119]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-119
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where there is a contravention of
  section 117 or the regulations, the Minister may, in writing, direct a
  manufacturer or importer of a nutrient, cleaning product or water
  conditioner to take any or all of the following measures in the manner
  and within the period directed by the Minister:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give public notice of the contravention and of any
    danger to the environment or to human life or health posed by the
    nutrient, cleaning product or water conditioner;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} mail a notice as described in paragraph (a) to
    manufacturers, processors, distributors or retailers of the
    nutrient, cleaning product or water conditioner;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} mail a notice as described in paragraph (a) to
    persons to whom the nutrient, cleaning product or water conditioner
    is known to have been delivered or sold;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} replace the nutrient, cleaning product or water
    conditioner with one that meets the applicable requirements;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} accept the return of the nutrient, cleaning product
    or water conditioner from the purchaser and refund the purchase
    price;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} take other measures for the protection of the
    environment or human life or health; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} report to the Minister on the steps taken in
    satisfaction of any direction under paragraphs (a) to (f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention of Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person fails to take any measures required under
  paragraph (1)(a), (b), (c) or (f), the Minister may take those
  measures or cause them to be taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs
  and expenses of and incidental to taking any measures under
  subsection (2) from the person referred to in that subsection.

### [DIVISION 2]{.HLabel2}[Protection of the Marine Environment from Land-based Sources of Pollution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-64620 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[120]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-120 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Division.

[[land-based sources]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[land-based sources]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means point and diffuse
    sources on land from which substances or energy reach the sea by
    water, through the air or directly from the coast. It includes any
    sources under the sea bed made accessible from land by tunnel,
    pipeline or other means. ([sources telluriques]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[marine pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[marine pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the introduction by
    humans, directly or indirectly, of substances or energy into the sea
    that results, or is likely to result, in

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} hazards to human health;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} harm to living resources or marine ecosystems;

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} damage to amenities; or

    - [(d)]{.lawlabel} interference with other legitimate uses of the
      sea. ([pollution des mers]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Objectives, guidelines and codes of
practice

- **[[121]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-121 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, after consultation with any other affected minister,
  issue environmental objectives, release guidelines and codes of
  practice to prevent and reduce marine pollution from land-based
  sources.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation and conferences

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} To carry out the functions set out in subsection (1),
  the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall offer to consult with the government of a
    province and the members of the Committee who are representatives of
    aboriginal governments and may consult with any government
    department or agency or any person interested in the protection of
    the sea;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may organize conferences relating to the prevention
    or reduction of marine pollution from land-based sources; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} may meet with the representatives of international
    organizations and agencies and other countries to examine the rules,
    standards, practices and procedures recommended under the United
    Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea, signed by Canada on
    October 7, 1982.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with paragraph
  (2)(a), the Minister may act under subsection (1) if the offer to
  consult is not accepted by the government of a province or members of
  the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

### [DIVISION 3]{.HLabel2}[Disposal at Sea]{.HTitleText2} {#h-64639 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64640 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

- **[[122]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  definitions in this subsection apply in this Division and in Part 10.

  [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a machine that is used or
      designed for navigation in the air, but does not include an air
      cushion vehicle. ([aéronef]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Canadian aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Canadian aircraft]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means an aircraft that
      is registered under an Act of Parliament. ([aéronef
      canadien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Canadian permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Canadian permit]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a permit that is
      issued under subsection 127(1) or 128(2). ([permis
      canadien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Canadian ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Canadian ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a ship that is
      registered under an Act of Parliament. ([navire
      canadien]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[condition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[condition]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, in respect of a permit,
      any term or condition of the permit. ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[contracting party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[contracting party]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a state that is a
      contracting party to the Convention or the Protocol. ([partie
      contractante]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Convention]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the Convention on the
      Prevention of Marine Pollution by Dumping of Wastes and Other
      Matter signed by Canada on December 29, 1972, as amended from time
      to time. ([Convention]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[disposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[disposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

      - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the disposal of a substance at sea from a ship,
        an aircraft, a platform or another structure,

      - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal of dredged material into the sea
        from any source not mentioned in paragraph (a),

      - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the storage on the seabed, in the subsoil of
        the seabed or on the ice in any area of the sea of a substance
        that comes from a ship, an aircraft, a platform or another
        structure,

      - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the deposit of a substance on the ice in an
        area of the sea,

      - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the disposal at sea of a ship or aircraft,

      - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the disposal or abandonment at sea of a
        platform or another structure, and

      - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other act or omission that constitutes a
        disposal under regulations made under paragraph 135(3)(c),

      but does not include

      - [(h)]{.lawlabel} a disposal of a substance that is incidental to
        or derived from the normal operations of a ship, an aircraft, a
        platform or another structure or of any equipment on a ship, an
        aircraft, a platform or another structure, other than the
        disposal of substances from a ship, an aircraft, a platform or
        another structure operated for the purpose of disposing of such
        substances at sea,

      - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the placement of a substance for a purpose
        other than its mere disposal if the placement is not contrary to
        the purposes of this Division and the aims of the Convention or
        the Protocol,

      - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the abandonment of any matter, such as a cable,
        pipeline or research device, placed on the seabed or in the
        subsoil of the seabed for a purpose other than its mere
        disposal, or

      - [(k)]{.lawlabel} a discharge or storage directly arising from,
        or directly related to, the exploration for, exploitation of and
        associated off-shore processing of seabed mineral
        resources. ([immersion]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[incineration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[incineration]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the combustion of a
      substance on board a ship, a platform or another structure at sea
      for the purpose of its thermal
      destruction. ([incinération]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[master]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[master]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the person in command or
      charge of a ship, but does not include a licensed pilot, within
      the meaning of section 1.1 of the [Pilotage
      Act](/eng/acts/P-14). ([capitaine]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[owner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[owner]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}, in relation to any ship, aircraft,
      platform or other structure, includes the person who has the
      possession or use, by law or contract, of the ship, aircraft,
      platform or other structure. ([propriétaire]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

  [[Protocol]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[Protocol]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means the 1996 Protocol to the
      Convention on the Prevention of Marine Pollution by Dumping of
      Wastes and Other Matter, 1972, as amended from time to
      time. ([Protocole]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes a vessel, boat or craft
      designed, used or capable of being used solely or partly for
      marine navigation, without regard to its method or lack of
      propulsion, and includes an air cushion
      vehicle. ([navire]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

  [[structure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[structure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a structure that is made
      by a person. ([Version anglaise
      seulement]{.DefinitionOtherLangOnly})

  [[waste or other matter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  :   [[waste or other matter]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means waste or other
      matter listed in Schedule 5. ([déchets ou autres
      matières]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

- Definition of [[sea]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In this Division and in Part 10,
  [[sea]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the territorial sea of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the internal waters of Canada, excluding all the
    rivers, lakes and other fresh waters in Canada and the St. Lawrence
    River as far seaward as the straight lines drawn

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} from Cap-des-Rosiers to the western-most point of
      Anticosti Island, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} from Anticosti Island to the north shore of the
      St. Lawrence River along the meridian of longitude sixty-three
      degrees west;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} any exclusive economic zone that may be created by
    Canada;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the arctic waters within the meaning of section 2
    of the [Arctic Waters Pollution Prevention Act](/eng/acts/A-12);

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} an area of the sea adjacent to the areas referred
    to in paragraphs (a) to (d) that is specified under paragraph
    135(1)(g);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} an area of the sea under the jurisdiction of a
    foreign state, other than its internal waters; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} an area of the sea, other than the internal waters
    of a foreign state, not included in the areas of the sea referred to
    in paragraphs (a) to (f).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 122
- 2005, c. 23, ss. 18, 49
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-122-20050628.html "Link to previous version of section 122"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Purpose]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64684 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Purpose

**[[122.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-122.1 .sectionLabel}** The purpose of this
Division is to protect the marine environment, particularly by
implementing the Convention and the Protocol.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 23, s. 19
:::

#### [Prohibitions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64689 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imports for disposal in waters under
Canadian jurisdiction

- **[[123]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-123 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person or ship shall import a substance for disposal in an area of the
  sea referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Export for disposal in waters under
  foreign jurisdiction

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person or ship shall export a substance for
  disposal in an area of the sea under the jurisdiction of a foreign
  state or in its internal waters.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 123
- 2005, c. 23, s. 20
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-123-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 123"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loading in Canada for disposal at sea

- **[[124]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-124 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall, in Canada, load a substance onto any ship, aircraft,
  platform or other structure for the purpose of disposal in an area of
  the sea referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) and (g)
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the loading is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Loading in Canada for disposal at sea

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} No ship shall, in Canada, load a substance onto
  itself for the purpose of disposal in an area of the sea referred to
  in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) and (g) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the loading is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibility of master and pilot in
  Canada

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The master of a ship or pilot in command of an
  aircraft shall not permit a substance to be loaded onto their ship or
  aircraft in Canada for the purpose of disposal in an area of the sea
  referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) and (g) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the loading is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Responsibility of master and pilot
  outside Canada

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The master of a Canadian ship or pilot in command of
  a Canadian aircraft shall not permit a substance to be loaded onto
  their ship or aircraft outside Canada for the purpose of disposal at
  sea.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (3) does not apply where

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal occurs in an area of the sea referred
    to in paragraph 122(2)(g) or in an area of the sea that is under the
    jurisdiction of the foreign state where the substance is loaded;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the disposal occurs in an area of the sea
    referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g) and the loading occurs in the
    territory of a foreign state that is a contracting party, the
    loading and disposal are done in accordance with a permit issued
    under the Convention or the Protocol by that state;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the disposal occurs in an area of the sea
    referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g) and the loading occurs in the
    territory of a foreign state that is not a contracting party, the
    loading and disposal are done in accordance with a Canadian permit;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if the disposal occurs in an area of the sea under
    the jurisdiction of a foreign state that is a contracting party, the
    loading and disposal are done in accordance with a permit issued
    under the Convention or the Protocol by that state; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} if the disposal occurs in an area of the sea under
    the jurisdiction of a foreign state that is not a contracting party,
    the loading is done in accordance with a Canadian permit and the
    disposal is authorized by that state.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 124
- 2005, c. 23, s. 21
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-124-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 124"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal in waters under Canadian
jurisdiction

- **[[125]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-125 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person or ship shall dispose of a substance in an area of the sea
  referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal from Canadian ship, etc., in
  waters that are not under the jurisdiction of any state

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall dispose of a substance from a
  Canadian ship, a Canadian aircraft or a Canadian platform or other
  structure in an area of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g)
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit or, if the substance was loaded in the territory of a state
    that is a contracting party, a permit issued under the Convention or
    the Protocol by that state.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal by Canadian ship in waters
  that are not under the jurisdiction of any state

  [(2.1)]{.lawlabel} No Canadian ship shall dispose of a substance in an
  area of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal is done in accordance with a Canadian
    permit or, if the substance was loaded in the territory of a state
    that is a contracting party, a permit issued under the Convention or
    the Protocol by that state.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal from Canadian ship, etc., in
  waters under foreign jurisdiction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall dispose of a substance from a
  Canadian ship, a Canadian aircraft or a Canadian platform or other
  structure in an area of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(f)
  unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the substance was loaded in the foreign state that
    has jurisdiction over that area;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state is a contracting party, the
    disposal is done in accordance with a permit issued under the
    Convention or the Protocol by that contracting party; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state is not a contracting party,
    that state has authorized the disposal and it is done in accordance
    with a Canadian permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal by Canadian ship in waters
  under foreign jurisdiction

  [(3.1)]{.lawlabel} No Canadian ship shall dispose of a substance in an
  area of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(f) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste or other matter;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the substance was loaded in the foreign state that
    has jurisdiction over that area;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state is a contracting party, the
    disposal is done in accordance with a permit issued under the
    Convention or the Protocol by that contracting party; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state is not a contracting party,
    that state has authorized the disposal and it is done in accordance
    with a Canadian permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of Canadian ship, etc., in
  waters that are not under the jurisdiction of any state

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall dispose of a Canadian ship, a
  Canadian aircraft or a Canadian platform or other structure in an area
  of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g) unless the disposal is
  done in accordance with a Canadian permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Disposal of Canadian ship etc., in
  waters under foreign jurisdiction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person shall dispose of a Canadian ship, a
  Canadian aircraft or a Canadian platform or other structure in an area
  of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(f) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state that has jurisdiction over
    that area is a contracting party, the disposal is done in accordance
    with a permit issued under the Convention or the Protocol by that
    contracting party; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the foreign state that has jurisdiction over
    that area is not a contracting party, that state has authorized the
    disposal and it is done in accordance with a Canadian permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} This section does not apply in respect of any
  disposal that is authorized under the [Canada Shipping Act,
  2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 125
- 2005, c. 23, ss. 22, 50
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-125-20050628.html "Link to previous version of section 125"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incineration

- **[[126]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-126 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall incinerate a substance on board a ship, a platform or
  another structure in an area of the sea referred to in any of
  paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste generated on board the ship,
    platform or other structure during normal operations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the incineration is done in accordance with a
    permit issued under subsection 128(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incineration by ship

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} No ship shall incinerate a substance on board the
  ship in an area of the sea referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a)
  to (e) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste generated on board the ship
    during normal operations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the incineration is done in accordance with a
    permit issued under subsection 128(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incineration in waters under foreign
  jurisdiction, etc.

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall incinerate a substance on board a
  Canadian ship or a Canadian platform or other structure in an area of
  the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(f) or (g) unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste generated on board the
    Canadian ship or the Canadian platform or other structure during
    normal operations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the incineration is done in accordance with a
    permit issued under subsection 128(2).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incineration by Canadian ship in waters
  under foreign jurisdiction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No Canadian ship shall, in an area of the sea
  referred to in paragraph 122(2)(f) or (g), incinerate a substance on
  board the ship unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance is waste generated on board the ship
    during normal operations; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the incineration is done in accordance with a
    permit issued under subsection 128(2).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 126
- 2005, c. 23, s. 23
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-126-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 126"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Permits]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64776 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permit

- **[[127]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-127 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may, on application, issue a permit authorizing the loading
  for disposal and disposal of waste or other matter and, subject to the
  regulations, renew it no more than four times.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The application must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in the prescribed form;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contain the information that may be prescribed or
    that may be required by the Minister for the purpose of complying
    with Schedule 6;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied by the prescribed fees; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied by evidence that notice of the
    application was published in a newspaper circulating in the vicinity
    of the loading or disposal described in the application or in any
    other publication specified by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Factors for consideration

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before issuing a permit under subsection (1) or
  renewing it, the Minister shall comply with Schedule 6 and shall take
  into account any factors that the Minister considers necessary.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 127
- 2012, c. 19, s. 157
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-127-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 127"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

- **[[128]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-128
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Paragraphs 125(1)(a), (2)(a),
  (2.1)(a), (3)(a) and (3.1)(a) do not apply if a permit is issued under
  this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permits for emergency disposal

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may, on application, issue a permit to
  dispose of or incinerate a substance if the Minister is of the opinion
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the disposal or incineration of a certain quantity
    of the substance is necessary to avert an emergency that poses an
    unacceptable risk relating to the environment or to human health;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is no other feasible solution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An application for a permit must

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} be in the prescribed form;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} contain the information that may be prescribed or
    that may be required by the Minister for the purpose of complying
    with Schedule 6;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} be accompanied by the prescribed fees; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4), be accompanied by
    evidence that notice of the application was published in a newspaper
    circulating in the vicinity of the loading, disposal or incineration
    described in the application or in any other publication specified
    by the Minister.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may permit the publication referred to
  in paragraph (3)(d) to be made at any time after the application is
  made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} offer to consult with any foreign state that is
    likely to be affected by the disposal or incineration and with the
    International Maritime Organization; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} endeavour to follow any recommendations that are
    received from the International Maritime Organization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall inform the International Maritime
  Organization of any action taken under this section.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 128
- 2005, c. 23, s. 24
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-128-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 128"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions of permit

- **[[129]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-129 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  Canadian permit shall contain any conditions that the Minister
  considers necessary for the protection of marine life, any legitimate
  uses of the sea or human life, including conditions relating to the
  following:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the nature and quantity of the substance for
    loading, disposal or incineration;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the method and frequency of the disposal or
    incineration authorized including, if necessary, the date or dates
    on which disposal or incineration is authorized;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the manner of loading and stowing the substance
    authorized for disposal or incineration;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the site at which disposal or incineration may take
    place;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the route to be followed by the ship or aircraft
    transporting the substance to the disposal or incineration site;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any special precautions to be taken respecting the
    loading, transporting, disposal or incineration of the substance;
    and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the monitoring of the disposal, the incineration
    and the disposal site to determine the effects of the disposal on
    the environment and human life.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duration of permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A Canadian permit shall specify that it is valid for
  a particular date or dates or for a particular period that shall not
  exceed one year. For greater certainty, this subsection applies to
  each renewal of a permit issued under subsection 127(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers to suspend, revoke or vary
  permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may suspend or revoke a Canadian permit
  or vary its conditions where, having regard to Schedule 6 or the
  establishment of, or any report of, a board of review under section
  333, the Minister considers it advisable to do so.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 129
- 2012, c. 19, s. 158
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-129-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 129"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Exception for Safety Reasons]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64829 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

- **[[130]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-130
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Despite the other provisions of this
  Division, a person may dispose of a substance if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} it is necessary to avert a danger to human life or
    to a ship, an aircraft, a platform or another structure at sea in
    situations caused by stress of weather or in any other case that
    constitutes a danger to human life or a threat to a ship, an
    aircraft, a platform or another structure at sea;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the disposal appears to be the only way of averting
    the danger or threat; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} it is probable that the damage caused by the
    disposal would be less than would otherwise occur.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Danger to be minimized

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Any disposal under subsection (1) shall be carried
  out in a manner that minimizes, as far as possible, danger to human
  life and damage to the marine environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Negligence not a defence

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) does not apply if the danger was
  caused or contributed to by the person's negligent act or omission.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Duty to report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If disposal takes place under subsection (1), the
  master of the ship, the pilot in command of the aircraft or the person
  in charge of the platform or other structure shall report the disposal
  without delay to an enforcement officer or any other person whom the
  Governor in Council may, by order, designate, at the location and in
  the manner that may be prescribed, and the report shall contain any
  information that may be prescribed.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 130
- 2005, c. 23, s. 25(E)
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-130-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 130"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Fisheries Act](/eng/acts/F-14) not
applicable

**[[131]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-131 .sectionLabel}** If a person disposes of
a substance in accordance with the conditions of a Canadian permit or
section 130, subsection 36(3) of the [Fisheries Act](/eng/acts/F-14) is
not applicable.

#### [Site Monitoring]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64846 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Monitoring of sites

**[[132]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-132 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
monitor sites selected by the Minister that are used for disposal or
incineration at sea.

#### [Publication]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64849 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

- **[[133]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-133 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} When
  issuing a Canadian permit or varying any of its conditions or renewing
  a permit issued under subsection 127(1), the Minister shall publish
  the text of the permit, the varied condition or the renewed permit, as
  the case may be, in the Environmental Registry.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication before disposal or loading

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Publication under subsection (1) shall be made

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a permit issued under subsection
    128(2), as soon as possible after the permit is issued; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in every other case, at least seven days before the
    effective date of the permit, the variation of its conditions or its
    renewal.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 133

#### [Notice of Objection]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64859 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice of objection

- **[[134]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-134 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Any
  person may file with the Minister a notice of objection requesting
  that a board of review be established under section 333 and stating
  the reasons for the objection, if the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} issues or refuses a Canadian permit;

  - [(a.1)]{.lawlabel} renews or refuses to renew a permit issued under
    subsection 127(1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} suspends or revokes a Canadian permit or varies its
    conditions, otherwise than in accordance with the recommendations of
    a report of a board of review established under section 333 in
    respect of the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Time for filing notice of objection

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice of objection shall be filed within seven
  days after

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the date the text of the Canadian permit or the
    permit renewed under subsection 127(1), as the case may be, is
    published in the Environmental Registry; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the date the person receives a notice from the
    Minister that the Canadian permit has been refused, suspended or
    revoked, that its conditions have been varied or that the renewal of
    a permit issued under subsection 127(1) has been refused.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 134
- 2012, c. 19, s. 160
:::

::: PITLink
