Version](section-134-20120629.html "Link to previous version of section 134"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64872 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[135]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-135 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of this
  Division and Schedule 6, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for carrying out and giving effect to the
    provisions of the Convention or the Protocol;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} defining the expression [[Canadian platform or
    other structure]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm};

  - [(b.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting time limits for issuing permits under
    subsection 127(1) or for refusing to issue them, specifying the
    circumstances under which any of those time limits do not apply and
    authorizing the Minister to extend any of those time limits or to
    decide that a time limit does not apply when the Minister considers
    that it is appropriate to do so;

  - [(b.2)]{.lawlabel} respecting the renewal of permits under
    subsection 127(1), including regulations respecting time limits for
    renewing them or for refusing to renew them, specifying the
    circumstances under which any of those time limits do not apply and
    authorizing the Minister to extend any of those time limits or to
    decide that a time limit does not apply when the Minister considers
    that it is appropriate to do so;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the report referred to in subsection
    130(4);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conduct of sampling, analyses,
    tests, measurements or monitoring;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions, test procedures and
    laboratory practices to be followed for sampling, analysing,
    testing, measuring or monitoring;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the monitoring of disposal sites;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} specifying, for the purpose of paragraph 122(2)(e),
    areas of the sea adjacent to areas referred to in any of paragraphs
    122(2)(a) to (d);

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} limiting the quantity or concentration of a
    substance contained in waste or other matter for disposal; and

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} prescribing any other thing that by this Division
    is to be prescribed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Amendments to Schedules 5 and 6

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister, by order, amend Schedules 5 and 6.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the form of an application for a
    Canadian permit or for the renewal of a permit issued under
    subsection 127(1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} specifying the information required to be contained
    in or to accompany an application referred to in paragraph (a);

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} specifying acts or omissions that constitute a
    disposal for the purposes of paragraph (g) of the definition
    [[disposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection 122(1);

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} specifying, for the purposes of paragraph (h) of
    the definition [[disposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection
    122(1), the operations that are deemed to be, or deemed not to be,
    the normal operations of a ship, an aircraft, a platform or another
    structure or of any equipment on a ship, an aircraft, a platform or
    another structure;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} specifying, for the purposes of subsections 125(1)
    to (3.1), disposals that are deemed to be, or deemed not to be,
    disposals of substances referred to in paragraph (h) of the
    definition [[disposal]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} in subsection 122(1),
    which specifications may refer, among other things, to any quantity
    or concentration of any substance or to any place or area; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} specifying, for the purposes of section 126, the
    operations that are deemed to be, or deemed not to be, normal
    operations of ships, including Canadian ships.

::: HistoricalNote

- 2012, c. 19, s. 161
:::

#### [Costs and Expenses of the Crown]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64899 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Costs and expenses recoverable

**[[136]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-136 .sectionLabel}** If the Minister directs
an action to be taken by or on behalf of Her Majesty in right of Canada
to remedy a condition or mitigate damage resulting from an offence under
this Act that arises out of this Division, the costs and expenses of and
incidental to taking that action, to the extent that they can be
established to have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances, are
recoverable by Her Majesty in right of Canada from the person or ship
that committed the offence with costs in proceedings brought or taken
therefor in the name of Her Majesty in any court of competent
jurisdiction.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 136
- 2005, c. 23, s. 27(E)
:::

[Previous
Version](section-136-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 136"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Service of Documents]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64904 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Manner of service

**[[137]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-137 .sectionLabel}** Except where otherwise
provided by any rules of the Federal Court that are applicable to
proceedings arising out of this Division, any document that, for the
purposes of any such proceedings, is to be served on a person may be
served

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in any case, by delivering a copy of the document
  personally to the person to be served or, if the person cannot be
  found, by leaving a copy at their latest known address;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the document is to be served on the master of a
  ship or on any other person employed on a ship and service cannot
  reasonably be effected in the manner provided in paragraph (a), by
  leaving a copy of the document for the master or other person on board
  the ship with the person who is, or appears to be, in command or
  charge of the ship;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} if the document is to be served on the pilot in
  command of an aircraft and service cannot reasonably be effected in
  the manner provided in paragraph (a), by leaving a copy of the
  document with the person who is, or appears to be, in charge of the
  aircraft; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} if the document is to be served on a person in their
  capacity as owner or master of a ship or owner or pilot in command of
  an aircraft and service cannot reasonably be effected in the manner
  provided in paragraph (a) and the ship or aircraft is within an area
  of the sea referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) or in
  Canada, by leaving a copy of the document with any agent of the owner
  residing in Canada or, where no such agent is known or can be found,
  by affixing a copy of it to a prominent part of the ship or aircraft.

### [DIVISION 4]{.HLabel2}[Fuels]{.HTitleText2} {#h-64911 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64912 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[138]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-138 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Division.

[[engine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[engine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a device that transforms one
    form of energy into another. ([moteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[national fuels mark]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[national fuels mark]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a mark established
    by regulation for use in respect of fuels. ([marque
    nationale]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

#### [General Requirements for Fuels]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64917 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[139]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-139 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall produce, import or sell a fuel that does not meet the
  prescribed requirements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} A person does not contravene subsection (1) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the fuel is in transit through Canada, from a place
    outside Canada to another place outside Canada, and there is written
    evidence establishing that the fuel is in transit;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} subject to the regulations, the fuel is produced or
    sold for export and there is written evidence establishing that the
    fuel will be exported;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} subject to the regulations, the fuel is being
    produced or imported and there is written evidence establishing that
    the fuel will meet the requirements of subsection (1) before the
    fuel is used or sold;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to the regulations, the fuel is being
    imported in a fuel tank that supplies the engine of a conveyance
    that is used for transportation by water, land or air; or

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} that person is exempted from the application of
    that subsection by a regulation made under subsection 140(3).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 139
- 2008, c. 31, s. 1
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-139-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 139"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[140]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-140 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for carrying out the purposes of section 139 and may make
  regulations respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the concentrations or quantities of an element,
    component or additive in a fuel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the physical or chemical properties of a fuel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the characteristics of a fuel, based on a formula
    related to the fuel's properties or conditions of use;

  - [(c.1)]{.lawlabel} the blending of fuels;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the transfer and handling of a fuel;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the keeping of books and records by persons who
    produce, sell or import fuel or blend fuels;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the auditing of the books and records and the
    submission of audit reports and copies of the books and records;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the submission by persons who produce, sell or
    import fuel or blend fuels of information regarding

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the fuel and any element, component or additive
      contained in the fuel,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} any physical or chemical property of the fuel or
      any substance intended for use as an additive to the fuel,

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} the adverse effects from the use of the fuel,
      or any additive contained in the fuel, on the environment, on
      human life or health, on combustion technology and on emission
      control equipment, and

    - [(iv)]{.lawlabel} the techniques that may be used to detect and
      measure elements, components, additives and physical and chemical
      properties;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of sampling, analyses, tests,
    measurements or monitoring of fuels and additives and the submission
    of the results;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the submission of samples of fuels and additives;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, test procedures and laboratory
    practices to be followed for conducting sampling, analyses, tests,
    measurements or monitoring; and

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the submission of reports on the quantity of fuel
    produced, imported or sold for export.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Significant contribution

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may make a regulation under
  any of paragraphs (1)(a) to (d) if the Governor in Council is of the
  opinion that the regulation could make a significant contribution to
  the prevention of, or reduction in, air pollution resulting from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} directly or indirectly, the fuel or any of its
    components; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fuel's effect on the operation, performance or
    introduction of combustion or other engine technology or emission
    control equipment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of
  the Minister, make regulations exempting from the application of
  subsection 139(1) any producer or importer in respect of any fuel that
  they produce or import in quantities of less than 400 m^3^ per year.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Before recommending a regulation to the Governor in
  Council under subsection (1), the Minister shall offer to consult with
  the government of a province and the members of the Committee who are
  representatives of aboriginal governments and may consult with a
  government department or agency, aboriginal people, representatives of
  industry and labour and municipal authorities or with persons
  interested in the quality of the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (4), the Minister may recommend a regulation to the Governor in
  Council under subsection (1) if the offer to consult is not accepted
  by the government of a province or members of the Committee who are
  representatives of aboriginal governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Review

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Within one year after this subsection comes into
  force and every two years thereafter, a comprehensive review of the
  environmental and economic aspects of biofuel production in Canada
  should be undertaken by such committee of the Senate, of the House of
  Commons or of both Houses of Parliament as may be designated or
  established by the Senate or the House of Commons, or by both Houses
  of Parliament, as the case may be, for that purpose.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The committee referred to in subsection (6) should,
  within one year after a review is undertaken pursuant to that
  subsection, submit a report on the review to Parliament, including a
  statement of any recommendations that the committee makes in respect
  of biofuel production in Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 140
- 2008, c. 31, s. 2
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-140-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 140"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [National Fuels Marks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-64965 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nature of mark

- **[[141]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-141 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  national fuels marks are national trademarks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The exclusive property in and, except as otherwise
  provided in this Division, the right to the use of the national fuels
  marks are hereby vested in Her Majesty in right of Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 141
- [2014, c. 20, s. 366(E)](#1034903-1145619){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1034903-1145619.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-141-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 141"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

- **[[142]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-142 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall use a national fuels mark except in accordance with this
  Division and the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confusing marks

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall use any other mark in such a manner
  that it is likely to be mistaken for a national fuels mark.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for use

**[[143]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-143 .sectionLabel}** A person may use a
national fuels mark in respect of a prescribed fuel if

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the use is authorized by the Minister;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fuel conforms to the requirements for that fuel
  provided for by regulations made under section 145 and any
  requirements that are applicable to that fuel and that may be provided
  for by regulations made under subsection 93(1) or 140(1);

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} evidence of such conformity has been obtained and
  produced in accordance with the regulations; and

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} prescribed information relating to the fuel has been
  submitted to the Minister in the prescribed manner.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cross-boundary shipments

- **[[144]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-144 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall import, or transport within Canada, a prescribed fuel if
  the requirements set out in paragraphs 143(b) to (d) are not met.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by the regulations,
  subsection (1) does not apply if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the requirements are met before the fuel is used or
    sold; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the fuel is being used in a fuel tank that supplies
    the engine of a conveyance that is used for transportation by water,
    land or air.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[145]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-145 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of sections
  141 to 144 and 147, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishing the national fuels marks;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} determining the fuels for which a national fuels
    mark may be used;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting any condition or requirement that must
    be met for a national fuels mark to be used if, in the opinion of
    the Governor in Council, regulations respecting that condition or
    requirement may not be made under section 140;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions and procedures for
    obtaining authorization to use a national fuels mark;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information or other evidence
    necessary under sections 143 and 144; and

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribing or providing for anything that by
    sections 141 to 144 and 147 is to be prescribed or provided for by
    the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before recommending a regulation to the Governor in
  Council under subsection (1), the Minister shall offer to consult with
  the government of a province and the members of the Committee who are
  representatives of aboriginal governments and may consult with a
  government department or agency, aboriginal people, representatives of
  industry and labour and municipal authorities or with persons
  interested in the quality of the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (2), the Minister may recommend a regulation to the Governor in
  Council under subsection (1) if the offer to consult is not accepted
  by the government of a province or members of the Committee who are
  representatives of aboriginal governments.

**[[146]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-146 .sectionLabel}** [\[Repealed, 2008, c.
31, s. 3\]]{.Repealed}

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-146-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 146"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Temporary Waivers]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65003 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Temporary waiver

**[[147]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-147 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may, in
prescribed circumstances, grant a temporary waiver from any of the
requirements of a regulation made under section 140 or 145 on any
conditions and for any period that may be determined by the Minister.

#### [Remedial Measures]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65006 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedial measures

- **[[148]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-148 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If,
  in respect of a fuel, there is a contravention of this Division or a
  regulation made under this Division, the Minister may, in writing,
  direct a producer, processor, importer, retailer or distributor of the
  fuel to take any or all of the following measures in a manner and
  within the period directed by the Minister:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} give public notice of the relevant characteristics
    of the fuel and of any danger to the environment or to human life or
    health that might be posed by the fuel;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} mail a notice as described in paragraph (a) to
    producers, processors, importers, retailers or distributors of the
    fuel;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} mail a notice as described in paragraph (a) to
    persons to whom the fuel is known to have been delivered or sold;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} replace the fuel with fuel that meets the
    applicable requirements;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} accept the return of the fuel from the purchaser
    and refund the purchase price;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} take other measures to mitigate the effect of the
    contravention on the environment or on human life or health; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} report to the Minister on the steps taken in
    satisfaction of any direction under paragraphs (a) to (f).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention of Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If a person fails to take any measures required under
  paragraph (1)(a), (b), (c) or (f), the Minister may take those
  measures or cause them to be taken.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of costs

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Her Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs
  and expenses of and incidental to taking any measures under
  subsection (2) from the person referred to in that subsection.

### [DIVISION 5]{.HLabel2}[Vehicle, Engine and Equipment Emissions]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65021 .Subheading}

#### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65022 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[149]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-149 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Division and in Part 10 as it relates to the
enforcement of this Division.

[[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[company]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a person who

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} is engaged in the business of manufacturing
      vehicles, engines or equipment in Canada;

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} is engaged in the business of selling to other
      persons, for the purpose of resale by those persons, vehicles,
      engines or equipment obtained directly from a person described in
      paragraph (a) or the agent of such a person; or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} imports any vehicle, engine or equipment into
      Canada for the purpose of sale. ([entreprise]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[engine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[engine]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any prescribed internal
    combustion engine, but does not include

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an engine designed to propel an aircraft as
      defined in subsection 3(1) of the [Aeronautics
      Act](/eng/acts/A-2);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} an engine designed to propel rolling stock as
      defined in section 6 of the [Canada Transportation
      Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4); or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a marine compression-ignition engine that is
      rated at 37 kW or more and is designed to propel a
      vessel. ([moteur]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[equipment]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any prescribed equipment
    that is designed for use in or on a vehicle or
    engine. ([équipement]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[manufacture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[manufacture]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any process of
    assembling or altering any vehicle, engine or equipment before its
    sale to the first retail purchaser. ([fabrication]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"} ou [construction]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[national emissions mark]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[national emissions mark]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a mark
    established by regulation for use in respect of emissions from
    vehicles, engines or equipment. ([marque nationale]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[standard]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a standard that governs the
    design, construction, functioning or marking of vehicles, engines or
    equipment for the purpose of controlling or monitoring their
    emissions. ([norme]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vehicle]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means any prescribed self-propelled
    vehicle, but does not include

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an aircraft as defined in subsection 3(1) of the
      [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2);

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} rolling stock as defined in section 6 of the
      [Canada Transportation Act](/eng/acts/C-10.4); or

    - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a vessel that is fitted, for the purpose of
      propulsion, with a marine compression-ignition engine that is
      rated at 37 kW or more. ([véhicule]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[vessel]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a boat, ship or craft designed,
    used or capable of being used solely or partly for navigation in,
    on, through or immediately above water. ([bâtiment]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 149
- 2001, c. 26, s. 331
:::

#### [National Emissions Marks]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65044 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Nature of marks

- **[[150]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-150 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  national emissions marks are national trademarks.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Property rights

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The exclusive property in and, except as otherwise
  provided in this Division, the right to use a national emissions mark
  are hereby vested in Her Majesty in right of Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall use a national emissions mark except
  in accordance with this Division and the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Confusing marks

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall use any other mark in such a manner
  that it is likely to be mistaken for a national emissions mark.

::: HistoricalNote

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of marks

**[[151]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-151 .sectionLabel}** A company authorized by
the Minister may, subject to this Division and the regulations, apply a
national emissions mark to vehicles, engines or equipment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Transportation within Canada

**[[152]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-152 .sectionLabel}** No company shall
transport within Canada a prescribed vehicle, engine or equipment that
does not have a national emissions mark applied to it.

#### [Vehicle, Engine and Equipment Standards]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65058 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance by companies

- **[[153]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-153 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  company shall apply a national emissions mark to any vehicle, engine
  or equipment, sell any vehicle, engine or equipment to which a
  national emissions mark has been applied or import any vehicle, engine
  or equipment unless

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle, engine or equipment conforms to the
    standards prescribed for vehicles, engines or equipment of its class
    at the time its main assembly or manufacture was completed;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} evidence of such conformity has been obtained and
    produced in the prescribed form and manner or, if the regulations so
    provide, in a form and manner satisfactory to the Minister;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} prescribed information relating to standards for
    emissions from the vehicle, engine or equipment has been submitted
    to the Minister in the prescribed manner;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} information is marked on the vehicle, engine or
    equipment in accordance with the regulations;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} if required by the regulations, prescribed
    documentation or accessories accompany the vehicle, engine or
    equipment;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} prescribed information relating to the operation or
    use of the vehicle, engine or equipment is disseminated in the
    prescribed form and manner;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} records are maintained and furnished in the
    prescribed form and manner in relation to the design, manufacture,
    testing and field performance of the vehicle, engine or equipment,
    for the purpose of

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} enabling an enforcement officer to determine
      whether the vehicle, engine or equipment conforms to all
      prescribed standards applicable to it, and

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} facilitating the identification and analysis of
      defects referred to in subsection 157(1); and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} in the case of engines and equipment, the company
    maintains a registration system in the prescribed form and manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exception

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Except as otherwise provided by the regulations,
  subsection (1) does not apply with respect to the application of a
  national emissions mark or an importation referred to in that
  subsection if the requirements under that subsection are met before
  the vehicle, engine or equipment leaves the possession or control of
  the company and, in the case of a vehicle, before the vehicle is
  presented for registration under the laws of a province or an
  aboriginal government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Certification by foreign agency

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Any vehicle, engine or equipment is deemed to conform
  to a prescribed standard if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the regulations provide that an enactment of a
    foreign government corresponds to that standard; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a prescribed agency of that government has
    certified that the vehicle, engine or equipment conforms to the
    enactment as applied by the agency, unless the Minister determines
    otherwise.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Compliance on importation

**[[154]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-154 .sectionLabel}** No person shall import
any vehicle, engine or equipment of a prescribed class unless the
requirements of paragraphs 153(1)(a), (b), (d) and (e) are met in
respect of the vehicle, engine or equipment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exceptions for certain importations

- **[[155]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-155
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Sections 153 and 154 do not apply in
  respect of the importation of any vehicle, engine or equipment if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the person importing the vehicle, engine or
    equipment makes a declaration in the prescribed form and manner that
    the vehicle, engine or equipment will be used in Canada solely for
    purposes of exhibition, demonstration, evaluation or testing and
    will remain in Canada for not longer than one year or any other
    period that the Minister specifies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle, engine or equipment is in transit
    through Canada, from a place outside Canada, to another place
    outside Canada and is accompanied by written evidence establishing
    that the vehicle, engine or equipment will not be sold or used in
    Canada; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle, engine or equipment is being imported
    exclusively for use by a visitor to Canada or by a person passing
    through Canada to another country.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Vehicle from United States or Mexico

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Sections 153 and 154 do not apply in respect of the
  importation of a vehicle that has been sold at the retail level in the
  United States, or that is a prescribed vehicle from Mexico, if the
  vehicle satisfies the conditions specified in the regulations, if any,
  and if the person importing it makes a declaration in the prescribed
  form and manner that,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within the prescribed period, the applicable
    requirements of those sections will be met and, if required by the
    regulations, the vehicle will be inspected in accordance with them;
    and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} before the vehicle is presented for licensing under
    the laws of a province or an aboriginal government, the vehicle will
    be certified, in accordance with the regulations, as conforming to
    the applicable requirements of those sections.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Change in standard since manufacture

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Sections 153 and 154 do not apply in respect of the
  importation of any vehicle, engine or equipment that does not conform
  to a standard prescribed for its class at the time of its main
  assembly or manufacture if, at the time of its importation, that
  standard is no longer in effect and

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the vehicle, engine or equipment conforms to the
    corresponding standard prescribed for its class at that time; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} there is no corresponding standard at that time.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Imported vehicle or engine

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A vehicle or engine that is imported and for which
  there is no prescribed standard must conform to the standard
  prescribed for the class of equivalent vehicles or engines before
  presentation for registration under the laws of a province or an
  aboriginal government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declarations binding

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} No person who makes a declaration referred to in
  paragraph (1)(a) or subsection (2), or provides evidence referred to
  in paragraph (1)(b), in respect of any vehicle, engine or equipment
  shall use or dispose of the vehicle, engine or equipment in a manner
  contrary to the terms of that declaration or evidence.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Record keeping

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Every person who makes a declaration referred to in
  paragraph (1)(a) or subsection (2), or provides evidence referred to
  in paragraph (1)(b), in respect of any vehicle, engine or equipment
  shall keep a record of the use or disposition of the vehicle, engine
  or equipment in accordance with the regulations.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 155
- 2011, c. 1, s. 4
:::

#### [Vehicle or Engine Exemptions]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65102 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exemption from standards

- **[[156]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-156 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} On
  application by a company in the prescribed form, supported by
  prescribed technical and financial information, the Governor in
  Council may, by order, grant an exemption for a specified period,
  subject to any conditions specified in the order, for any model of
  vehicle or engine manufactured or imported by the company from
  conformity with any prescribed standard applicable to that model if
  conformity with that standard would, in the opinion of the Governor in
  Council,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} create substantial financial hardship for the
    company;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} impede the development of new features for safety,
    emission monitoring or emission control that are equivalent to or
    superior to those that conform to prescribed standards; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} impede the development of new kinds of vehicles,
    engines or vehicle or engine systems or components.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Period and extent of exemption

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An exemption for a model may be granted for a period
  not exceeding

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} three years, if paragraph (1)(a) applies; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} two years, in respect of a stated number of units
    of that model not exceeding 1,000 units, if paragraph (1)(b) or (c)
    applies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Conditions for granting exemption

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An exemption may not be granted for a model if the
  exemption would substantially diminish the control of emissions from
  it or if the company applying for the exemption has not provided
  evidence that satisfies the Governor in Council that it has attempted
  in good faith to bring the model into conformity with all applicable
  prescribed standards.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Further conditions

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} An exemption for substantial financial hardship may
  not be granted under paragraph (1)(a) if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the world production of vehicles or engines
    manufactured by the company, or by the manufacturer of the model
    that is the subject of the application, exceeded 10,000 vehicles or
    engines in the 12-month period beginning two years before the
    beginning of the exemption period; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the total number of vehicles or engines
    manufactured for, or imported into, the Canadian market by the
    company exceeded 1,000 vehicles or engines in that 12-month period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Renewal of exemption

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} On the expiry of the exemption period, a new
  exemption may be granted in accordance with this section.

#### [Notice of Defects]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65121 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obligation to give notice

- **[[157]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-157 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  company that manufactures, sells or imports any vehicle, engine or
  equipment of a class for which standards are prescribed shall, on
  becoming aware of a defect in the design, construction or functioning
  of the vehicle, engine or equipment that affects or is likely to
  affect its compliance with a prescribed standard, cause notice of the
  defect to be given in the prescribed manner to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} each person who has obtained such a vehicle, engine
    or equipment from the company; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} each current owner of such a vehicle, engine or
    equipment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determining owners

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Current owners are to be determined for the purpose
  of subsection (1)

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from a warranty issued by the company with respect
    to the functioning of the vehicle, engine or equipment that has, to
    its knowledge, been given, sold or transferred to the current owner;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vehicle, from registration records
    of a government; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of an engine or equipment, from a
    registration system referred to in paragraph 153(1)(h).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}If notice previously given

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A company is not required to cause notice to be given
  of a defect of which notice has already been given under this section
  or under section 10 of the [Motor Vehicle Safety
  Act](/eng/acts/M-10.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of notice

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is satisfied that the name of the
  current owner of the vehicle, engine or equipment cannot reasonably be
  determined by a company in accordance with subsection (2), the
  Minister may

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} order the company to give notice of the defect by
    publication in the prescribed form for a period of five consecutive
    days in two major daily newspapers in each of the following six
    regions, namely, the Atlantic provinces, Quebec, Ontario, the
    Prairie provinces, British Columbia and the Territories, or by
    dissemination in an alternative medium for any period that the
    Minister determines; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} order that the current owner need not be notified.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A notice required to be given under subsections (1)
  and (4) shall contain, in the form and to the extent prescribed, a
  description of the defect, an evaluation of the pollution risk arising
  from it and directions for correcting it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Particulars to responsible authorities

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} On receiving a notice under subsection (1), the
  Minister shall forward full particulars of the notice to the person
  responsible for vehicle or engine administration in each government.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Follow-up reports

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Every company that causes notice to be given under
  subsection (1) shall submit an initial report and subsequent regular
  reports respecting the defect and its correction in accordance with
  the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Frequency of reports

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Unless the Minister directs otherwise, the reports
  referred to in subsection (7) shall be submitted for a period of two
  years after the day on which notice was given under subsection (1).

#### [Research and Testing]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65147 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Minister

**[[158]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-158 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} conduct any research, studies and evaluations that
  the Minister considers necessary for the administration and
  enforcement of this Division;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} undertake research and development programs for the
  study of the effect of vehicles, engines or equipment or emissions on
  air pollution, energy conservation and the environment and for the
  promotion of measures to control that effect;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establish and operate facilities for the testing of
  vehicles, engines or equipment and components, and acquire test
  equipment for that purpose;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} make the facilities referred to in paragraph (c) and
  all related material, parts and services available to any person; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} publish or otherwise disseminate information relating
  to the activities of the Minister under this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission control tests

- **[[159]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-159 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} At
  the request of the Minister and subject to payment by the Minister of
  the cost of transportation and of rental at a prescribed rate based on
  capital value, a company shall make available for testing any vehicle,
  engine, equipment or component that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} was used in tests conducted by or for the company
    in order to establish information submitted to the Minister under
    paragraph 153(1)(c); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for the purpose of that testing, is equivalent to a
    vehicle, an engine, equipment or a component referred to in
    paragraph (a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Testing by Minister

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may examine and dismantle any vehicle,
  engine, equipment or component made available under subsection (1) and
  conduct all necessary tests to verify the accuracy of tests referred
  to in paragraph (1)(a).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Detention of vehicles or engines

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may not detain any vehicle, engine,
  equipment or component for more than 90 days after completion of the
  tests conducted under subsection (2) unless, before that time,
  proceedings have been instituted in respect of an offence related to
  the vehicle, engine, equipment or component, in which case it may be
  detained until the proceedings are concluded.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 159
- 2009, c. 14, s. 52
:::

[Previous
Version](section-159-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 159"){.PITLink}
:::

#### [Regulations]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65166 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[160]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-160 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for carrying out the purposes and provisions of this
  Division, including regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} respecting emissions and prescribing standards in
    relation to emissions;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} establishing the national emissions marks;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the conditions that must be met for a
    national emissions mark to be used in relation to vehicles, engines
    or equipment or a class of vehicles, engines or equipment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the manner of applying national
    emissions marks;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} prescribing the period for which records referred
    to in paragraph 153(1)(g) or a registration system referred to in
    paragraph 153(1)(h) shall be retained;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting exemptions from sections 153 and 154;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the information to be submitted under
    section 153;

  - [(g.1)]{.lawlabel} respecting inspection and certification referred
    to in subsection 155(2) and, in particular, designating any person
    who is authorized to inspect or certify vehicles; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing or providing for anything that by this
    Division is to be prescribed or provided for by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Progressive application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations prescribing a standard may be made
  applicable in respect of a specified proportion of vehicles, engines
  or equipment of a class before they are made applicable in respect of
  all vehicles, engines or equipment of that class.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 160
- 2011, c. 1, s. 5
:::

Version](section-160-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 160"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[technical standards document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[161]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-161 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  this section, [[technical standards document]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}
  means a document, published in the prescribed manner by authority of
  the Minister, that reproduces in the official languages of Canada an
  enactment of a foreign government with any adaptations of form and
  reference that will facilitate the incorporation of the enactment
  under this section.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Incorporation of document

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For greater certainty, regulations made under this
  Division may incorporate by reference a technical standards document
  as it reads on a prescribed day or as it is amended from time to time
  following the incorporation by reference, and may extend, qualify or
  exclude the application of any provision of the document so
  incorporated.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person is required to comply with a provision of a
  technical standards document incorporated by the regulations until six
  months after the publication of the provision in the prescribed
  manner, if the person continues to comply with the provision for which
  that provision is substituted.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Document not a regulation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A technical standards document is not a regulation
  for the purposes of the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission credits

- **[[162]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-162
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Regulations that prescribe standards
  in relation to emissions may provide for a system of credits based on
  the following principles:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a company may establish that vehicles, engines or
    equipment conform to those standards by applying credits against
    emissions of the vehicles, engines or equipment in the prescribed
    manner and within prescribed limits;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} credits may be obtained by a company in the
    prescribed manner

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} by reference to emissions of the vehicles,
      engines or equipment that more than meet the requirements of those
      standards, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} by the payment of an amount to the Receiver
      General determined at a prescribed rate in relation to emissions
      of the vehicle, engine or equipment; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} credits obtained by reference to emissions may be
    transferred to or from a company in the prescribed manner.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Deemed conformity to standard

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations referred to in subsection (1) may provide
  that any vehicle, engine or equipment is deemed to conform to a
  standard if the application of those regulations to all vehicles,
  engines or equipment of its class sold in Canada and the United States
  would result in that vehicle, engine or equipment so conforming.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Emission report

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Every company shall submit to the Minister, in the
  prescribed form and manner and at the prescribed time, a report
  setting out, with respect to a prescribed period, an account of any
  emission credits obtained or applied by the company and a description
  of each of the following vehicles, engines or pieces of equipment for
  which credits were obtained or applied:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} vehicles, engines and equipment to which the
    company applied a national emissions mark during that period, other
    than those that were exported;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} vehicles, engines and equipment bearing a national
    emissions mark that were sold by the company in Canada during that
    period; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} vehicles, engines and equipment that were imported
    by the company during that period for the purpose of sale in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of report

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If credits applied under this section were obtained
  on the basis of emissions from vehicles, engines or equipment not
  referred to in paragraph (3)(a), (b) or (c), the report submitted in
  respect of the application of those credits shall include a
  description of those vehicles, engines or equipment.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

- **[[163]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-163 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If
  an enactment of a foreign government corresponds to the regulations
  made under this Division and that enactment is amended by that
  government or its operation is affected by a decision of a foreign
  court, the Minister may issue an interim order suspending or modifying
  the operation of the regulations to the extent that they are
  inconsistent with the enactment as amended or given effect.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interim order has effect from the time it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Governor in Council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An interim order ceases to have effect 14 days after
  it is made unless it is approved by the Governor in Council within
  that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of regulation modified by
  an unpublished order

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence consisting
  of a contravention of a regulation to the extent that it is modified
  or suspended by an interim order that, at the time of the alleged
  contravention, had not been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) unless it is proved that, at the
  time of the alleged contravention, the person had been notified of the
  interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of effect

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an interim order ceases to
  have effect on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day it is repealed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day the regulation is amended or repealed to
    give effect to the order, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} one year after the order is made.

#### [Evidence]{.HTitleText3} {#h-65223 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

**[[164]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-164 .sectionLabel}** In a prosecution under
this Act, evidence that any vehicle, engine or equipment bore a name or
mark purporting to be the name or mark of a company engaged in the
business of manufacturing, importing or selling vehicles, engines or
equipment is, in the absence of evidence to the contrary, proof that the
vehicle, engine or equipment was manufactured, imported or sold, as the
case may be, by the company.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Evidence

**[[165]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-165 .sectionLabel}** In a prosecution under
this Act, evidence that any vehicle, engine or equipment bearing a
national emissions mark was manufactured by a company is, in the absence
of evidence to the contrary, proof that the national emissions mark was
applied by the company.

### [DIVISION 6]{.HLabel2}[International Air Pollution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65228 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of international air
pollution

- **[[166]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-166
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  Minister shall act under subsections (2) and (3) only if the Ministers
  have reason to believe that a substance released from a source in
  Canada into the air creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to
  contribute to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} air pollution in a country other than Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} air pollution that violates, or is likely to
    violate, an international agreement binding on Canada in relation to
    the prevention, control or correction of pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation with other governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the source referred to in subsection (1) is not a
  federal source, the Minister shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} consult with the government responsible for the
    area in which the source is situated to determine whether that
    government can prevent, control or correct the air pollution under
    its laws; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the government referred to in paragraph (a) can
    prevent, control or correct the air pollution, offer it an
    opportunity to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial action

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the source referred to in subsection (1) is a
  federal source or if the government referred to in paragraph (2)(a)
  cannot prevent, control or correct the air pollution under its laws or
  does not do so, the Minister shall take at least one of the following
  courses of action:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on approval by the Governor in Council, publish a
    notice under subsection 56(1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recommend regulations to the Governor in Council
    for the purpose of preventing, controlling or correcting the air
    pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reciprocity with other country

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the air pollution referred to in paragraph (1)(a)
  is in a country where Canada does not have substantially the same
  rights with respect to the prevention, control or correction of air
  pollution as that country has under this Division, the Minister shall
  decide whether to act under subsections (2) and (3) or to take no
  action at all.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other factors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When recommending regulations under paragraph (3)(b),
  the Minister shall take into account comments made under subsection
  168(2), notices of objection filed under subsection 332(2) and any
  report of a board of review submitted under subsection 340(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[167]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-167 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations with
respect to a substance released from a source in Canada into the air
that creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to contribute to air
pollution referred to in subsection 166(1) for the purpose of
preventing, controlling or correcting the air pollution, including
regulations respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity or concentration of the substance that
  may be released into the air;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and conditions under which the
  substance may be released into the air, either alone or in combination
  with any other substance;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of books and records for the
  administration of any regulation made under this section;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of sampling, analyses, tests,
  measurements or monitoring of the substance and the submission of the
  results to the Minister; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, test procedures and laboratory
  practices to be followed for conducting sampling, analyses, tests,
  measurements or monitoring of the substance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to other country

- **[[168]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-168 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall advise the government of any country that would be
  affected by or benefit from the regulation before it is published
  under subsection 332(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of a proposed
  regulation under subsection 332(1), any person, including a
  representative of the government of any country that would be affected
  by or benefit from it, may file with the Minister written comments on
  the proposed regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to other governments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At the end of the period of 60 days referred to in
  subsection (2), the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall advise each government referred to in
    paragraph 166(2)(a) of any notice of objection filed under
    subsection 332(2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall publish in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), and may publish in any other
    manner that the Minister considers appropriate, a report or a notice
    of the availability of a report that summarizes how any written
    comments filed under subsection (2) or a notice of objection filed
    under subsection 332(2) were dealt with.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report and remedial measures

- **[[169]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-169
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where there occurs or there is a
  likelihood of a release into the air of a substance in contravention
  of a regulation made under section 167, any person described in
  subsection (2) shall, as soon as possible in the circumstances,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4) and the regulations,
    notify an enforcement officer or any other person designated
    pursuant to the regulations and provide a written report on the
    matter to the enforcement officer or other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable measures consistent with the
    protection of the environment and public safety to prevent the
    release or, if it cannot be prevented, to remedy any dangerous
    condition or reduce or mitigate any danger to the environment or to
    human life or health that results from the release of the substance
    or may reasonably be expected to result if the substance is
    released; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make a reasonable effort to notify any member of
    the public who may be adversely affected by the release or likely
    release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to any person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns or has charge of a substance immediately
    before its release or its likely release into the air; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} causes or contributes to the release or increases
    the likelihood of the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by property owner

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where there occurs a release of a substance as
  described in subsection (1), any person, other than a person described
  in subsection (2), whose property is affected by the release shall, as
  soon as possible in the circumstances and subject to subsection (4),
  report the matter to an enforcement officer or to any person that is
  designated by regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to official

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where there are in force, by or under the laws of a
  province or an aboriginal government, provisions that the Governor in
  Council, by regulation, declares to be adequate for dealing with a
  release described in subsection (1), a report required by paragraph
  (1)(a) or subsection (3) shall be made to a person designated by those
  provisions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by enforcement officer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a person fails to take any measures required
  under subsection (1), an enforcement officer may take those measures,
  cause them to be taken or direct any person referred to in
  subsection (2) to take them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on power of direction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any direction of an enforcement officer under
  subsection (5) that is inconsistent with a requirement imposed by or
  under any other Act of Parliament is void to the extent of the
  inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to property

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Any enforcement officer or other person authorized or
  required to take any measures under subsection (1) or (5) may enter
  and have access to any place or property and may do any reasonable
  things that may be necessary in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Any person, other than a person described in
  subsection (2), who provides assistance or advice in taking the
  measures required by subsection (1) or who takes any measures
  authorized under subsection (5) is not personally liable either
  civilly or criminally in respect of any act or omission in the course
  of providing assistance or advice or taking any measures under those
  subsections unless it is established that the person acted in bad
  faith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of reasonable costs and expenses
by Her Majesty

- **[[170]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-170 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Her
  Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs and expenses of and
  incidental to taking any measures under subsection 169(5) from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 169(2)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 169(2)(b) to
    the extent of the person's negligence in causing or contributing to
    the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Only if reasonably incurred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses referred to in subsection (1)
  shall only be recovered to the extent that they can be established to
  have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the persons referred to in
  subsection (1) are jointly and severally liable or solidarily liable
  for the costs and expenses referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in paragraph 169(2)(b) shall not
  be held liable under subsection (3) to an extent greater than the
  extent of the person's negligence in causing or contributing to the
  release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A claim under this section may be sued for and
  recovered by Her Majesty in right of Canada with costs in proceedings
  brought or taken therefor in the name of Her Majesty in right of
  Canada in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse or indemnity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit or restrict any right of
  recourse or indemnity that a person may have against any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where events giving rise to a claim under this
  section occur, no proceedings in respect of the claim may be
  instituted after five years from the date on which the events occur or
  become evident to the Minister, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to have been issued by the
  Minister certifying the day on which the events giving rise to a claim
  under this section came to the knowledge of the Minister shall be
  received in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary, the document shall be considered as proof of that fact
  without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the document and without further
  proof.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[171]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-171 .sectionLabel}** No person shall carry
on a work, undertaking or activity that results in the release of a
substance in contravention of a regulation made under section 167.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans and specifications

- **[[172]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-172 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person carries on or proposes to carry on a work, undertaking or
  activity that results or may result in the release of a substance that
  creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to create, air pollution,
  the person shall, at the request of the Minister for the purposes of
  this Division and within the time that the Minister may specify,
  provide the Minister with plans, specifications, studies, procedures,
  schedules, analyses, samples or other information relating to the
  work, undertaking or activity and with analyses, samples, evaluations,
  studies, mitigation methodologies or other information relating to the
  substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obtaining information from government

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making a request to a person under subsection
  (1), the Minister shall try to obtain, within a reasonable time, the
  samples or information from the government responsible for the area in
  which the person is situated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

- **[[173]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-173 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may make an interim order that contains any provision that
  may be contained in a regulation under section 167 if the Minister
  believes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the substance or source referred to in
    subsection 166(1) is not adequately regulated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that immediate action is required to deal with a
    significant danger to the environment or to human life or health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interim order has effect from the time it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Governor in Council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An interim order ceases to have effect 14 days after
  it is made unless it is approved by the Governor in Council within
  that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not approve an interim
  order unless the Minister has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 24 hours after making the order, offered to
    consult with all affected governments to determine whether they are
    prepared to take sufficient action to deal with the significant
    danger; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} consulted with other ministers of the Crown in
    right of Canada to determine whether any action can be taken under
    any other Act of Parliament to deal with the significant danger.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial action

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Governor in Council approves an interim
  order, the Minister shall, within 90 days after the approval, take
  measures to comply with section 166 in order to address the
  significant danger that gave rise to the interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of unpublished order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence consisting
  of a contravention of an interim order that, at the time of the
  alleged contravention, had not been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) unless it is proved that, at the
  time of the alleged contravention, the person had been notified of the
  interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of effect

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an interim order ceases to
  have effect on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day it is repealed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day a regulation is made in accordance with
    subsection (5), and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two years after the order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Repeal of interim order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} No action is required to be taken under
  subsection (5) if the interim order is repealed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

**[[174]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-174 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
include in the annual report required by section 342 a report on the
administration of this Division.

### [DIVISION 7]{.HLabel2}[International Water Pollution]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65336 .Subheading}

Definition of [[water pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[175]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-175 .sectionLabel}** In this Division,
[[water pollution]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means a condition of water,
arising wholly or partly from the presence in water of any substance,
that directly or indirectly

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} endangers the health, safety or welfare of humans;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} interferes with the normal enjoyment of life or
  property;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} endangers the health of animal life;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} causes damage to plant life or to property; or

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} degrades or alters, or forms part of a process of
  degrading or alterating, an ecosystem to an extent that is detrimental
  to its use by humans, animals or plants.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Determination of international water
pollution

- **[[176]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-176
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the
  Minister shall act under subsections (2) and (3) only if the Ministers
  have reason to believe that a substance released from a source in
  Canada into water creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to create,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} water pollution in a country other than Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} water pollution that violates, or is likely to
    violate, an international agreement binding on Canada in relation to
    the prevention, control or correction of pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation with other governments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the source referred to in subsection (1) is a not
  a federal source, the Minister shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} consult with the government responsible for the
    area in which the source is situated to determine whether that
    government can prevent, control or correct the water pollution under
    its laws; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the government referred to in paragraph (a) can
    prevent, control or correct the water pollution, offer it an
    opportunity to do so.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial action

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} If the source referred to in subsection (1) is a
  federal source, or if the government referred to in paragraph (2)(a)
  cannot prevent, control or correct the water pollution under its laws
  or does not do so, the Minister shall take at least one of the
  following courses of action:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} on approval by the Governor in Council, publish a
    notice under subsection 56(1); or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} recommend regulations to the Governor in Council
    for the purpose of preventing, controlling or correcting the water
    pollution.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reciprocity with other country

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} If the water pollution referred to in paragraph
  (1)(a) is in a country where Canada does not have substantially the
  same rights with respect to the prevention, control or correction of
  water pollution as that country has under this Division, the Minister
  shall decide whether to act under subsections (2) and (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Other factors

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} When recommending regulations under paragraph (3)(b),
  the Minister shall take into account comments made under subsection
  178(2), notices of objection filed under subsection 332(2) and any
  report of a board of review submitted under subsection 340(1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[177]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-177 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations with
respect to a substance released from a source in Canada into water that
creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to create, water pollution
referred to in subsection 176(1) for the purpose of preventing,
controlling or correcting the water pollution, including regulations
respecting

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity or concentration of the substance that
  may be released into water;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and conditions under which the
  substance may be released into water, either alone or in combination
  with any other substance;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of books and records for the
  administration of any regulation made under this section;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of sampling, analyses, tests,
  measurements or monitoring of the substance and the submission of the
  results to the Minister; and

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, test procedures and laboratory
  practices to be followed for conducting sampling, analyses, tests,
  measurements or monitoring of the substance.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to other country

- **[[178]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-178 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall advise the government of any country that would be
  affected by or benefit from the regulation before it is published
  under subsection 332(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Comments

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Within 60 days after the publication of a proposed
  regulation under subsection 332(1), any person, including a
  representative of the government of any country that would be affected
  by or benefit from it, may file with the Minister written comments on
  the proposed regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Notice to other governments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At the end of the period of 60 days referred to in
  subsection (2), the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall advise each government referred to in
    paragraph 176(2)(a) of any notice of objection filed under
    subsection 332(2); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} shall publish in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), and may publish in any other
    manner that the Minister considers appropriate, a report or a notice
    of the availability of a report that summarizes how any written
    comments filed under subsection (2) or a notice of objection filed
    under subsection 332(2) were dealt with.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report and remedial measures

- **[[179]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-179
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Where there occurs or there is a
  likelihood of a release into waters of a substance in contravention of
  a regulation made under section 177, any person described in
  subsection (2) shall, as soon as possible in the circumstances,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (4) and the regulations,
    notify an enforcement officer or any other person designated
    pursuant to the regulations and provide a written report on the
    matter to the enforcement officer or other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable measures consistent with the

    condition or reduce or mitigate any danger to the environment or to
    human life or health that results from the release of the substance
    or may reasonably be expected to result if the substance is
    released; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make a reasonable effort to notify any member of
    the public who may be adversely affected by the release or likely

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to any person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns or has charge of a substance immediately
    before its release or its likely release into water; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} causes or contributes to the release or increases
    the likelihood of the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by property owner

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where there occurs a release of a substance as
  described in subsection (1), any person, other than a person described
  in subsection (2), whose property is affected by the release shall, as
  soon as possible in the circumstances and subject to subsection (4),
  report the matter to an enforcement officer or to any person that is
