- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to official

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where there are in force, by or under the laws of a
  province or an aboriginal government, provisions that the Governor in
  Council, by regulation, declares to be adequate for dealing with a
  release described in subsection (1), a report required by paragraph
  (1)(a) or subsection (3) shall be made to a person designated by those
  provisions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by enforcement officer

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where any person fails to take any measures required
  under subsection (1), an enforcement officer may take those measures,
  cause them to be taken or direct any person referred to in
  subsection (2) to take them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on power of direction

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any direction of an enforcement officer under
  subsection (5) that is inconsistent with a requirement imposed by or
  under any other Act of Parliament is void to the extent of the
  inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to property

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Any enforcement officer or other person authorized or
  required to take any measures under subsection (1) or (5) may enter
  and have access to any place or property and may do any reasonable
  things that may be necessary in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Any person, other than a person described in
  subsection (2), who provides assistance or advice in taking the
  measures required by subsection (1) or who takes any measures
  authorized under subsection (5) is not personally liable either
  civilly or criminally in respect of any act or omission in the course
  of providing assistance or advice or taking any measures under those
  subsections unless it is established that the person acted in bad
  faith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of reasonable costs and expenses
by Her Majesty

- **[[180]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-180 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Her
  Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs and expenses of and
  incidental to taking any measures under subsection 179(5) from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 179(2)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 179(2)(b) to
    the extent of the person's negligence in causing or contributing to
    the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Only if reasonably incurred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses referred to in subsection (1)
  shall only be recovered to the extent that they can be established to
  have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the persons referred to in
  subsection (1) are jointly and severally liable or solidarily liable
  for the costs and expenses referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in paragraph 179(2)(b) shall not
  be held liable under subsection (3) to an extent greater than the
  extent of the person's negligence in causing or contributing to the
  release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A claim under this section may be sued for and
  recovered by Her Majesty in right of Canada with costs in proceedings
  brought or taken therefor in the name of Her Majesty in right of
  Canada in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse or indemnity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit or restrict any right of
  recourse or indemnity that a person may have against any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where events giving rise to a claim under this
  section occur, no proceedings in respect of the claim may be
  instituted after five years from the date on which the events occur or
  become evident to the Minister, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to have been issued by the
  Minister certifying the day on which the events giving rise to a claim

  received in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary, the document shall be considered as proof of that fact
  without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the document and without further

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition

**[[181]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-181 .sectionLabel}** No person shall carry
on a work, undertaking or activity that results in the release of a
substance in contravention of a regulation made under section 177.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plans and specifications

- **[[182]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-182 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person carries on or proposes to carry on a work, undertaking or
  activity that results or may result in the release of a substance that
  creates, or may reasonably be anticipated to create, water pollution,
  the person shall, at the request of the Minister for the purposes of
  this Division and within the time that the Minister may specify,
  provide the Minister with plans, specifications, studies, procedures,
  schedules, analyses, samples or other information relating to the
  work, undertaking or activity and with analyses, samples, evaluations,

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Obtaining information from government

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Before making a request to a person under subsection
  (1), the Minister shall try to obtain, within a reasonable time, the
  samples or information from the government responsible for the area in
  which the person is situated.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim order

- **[[183]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-183 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may make an interim order that contains any provision that
  may be contained in a regulation under section 177 if the Minister
  believes

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that the substance or source referred to in
    subsection 176(1) is not adequately regulated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that immediate action is required to deal with a
    significant danger to the environment or to human life or health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effective date of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An interim order has effect from the time it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Governor in Council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An interim order ceases to have effect 14 days after
  it is made unless it is approved by the Governor in Council within
  that period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not approve an interim
  order unless the Minister has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 24 hours after making the order, offered to
    consult with all affected governments to determine whether they are
    prepared to take sufficient action to deal with the significant
    danger; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} consulted with other ministers of the Crown in
    right of Canada to determine whether any action can be taken under

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Ministerial action

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where the Governor in Council approves an interim
  order, the Minister shall, within 90 days after the approval, take

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of unpublished order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence consisting

  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) unless it is proved that, at the

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day it is repealed,

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report to Parliament

**[[184]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-184 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall

### [DIVISION 8]{.HLabel2}[Control of Movement of Hazardous Waste and Hazardous Recyclable Material and of Prescribed Non-hazardous Waste for Final Disposal]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65451 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Import, export and transit

- **[[185]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-185 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall import, export or convey in transit a hazardous waste or
  hazardous recyclable material, or prescribed non-hazardous waste for
  final disposal, except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} after notifying the Minister and paying the
    prescribed fee;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} after receiving from the Minister whichever one of
    the following permits is applicable:

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} an import permit or export permit that, except in
      the case of a permit issued under subsection (4), states that the
      authorities of the country of destination and, if applicable, of
      the country of transit have authorized the movement, and that the
      authorities of the jurisdiction of destination have authorized the
      final disposal or recycling of the waste or material, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a transit permit that states that the Minister
      has authorized the movement; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} in accordance with the prescribed conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Refusal to issue permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} If the Minister is of the opinion that the waste or
  material will not be managed in a manner that will protect the
  environment and human health against the adverse effects that may
  result from that waste or material, the Minister may refuse, in
  accordance with the criteria set out in the regulations, to issue a
  permit even if the relevant authorities have given their
  authorization.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation with governments

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before refusing under subsection (2) to issue a
  permit to import, the Minister shall consult with the government of
  the jurisdiction of destination.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Special circumstances to issue permits

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister is of the opinion that the waste
  or material will be managed in a manner that will protect the
  environment and human health against the adverse effects that may
  result from that waste or material, the Minister may issue a permit if
  the relevant authorities inform the Minister that they lack the legal
  authority to authorize the movement, final disposal or recycling but
  are not opposed to it.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibitions --- import, export and
transit

- **[[186]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-186 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of implementing international agreements respecting the
  environment, the Minister may, with the approval of the Governor in
  Council and taking into account Canada's international obligations,
  prohibit, completely or partially and under any conditions that may be
  prescribed, the import, export or transit of waste or material
  referred to in subsection 185(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Prohibition of abandonment

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} No person shall abandon any waste or material
  referred to in subsection 185(1) in the course of import, export or
  transit.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

**[[187]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-187 .sectionLabel}** After the Minister
receives a notification of the proposed import, export or transit of a
waste or material referred to in paragraph 185(1)(a), the Minister shall
publish in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or in any
other manner that the Minister considers appropriate, the name or
specifications of the waste or material and

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a proposed import, the name of the
  jurisdiction of origin and the name of the importer;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a proposed export, the name of the
  jurisdiction of destination and the name of the exporter; and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a proposed transit, the names of the
  jurisdictions of origin and of destination and the name of the
  conveyor.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduction of export for final disposal

- **[[188]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-188 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of reducing or phasing out the export of hazardous waste
  or prescribed non-hazardous waste for final disposal, the Minister may
  require an exporter, or a class of exporters, of hazardous waste to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} submit to the Minister, at the same time as the
    notification referred to in paragraph 185(1)(a) and at any other
    prescribed time, a plan in accordance with the regulations; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} implement that plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Declaration of implementation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every person who is required to implement a plan
  under paragraph (1)(b) shall file with the Minister, within 30 days
  after the completion of each stage of the plan, a written declaration
  that the implementation has been completed.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Sanction

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may refuse to issue a permit to an
  exporter who does not comply with subsection (1) or (2).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Movement within Canada

- **[[189]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-189 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} No
  person shall undertake movement within Canada of hazardous waste or
  hazardous recyclable material otherwise than in accordance with this
  Division and the regulations and unless the person pays the prescribed
  fee.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate, information derived from documents
  received under regulations made for the purpose of this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Permits based on equivalent environmental
safety level

- **[[190]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-190 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may issue a permit authorizing, subject to conditions fixed
  by the Minister, any activity to be conducted in a manner that does
  not comply with this Division if the Minister is satisfied that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which the activity will be conducted
    provides a level of environmental safety at least equivalent to that
    provided by compliance with this Division; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} in the case of the importation, exportation or
    transit of a waste or material referred to in subsection 185(1), the
    activity is consistent with international environmental agreements
    binding on Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Scope of permit

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The permit may authorize the activity in terms of the
  persons who may conduct the activity and in terms of the waste and
  material that it may involve.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Revocation of permit

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may revoke the permit if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the Minister is of the opinion that paragraph
    (1)(a) or (b) no longer applies;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the regulations have been amended and address the
    activity authorized by the permit; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the permit holder does not comply with the
    conditions of the permit.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Minister shall publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate, a copy of each permit issued under
  this section.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

**[[191]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-191 .sectionLabel}** The Governor in Council
may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make regulations generally
for carrying out the purposes and provisions of this Division, including
regulations

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} defining, for the purposes of this Division and Part
  10, words and expressions used in this Division, and providing
  criteria, testing protocols and standards in relation to those
  definitions;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} respecting the notification referred to in paragraph
  185(1)(a) and the procedure for applying for a permit under this
  Division;

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} establishing criteria for the purpose of subsection
  185(2) that take into account obligations arising from international
  agreements to which Canada is a party;

- [(d)]{.lawlabel} for establishing a classification system for waste
  and material;

- [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting information and documents to be provided
  to the Minister;

- [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting conditions governing the import, export,
  transit and movement within Canada of waste and material;

- [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting plans referred to in subsection 188(1),
  taking into account

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the benefit of using the nearest appropriate
    disposal facility, and

  - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} changes in the quantity of goods the production of
    which generates hazardous waste to be disposed of by an exporter or
    class of exporters; and

- [(h)]{.lawlabel} prescribing anything that by this Division is to be
  prescribed.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Forms

**[[192]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-192 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may
establish forms for the purposes of this Division.

## [PART 8]{.HLabel1}[Environmental Matters Related to Emergencies]{.HTitleText1} {#h-65518 .Part}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[193]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-193 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[environmental emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[environmental emergency]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means

    - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an uncontrolled, unplanned or accidental release,
      or release in contravention of regulations or interim orders made
      under this Part, of a substance into the environment; or

    - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the reasonable likelihood of such a release into
      the environment. ([urgence environnementale]{.DefinedTermLink
      lang="fr"})

[[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means, except in sections 199 and
    200.1, a substance on a list of substances established under
    regulations or interim orders made under this
    Part. ([substance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 193
- 2004, c. 15, s. 26
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-193-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 193"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

**[[194]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-194 .sectionLabel}** For the purposes of
this Part, any power, duty or function conferred or imposed under this
Part may only be exercised or performed in relation to those aspects of
an environmental emergency that

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} have or may have an immediate or long-term harmful
  effect on the environment;

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger to the
  environment on which human life depends; or

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger in Canada to
  human life or health.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Research

**[[195]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-195 .sectionLabel}** Despite subsection
36(3) of the [Fisheries Act](/eng/acts/F-14), subsection 123(1) and
regulations made under paragraphs 93(1)(a), (b), (c) and (d) and
209(2)(a), (b), (c) and (d), the Minister may

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} examine and conduct research, including tests,
  respecting the causes, circumstances and effects of and remedial
  measures for an environmental emergency; and

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} conduct and publicize demonstration projects.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Guidelines and codes of practice

**[[196]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-196 .sectionLabel}** The Minister may issue
guidelines and codes of practice respecting the prevention of,
preparedness for and response to an environmental emergency and for
restoring any part of the environment damaged by or during an emergency.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

- **[[197]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-197 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} In
  carrying out the responsibilities conferred by section 196, the
  Minister shall offer to consult with the government of a province and
  the members of the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal
  governments and may consult with a government department or agency,
  aboriginal people, representatives of industry and labour and
  municipal authorities or with persons interested in the quality of the
  environment or environmental emergencies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with subsection
  (1), the Minister may act under section 196 if the offer to consult is
  not accepted by the government of a province or members of the
  Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Publication of guidelines and codes of
practice

**[[198]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-198 .sectionLabel}** The Minister shall
publish in the [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), or in any
other manner that the Minister considers appropriate, guidelines and
codes of practice issued under section 196 or a notice stating where
copies of those documents may be obtained.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirements for environmental emergency
plans

- **[[199]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-199 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may at any time publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate, a notice requiring any person or class
  of persons described in the notice to prepare and implement an
  environmental emergency plan respecting the prevention of,
  preparedness for, response to or recovery from an environmental
  emergency in respect of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} a substance or group of substances specified in
    Part 1 or 2 of the list of toxic substances in Schedule 1; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} a substance or group of substances in relation to
    which there has been published in the [Canada
    Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/)

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a statement of the Ministers under paragraph
      77(6)(b) indicating that the measure that they propose to take, as
      confirmed or amended, is a recommendation that the substance be
      added to Part 1 or 2 of the list of toxic substances in Schedule
      1, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} a copy of an order proposed to be made under
      subsection 90(1).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contents of notice

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The notice shall specify

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance or group of substances in relation to
    which the plan is to be prepared;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the period within which the plan is to be prepared;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the period within which the plan is to be
    implemented; and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} any other matter that the Minister considers
    necessary.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Extension of time

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where the Minister is of the opinion that further
  time is necessary to prepare or implement the plan, the Minister may
  extend the period for a person who submits a written request before
  the expiry of the period referred to in the notice or of any extended
  period.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Plan prepared or implemented for
  another purpose

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (5), where a person who is
  required to prepare or implement an environmental emergency plan under
  a notice published under this section has prepared or implemented a
  plan in respect of environmental emergencies on a voluntary basis or
  for another government or under another Act of Parliament that meets
  all or some of the requirements of the notice, the person may use that
  plan for the purposes of meeting the requirements of this Part and, in
  that case, the plan shall be considered to be an environmental
  emergency plan that has been prepared or implemented under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Where partial requirements met

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Where a person uses a plan under subsection (4) that
  does not meet all of the requirements of the notice, the person shall

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} amend the plan so that it meets all of those
    requirements; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prepare an additional environmental emergency plan
    that meets the remainder of those requirements.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of provisions re
  declarations and keeping plans

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Sections 58 and 59 apply to environmental emergency
  plans, with any modifications that the circumstances require, as if
  any reference to a pollution prevention plan were read as a reference
  to an environmental emergency plan.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Submission of plans

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may publish in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/), and in any other manner that the
  Minister considers appropriate, a notice requiring any person or class
  of persons described in the notice who are required to prepare or
  implement an environmental emergency plan under subsection (1) or
  section 291 or under an agreement in respect of environmental
  protection alternative measures to submit, within the period specified
  by the Minister, the plan or any part of the plan.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 199
- [2023, c. 12, s. 45](#1403040-1404605){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403040-1404605.html"
  role="button"}
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-199-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 199"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations

- **[[200]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister and
  after the Committee is given an opportunity to provide its advice to
  the Minister under section 6, make regulations

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} establishing a list of substances that, if they
    enter the environment as a result of an environmental emergency,

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} have or may have an immediate or long-term
      harmful effect on the environment or its biological diversity,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger to the
      environment on which human life depends, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} constitute or may constitute a danger in Canada
      to human life or health;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} prescribing, in respect of a substance on the list
    established under paragraph (a), a minimum quantity;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} respecting the identification of the places in
    Canada where a substance referred to in paragraph (a), in any
    quantity or in the quantity prescribed for that substance under
    paragraph (b), is located and requiring notification to the Minister
    of those places;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} respecting the prevention of, preparedness for,
    response to and recovery from an environmental emergency in respect
    of a substance;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} respecting the notification and reporting of an
    environmental emergency;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} respecting the notification and reporting of the
    measures taken

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to prevent the environmental emergency, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to repair, reduce or mitigate any negative
      effects on the environment or human life or health that result
      from the environmental emergency or that may reasonably be
      expected to result from it;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} respecting the implementation of international
    agreements entered into by Canada in relation to environmental
    emergencies; and

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} respecting any other matter necessary for the
    purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Environmental emergencies regulated
  under other Acts of Parliament

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not make a regulation
  under subsection (1) in respect of a matter if, by order, the Governor
  in Council states that it is of the opinion that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the matter is regulated by or under any other Act
    of Parliament that contains provisions that are similar in effect to
    sections 194 to 205; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that Act or any regulation made under that Act
    provides sufficient protection to human health and the environment
    or its biological diversity.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Interim orders

- **[[200.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-200.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The Minister may make an interim
  order, in respect of a substance, that contains any provision that may
  be contained in a regulation made under this Part, if

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the substance

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} is not on the list established under regulations
      made under this Part and the Ministers believe that, if it enters
      the environment as a result of an environmental emergency,

      - [(A)]{.lawlabel} it would have or may have an immediate or
        long-term harmful effect on the environment or its biological
        diversity,

      - [(B)]{.lawlabel} it would constitute or may constitute a danger
        to the environment on which human life depends, or

      - [(C)]{.lawlabel} it would constitute or may constitute a danger
        in Canada to human life or health, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} is on that list and the Ministers believe that
      it is not adequately regulated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the Ministers believe that immediate action is
    required to deal with a significant danger to the environment or to
    human life or health.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Effect of order

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an interim order has
  effect

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} from the time it is made; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} as if it were a regulation made under this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Approval of Governor in Council

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} An interim order ceases to have effect unless it is
  approved by the Governor in Council within 14 days after it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} The Governor in Council shall not approve an interim
  order unless the Minister has

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} within 24 hours after making the order, offered to
    consult with all affected governments to determine whether they are
    prepared to take sufficient action to deal with the significant
    danger; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} consulted with other ministers of the Crown in
    right of Canada to determine whether any action can be taken under
    any other Act of Parliament to deal with the significant danger.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recommendation of regulations

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the Governor in Council approves an interim order,
  the Minister shall, within 90 days after the approval, publish in the
  [Canada Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) a statement indicating
  whether the Minister intends to recommend to the Governor in Council

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that a regulation having the same effect as the
    order be made under this Part; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} if the order was made in respect of a substance
    that was not on the list established under regulations made under
    this Part, that the substance be added to that list.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Contravention of unpublished order

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} No person shall be convicted of an offence consisting
  of a contravention of an interim order that, at the time of the
  alleged contravention, had not been published in the [Canada
  Gazette](http://www.gazette.gc.ca/) unless it is proved that, at the
  time of the alleged contravention, the person had been notified of the
  interim order or reasonable steps had been taken to bring the purport
  of the interim order to the notice of those persons likely to be
  affected by it.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Cessation of effect

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (3), an interim order ceases to
  have effect on the earliest of

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the day it is repealed,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the day a regulation referred to in subsection (5)
    is made, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} two years after the order is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Tabling of order

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A copy of each interim order must be tabled in each
  House of Parliament within 15 days after it is made.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}House not sitting

  [(9)]{.lawlabel} In order to comply with subsection (8), the interim
  order may be sent to the Clerk of the House if the House is not
  sitting.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2004, c. 15, s. 27
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Remedial measures

- **[[201]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-201
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to any regulations made
  under subsection 200(1) or any interim orders made under section
  200.1, if there occurs an environmental emergency in respect of a
  substance on a list established under the regulations or interim
  orders, any person described in subsection (2) shall, as soon as
  possible in the circumstances,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} notify an enforcement officer or any other person
    designated by regulation or interim order and provide a written
    report on the environmental emergency to the enforcement officer or
    other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable emergency measures consistent
    with the protection of the environment and public safety

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} to prevent the environmental emergency, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} to repair, reduce or mitigate any negative
      effects on the environment or human life or health that result
      from the environmental emergency or that may reasonably be
      expected to result from it; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make a reasonable effort to notify any member of
    the public who may be adversely affected by the environmental
    emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to any person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns or has the charge, management or control of a
    substance immediately before the environmental emergency; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} causes or contributes to the environmental
    emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person, other than a person described in subsection
  (2), shall, as soon as possible in the circumstances, report an
  environmental emergency to an enforcement officer or to a person
  designated by regulation or interim order if their property is
  affected by the environmental emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by enforcement officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where any person fails to take any measures required
  under subsection (1), an enforcement officer may take those measures,
  cause them to be taken or direct any person referred to in
  subsection (2) to take them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on power of direction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Any direction of an enforcement officer under
  subsection (4) that is inconsistent with a requirement imposed by or
  under any other Act of Parliament is void to the extent of the
  inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to property

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Any enforcement officer or other person authorized or
  required to take any measures under subsection (1) or (4) may enter
  and have access to any place or property and may do anything
  reasonable that may be necessary in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal liability

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Any person, other than a person described in
  subsection (2), who provides assistance or advice in taking the
  measures required by subsection (1) or who takes any measures
  authorized under subsection (4) is not personally liable either
  civilly or criminally in respect of any act or omission in the course
  of providing assistance or advice or taking any measures under those
  subsections unless it is established that the person acted in bad
  faith.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 201
- 2004, c. 15, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-201-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 201"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary report

- **[[202]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-202 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person knows about an environmental emergency but the person is not
  required to report the matter under this Act, the person may report
  any information about the environmental emergency to an enforcement
  officer or to a person designated by regulation or interim order.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person making the report may request that their
  identity and any information that could reasonably reveal their
  identity not be released.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement of confidentiality

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall disclose, or have disclosed, the
  identity of the person making the request or any information that
  could reasonably be expected to reveal their identity unless the
  person making the request authorizes the disclosure in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee protection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament, no employer
  shall dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or otherwise
  disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of employment,
  by reason that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee has made a report under subsection
    (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has refused or stated an intention of refusing
    to do anything that is an offence under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has done or stated an intention of doing
    anything that is required to be done by or under this Act.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 202
- 2004, c. 15, s. 29
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-202-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 202"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of costs and expenses

- **[[203]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-203 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Her
  Majesty in right of Canada may recover the costs and expenses of and
  incidental to taking any measures under subsection 201(4) from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 201(2)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 201(2)(b) to
    the extent of their negligence or wilful conduct in causing or
    contributing to the environmental emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonably incurred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses referred to in subsection (1)
  shall only be recovered to the extent that they can be established to
  have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (4), the persons referred to in
  subsection (1) are jointly and severally liable or solidarily liable
  for the costs and expenses referred to in that subsection.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} A person referred to in paragraph 201(2)(b) shall not
  be held liable under subsection (3) to an extent greater than the
  extent of their negligence or wilful conduct in causing or
  contributing to the environmental emergency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Procedure

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} A claim under this section may be sued for and
  recovered by Her Majesty in right of Canada with costs in proceedings
  brought or taken therefor in the name of Her Majesty in right of
  Canada in any court of competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse or indemnity

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit or restrict any right of
  recourse or indemnity that a person may have against any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} Where events giving rise to a claim under this
  section occur, no proceedings in respect of the claim may be
  instituted after five years from the date on which the events occur or
  become evident to the Minister, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's certificate

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to have been issued by the
  Minister certifying the day on which the events giving rise to a claim
  under this section came to the knowledge of the Minister shall be
  received in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary, the document shall be considered as proof of that fact
  without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the document and without further
  proof.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}National notification and reporting
system

- **[[204]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-204 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may establish, in cooperation with the governments of the
  provinces and aboriginal governments and government departments or
  agencies, a national system for the notification and reporting of
  environmental emergencies.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Copies

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subject to section 314, any person may have access to
  and a copy of any information contained in the national system
  established under subsection (1).

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability of owner of substance

- **[[205]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-205
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to this Part, the person who
  owns or has the charge, management or control of a substance
  immediately before an environmental emergency is liable

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} for restoring any part of the environment damaged
    by or during the emergency;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} for costs and expenses incurred by a public
    department within the meaning of the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46)
    or other public authority in Canada in respect of measures taken to
    prevent, repair, remedy or minimize the damage to the environment
    resulting from the emergency, including measures taken in
    anticipation of the environmental emergency, to the extent that the
    measures taken and the costs and expenses are reasonable, and for
    any loss or damage caused by such measures; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} for costs and expenses incurred by the Minister in
    respect of measures taken to prevent, repair, remedy or minimize the
    environmental emergency to the extent that the measures taken and
    the costs and expenses are reasonable, and for any loss or damage
    caused by such measures.

- Definition of [[public authority in Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In subsection (1), [[public authority in
  Canada]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means Her Majesty in right of a province,
  an aboriginal government or any other body designated by the Governor
  in Council for the purposes of that subsection as a public authority
  in Canada.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Strict liability subject to certain
  defences

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} The person's liability under subsection (1) does not
  depend on proof of fault or negligence, but the owner is not liable
  under that subsection if the owner establishes that the environmental
  emergency

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} resulted from an act of war, hostilities or
    insurrection or from a natural phenomenon of an exceptional,
    inevitable and irresistible character;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} was wholly caused by an act or omission of a third
    party with intent to cause damage; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} was wholly caused by the negligence or other
    wrongful act of government, public department or public authority.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reduced liability

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where the person who is liable under subsection (1)
  establishes that the environmental emergency resulted wholly or
  partially from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} an act or omission done by the person who suffered
    the damage with intent to cause damage, or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the negligence or wilful conduct of that person,

  the liability of the person referred to in subsection (1) to that
  other person is reduced or nullified in proportion to the degree to
  which the environmental emergency resulted from the factors mentioned
  in paragraphs (a) and (b).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person's rights against third parties
  preserved

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Nothing in this Part shall be construed as limiting
  or restricting any right of recourse that the person who is liable
  under subsection (1) may have against any other person.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Person's own claim for costs and
  expenses

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} Costs and expenses incurred by the person referred to
  in subsection (1) in respect of measures voluntarily taken to prevent,
  repair, remedy or minimize damage from the environmental emergency,
  including measures taken in anticipation of an environmental
  emergency, to the extent that the measures taken and the costs and
  expenses are reasonable, rank equally with other claims against any
  security given by that person in respect of that person's liability
  under this section.

## [PART 9]{.HLabel1}[Government Operations and Federal and Aboriginal Land]{.HTitleText1} {#h-65711 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65712 .Subheading}

Definition of [[regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

**[[206]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-206 .sectionLabel}** In this Part,
[[regulations]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means regulations made under this
Part.

### [Application]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65715 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application to Government, etc.

- **[[207]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-207 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} This
  Part applies to

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} departments, boards and agencies of the Government
    of Canada;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} federal works and undertakings;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} aboriginal land, federal land, persons on that land
    and other persons in so far as their activities involve that land;
    and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} Crown corporations, as defined in subsection 83(1)
    of the [Financial Administration Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Yukon

  [(1.1)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not apply to public real property
  under the administration and control of the Commissioner of Yukon
  pursuant to the [Yukon Act](/eng/acts/Y-2.01).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} This Part does not restrict any of the following
  powers in so far as they may be exercised in relation to air and all
  layers of the atmosphere above federal land or aboriginal land:

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} powers under the [Aeronautics Act](/eng/acts/A-2)
    or a provision of any other Act of Parliament relating to
    aeronautics or air transportation; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} powers under the [National Defence
    Act](/eng/acts/N-5) or a provision of any other Act of Parliament
    relating to national defence and security.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 207
- 2002, c. 7, s. 125
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-207-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 207"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Objectives, Guidelines and Codes of Practice]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65731 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister shall establish

- **[[208]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-208 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister shall establish objectives, guidelines and codes of practice
  for the purpose of carrying out the Minister's duties and functions
  under this Part related to the quality of the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} In establishing an objective, a guideline or a code
  of practice under subsection (1), the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall offer to consult with the government of a
    territory if the objective, guideline or code of practice applies to
    that territory, and with the members of the Committee who are
    representatives of aboriginal governments if it applies to
    aboriginal land over which an aboriginal government has
    jurisdiction; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may consult with a department, board or agency of
    the Government of Canada, or a Crown corporation as defined in
    subsection 83(1) of the [Financial Administration
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with paragraph
  (2)(a), the Minister may act under subsection (1) if the offer to
  consult is not accepted by the government of a territory or members of
  the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal governments.

### [Regulations]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65741 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Regulations for the protection of the
environment

- **[[209]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-209 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Governor in Council may, on the recommendation of the Minister, make
  regulations for the protection of the environment, including
  regulations respecting

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the establishment of environmental management
    systems;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} pollution prevention and pollution prevention
    plans;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} environmental emergencies, releases of substances
    and likely releases, including their prevention, preparedness for
    them, reporting them, both as soon as possible in the circumstances
    and in detail at a later stage, and the measures to be taken to
    respond to them and to correct damage to the environment;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the designation of persons for the purposes of
    paragraph 212(1)(a) and subsections 212(3) and 213(1) and
    prescribing the form of the report to be made under those provisions
    and the information to be contained in it;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances in which a report is not required
    under paragraph 212(1)(a);

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} any substance; and

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any other matter necessary to carry out the
    purposes of this Part.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Content of the regulations

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Regulations with respect to any substance may provide
  for, or impose requirements respecting,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the quantity or concentration of any substance that
    may be released into the environment either alone or in combination
    with any other substance from any source or type of source;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the places or areas where the substance may be
    released;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the commercial, manufacturing, processing or other
    activity in the course of which the substance may be released;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and the conditions under which
    the substance may be released into the environment, either alone or
    in combination with any other substance;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the quantity of the substance that may be
    manufactured, processed, used, offered for sale or sold in Canada;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} the purposes for which the substance, a product
    that contains the substance or a product that may release the
    substance into the environment may be imported, exported,
    manufactured, processed, used, offered for sale or sold;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and the conditions under which
    the substance, a product that contains the substance or a product
    that may release the substance into the environment may be imported,
    exported, manufactured, processed or used;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} the quantities or concentrations in which the
    substance may be used;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} the quantities or concentrations of the substance
    that may be imported or exported;

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} the countries from or to which the substance may be
    imported or exported;

  - [(k)]{.lawlabel} the conditions under which, the manner in which and
    the purposes for which the substance may be imported or exported;

  - [(l)]{.lawlabel} the total, partial or conditional prohibition of
    the manufacture, use, processing, sale, offering for sale, import or
    export of the substance, a product that contains the substance or a
    product that may release the substance into the environment and the
    total, partial or conditional prohibition of the manufacture, import
    or export of a product that is intended to contain the substance;

  - [(m)]{.lawlabel} the quantity or concentration of the substance that
    may be contained in any product manufactured, imported, exported,
    sold or offered for sale in Canada, or that any such product may
    release into the environment;

  - [(n)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which, the conditions under which and
    the purposes for which the substance, a product that contains the
    substance or a product that may release the substance into the
    environment may be advertised or offered for sale;

  - [(o)]{.lawlabel} the manner in which and the conditions under which
    the substance, a product that contains the substance or a product
    that may release the substance into the environment may be stored,
    displayed, handled, transported or offered for transport;

  - [(p)]{.lawlabel} the packaging and labelling of the substance, a
    product that contains the substance or a product that may release
    the substance into the environment;

  - [(q)]{.lawlabel} the manner, conditions, places and method of
    disposal or recycling of the substance, a product that contains the
    substance or a product that may release the substance into the
    environment, including standards for the construction, maintenance
    and inspection of disposal or recycling sites;

  - [(r)]{.lawlabel} the submission to the Minister, on request or at
    any times that are prescribed, of information relating to the
    substance, a product that contains the substance or a product that
    may release the substance into the environment;

  - [(s)]{.lawlabel} the maintenance of books and records for the
    administration of any regulation made under this section;

  - [(t)]{.lawlabel} the conduct of sampling, analyses, tests,
    measurements or monitoring of the substance, a product that contains
    the substance or a product that may release the substance into the
    environment and the submission of the results to the Minister;

  - [(u)]{.lawlabel} the submission to the Minister of samples of the
    substance, a product that contains the substance or a product that
    may release the substance into the environment;

  - [(v)]{.lawlabel} the conditions, test procedures and laboratory
    practices to be followed for conducting sampling, analyses, tests,
    measurements or monitoring of the substance, a product that contains
    the substance or a product that may release the substance into the
    environment;

  - [(w)]{.lawlabel} the circumstances or conditions under which the
    Minister may, for the proper administration of this Act, modify

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any requirement for sampling, analyses, tests,
      measurements or monitoring, or

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} conditions, test procedures and laboratory
      practices for conducting any required sampling, analyses, tests,
      measurements or monitoring; and

  - [(x)]{.lawlabel} the decommissioning and decontamination of storage,
    handling, transportation, disposal and recycling sites for the
    substance.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Consultation

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Before recommending to the Governor in Council a
  regulation under this section, the Minister

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} shall offer to consult with the government of a
    territory if the regulation applies to that territory, and with the
    members of the Committee who are representatives of aboriginal
    governments if it applies to aboriginal land over which an
    aboriginal government has jurisdiction; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} may consult with a department, board or agency of
    the Government of Canada, or a Crown corporation as defined in
    subsection 83(1) of the [Financial Administration
    Act](/eng/acts/F-11).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may act

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} At any time after the 60th day following the day on
  which the Minister offers to consult in accordance with paragraph
  (3)(a), the Minister may recommend a regulation to the Governor in
  Council under this section if the offer to consult is not accepted by
  the government of a territory or members of the Committee who are
  representatives of aboriginal governments.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of Minister

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} Regulations made under this section may authorize the
  Minister, in the circumstances and subject to the conditions and
  limits that may be specified in the regulations, to issue, amend,
  suspend and revoke permits and other authorizations in relation to any
  matter that is the subject of the regulations and to set their terms
  and conditions.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}[Statutory Instruments
  Act](/eng/acts/S-22)

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} A permit or other authorization issued under
  regulations made under this section is not a [[statutory
  instrument]{.dfn}]{.DefinitionRef} as defined in subsection 2(1) of
  the [Statutory Instruments Act](/eng/acts/S-22).

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 209
- 2017, c. 26, s. 63(E)
- [2023, c. 12, s. 46](#1403042-1404623){.wb-lbx
  aria-controls="centred-popup" ajax-after="1403042-1404623.html"
  role="button"}
:::

[Previous
Version](section-209-20171212.html "Link to previous version of section 209"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Non-application of regulations

**[[210]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-210 .sectionLabel}** Where the Governor in
Council is of the opinion that provisions of any other Part of this Act
or any other Act of Parliament, or regulations made under them,

- [(a)]{.lawlabel} are in force in respect of an aspect of the
  protection of the environment,

- [(b)]{.lawlabel} apply to a federal work or undertaking, federal land
  or aboriginal land, and

- [(c)]{.lawlabel} provide sufficient protection to the environment and
  human health,

the Governor in Council may make an order stating that opinion and, if
such an order is made, regulations made under this Part relating to the
same aspect do not apply to the federal work or undertaking, the federal
land or the aboriginal land.

### [Information about Works and Activities]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65794 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister may require information

- **[[211]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-211 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For
  the purpose of making regulations, the Minister may require
  information from any person who carries on, or proposes to carry on, a
  federal work or undertaking or an activity on federal land or
  aboriginal land.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Kind of information

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The required information shall be information that
  will enable the Minister to determine any environmental effects that
  the work, undertaking or activity may have, and it may include

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} plans, specifications, studies, procedures,
    schedules, analyses, samples or other information relating to the
    work, undertaking or activity; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} analyses, samples, evaluations, studies or other
    information relating to the environment that is or is likely to be
    affected by the work, undertaking or activity.

### [Release of Substances]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65802 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report and remedial measures

- **[[212]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-212 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  substance is released into the environment in contravention of a
  regulation, or if there is a likelihood of such a release, a person
  described in subsection (2) shall, as soon as possible in the
  circumstances,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} subject to the regulations, notify an enforcement
    officer or any other person designated pursuant to the regulations
    and provide a written report on the matter to the enforcement
    officer or other person;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} take all reasonable measures consistent with the
    protection of the environment and public safety to prevent or
    eliminate any dangerous condition or minimize any danger to the
    environment or to human life or health that results from the release
    or may reasonably be expected to result if the substance is
    released; and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} make a reasonable effort to notify any members of
    the public who may be adversely affected by the release or likely
    release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Application of subsection (1)

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Subsection (1) applies to any person who

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} owns or has the charge, management or control of
    the substance immediately before its release or its likely release
    into the environment; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} causes or contributes to the release or increases
    the likelihood of the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Report by other persons

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A person, other than a person described in subsection
  (2), shall, as soon as possible in the circumstances, report a release
  of a substance to an enforcement officer or to a person designated by
  the regulations if their property is affected by the release and they
  know that the substance has been released in contravention of a
  regulation.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Intervention by enforcement officer

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where measures required by subsection (1) are not
  taken, an enforcement officer may take them, have them taken or direct
  a person described in subsection (2) to take them.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation on direction

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} If the direction to the person described in
  subsection (2) is inconsistent with a requirement imposed by or under
  any other Act of Parliament, it is void to the extent of the
  inconsistency.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Access to property

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer or other person authorized or
  required to take measures under subsection (1) or (4) may, for the
  purpose of taking those measures, enter and have access to any place
  or property and may do anything reasonable that may be necessary in
  the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Personal liability

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} A person who provides assistance or advice in taking
  the measures required by subsection (1), or who takes any measures
  authorized under subsection (4), is not personally liable either
  civilly or criminally for any act or omission in the course of
  providing assistance or advice or taking any measures under those
  subsections, unless it is established that the person acted in bad
  faith.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Voluntary report

- **[[213]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-213 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} If a
  person knows about a release or likely release of a substance into the
  environment in contravention of a regulation but the person is not
  required to report the matter under this Act, the person may report
  any information about the release or likely release to an enforcement
  officer or to a person designated by the regulations.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Request for confidentiality

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The person making the report may request that their
  identity and any information that could reasonably reveal their
  identity not be released.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Requirement of confidentiality

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} No person shall disclose, or have disclosed, the
  identity of the person making the request or any information that
  could reasonably be expected to reveal their identity unless the
  person making the request authorizes the disclosure in writing.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Employee protection

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Despite any other Act of Parliament, no employer
  shall dismiss, suspend, demote, discipline, harass or otherwise
  disadvantage an employee, or deny an employee a benefit of employment,
  by reason that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the employee has made a report under subsection
    (1);

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has refused or stated an intention of refusing
    to do anything that is an offence under this Act; or

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} the employee, acting in good faith and on the basis
    of reasonable belief, has done or stated an intention of doing
    anything that is required to be done by or under this Act.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recovery of costs and expenses

- **[[214]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-214 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  costs and expenses related to taking any measures under subsection
  212(4) may be recovered by Her Majesty in right of Canada from

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 212(2)(a); and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} any person referred to in paragraph 212(2)(b) to
    the extent of their negligence or wilful conduct in causing or
    contributing to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Reasonably incurred

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} The costs and expenses may only be recovered to the
  extent that they have been reasonably incurred in the circumstances.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Liability

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Persons from whom the costs and expenses may be
  recovered are jointly and severally liable or solidarily liable for
  them. However, a person mentioned in paragraph 212(2)(b) is not liable
  to an extent greater than the extent of their negligence or wilful
  conduct in causing or contributing to the release.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Recourse or indemnity

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} This section does not limit or restrict any right of
  recourse or indemnity that a person may have against any other person.

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Court and costs

- **[[215]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-215 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} A
  claim under section 214 may be recovered with costs in any court of
  competent jurisdiction.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Limitation period

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Where events giving rise to the claim occur, no
  proceedings in respect of the claim may be instituted more than five
  years after the date on which the events occur or the Minister becomes
  aware of them, whichever is later.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Minister's certificate

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} A document purporting to have been issued by the
  Minister certifying the day on which the events giving rise to a claim
  under section 214 came to the knowledge of the Minister shall be
  received in evidence and, in the absence of any evidence to the
  contrary, the document shall be considered as proof of that fact
  without proof of the signature or of the official character of the
  person appearing to have signed the document and without further
  proof.

## [PART 10]{.HLabel1}[Enforcement]{.HTitleText1} {#h-65853 .Part}

### [Interpretation]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65854 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Definitions

**[[216]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216 .sectionLabel}** The definitions in this
section apply in this Part.

[[conveyance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[conveyance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any vehicle, ship or
    aircraft. ([moyen de transport]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[foreign national]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[foreign national]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} has the same meaning as in
    subsection 2(1) of the [Immigration and Refugee Protection
    Act](/eng/acts/I-2.5). ([étranger]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

[[place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[place]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes any platform anchored at sea,
    shipping container or conveyance. ([lieu]{.DefinedTermLink
    lang="fr"})

[[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

:   [[substance]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} includes hazardous wastes,
    hazardous recyclable material or non-hazardous waste, as those
    expressions are defined by regulations made under section 191, and
    waste or other matter listed in Schedule
    5. ([substance]{.DefinedTermLink lang="fr"})

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 216
- 2005, c. 23, s. 28
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 216"){.PITLink}
:::

Definition of [[ship that is not a Canadian ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

- **[[216.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-216.1
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsections
  217(6), 218(16) and (17) and 220(5.1) and section 275.1, a [[ship that
  is not a Canadian ship]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} does not include a ship
  that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} that is not registered, listed or licensed under
    the [Canada Shipping Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15) or under the laws
    of another state but is owned by one or more persons each of whom is

    - [(i)]{.lawlabel} a Canadian citizen,

    - [(ii)]{.lawlabel} in the case of a vessel that is not required to
      be registered, listed or licensed under that Act, a person who is
      resident and domiciled in Canada, or

    - [(iii)]{.lawlabel} a corporation incorporated under the laws of
      Canada or a province, that has its principal place of business in
      Canada; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} that is not registered, listed or licensed under
    the [Canada Shipping Act, 2001](/eng/acts/C-10.15) but is owned or
    operated by Her Majesty in right of Canada.

- Definition of [[committed in the course of enforcement of this
  Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm}

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} For the purpose of subsections 217(4) and 271.1(2),
  sections 275.1 and 278.1 and subsections 279(1) and (3), [[committed
  in the course of enforcement of this Act]{.dfn}]{.DefinedTerm} means
  committed by or against a person who is engaged in carrying out duties
  or functions, or exercising a power, under this Act or a person acting
  under their direction and control.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2005, c. 23, ss. 29, 51
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-216.1-20050628.html "Link to previous version of section 216.1"){.PITLink}
:::

### [Designation of Enforcement Officers and Analysts]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65875 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Designation

- **[[217]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217 .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} The
  Minister may designate as enforcement officers or analysts for the
  purposes of this Act, or any provision of this Act,

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} persons or classes of persons who, in the
    Minister's opinion, are qualified to be so designated; and

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} with the approval of a government, persons or
    classes of persons employed by the government in the administration
    of a law respecting the protection of the environment.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Production of certificate of
  designation

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} Every enforcement officer or analyst shall be
  furnished with a certificate of designation as an enforcement officer
  or analyst, as the case may be, and on entering any place under
  section 218 or 220, as the case may be, shall, if so requested,
  produce the certificate to the person in charge of the place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers of enforcement officers

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act and the regulations,
  enforcement officers have all the powers of a peace officer, but the
  Minister may specify limits on those powers when designating any
  person or class of persons.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers in exclusive economic zone

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Every power --- including arrest, entry, search and
  seizure --- that may be exercised in Canada in respect of an offence
  under this Act or the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) may, in respect
  of an offence arising out of a contravention of Division 3 of Part 7
  or of any regulation made under that Division, or in respect of an
  offence under the [Criminal Code](/eng/acts/C-46) that is committed in
  the course of enforcement of this Act, be exercised in an area of the
  sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(c) if the offence was committed in
  that area of the sea.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Exercising powers in cases of hot
  pursuit

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The powers referred to in subsection (4) may be
  exercised in an area of the sea referred to in paragraph 122(2)(g) if
  hot pursuit has been commenced in Canada or in an area of the sea
  referred to in any of paragraphs 122(2)(a) to (e) and (g).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}When consent of Attorney General of
  Canada required

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} The powers referred to in subsection (4) may not be
  exercised under that subsection or subsection (5) in relation to a
  ship that is not a Canadian ship, or to a foreign national who is on
  board such a ship, without the consent of the Attorney General of
  Canada.

::: HistoricalNote
- 1999, c. 33, s. 217
- 2005, c. 23, s. 30
:::

::: PITLink
[Previous
Version](section-217-20021231.html "Link to previous version of section 217"){.PITLink}
:::

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Immunity

**[[217.1]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-217.1 .sectionLabel}** Enforcement
officers and analysts are not personally liable for anything they do or
omit to do in good faith while carrying out duties or exercising powers
under this Act, including any failure to exercise a discretionary
authority.

::: HistoricalNote
- 2009, c. 14, s. 53
:::

### [Inspection]{.HTitleText2} {#h-65897 .Subheading}

[Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Inspection

- **[[218]{.sectionLabel}]{#s-218
  .sectionLabel}** [(1)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), for the
  purposes of this Act and the regulations, an enforcement officer may,
  at any reasonable time, enter and inspect any place if the enforcement
  officer has reasonable grounds to believe that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} there can be found in the place a substance with
    respect to which this Act applies, a product that contains such a
    substance or a product that may release such a substance into the
    environment;

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} fuels to which this Act applies are being or have
    been produced or blended, or can be found, in the place;

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} a cleaning product or water conditioner, as defined
    in section 116, is being or has been produced or can be found in the
    place;

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} regulations made under section 209 apply to or in
    respect of the place;

  - [(e)]{.lawlabel} the place is a source in respect of which
    regulations have been made under section 167 or 177 or a place in
    respect of which regulations have been made under section 200;

  - [(f)]{.lawlabel} a substance is being loaded for the purpose of
    disposal at sea or is being disposed of at sea;

  - [(g)]{.lawlabel} any vehicle, engine or equipment of a class for
    which standards for emissions have been prescribed that is owned by
    or is on the premises of a company or a consignee of imported
    vehicles or engines or imported equipment can be found in the place;

  - [(h)]{.lawlabel} any component to be used in the manufacture of a
    vehicle, engine or equipment for which standards for emissions have
    been prescribed can be found in the place;

  - [(i)]{.lawlabel} any record in relation to the design, manufacture,
    testing and field performance of a vehicle, engine or equipment in
    so far as it relates to emissions can be found in the place; or

  - [(j)]{.lawlabel} any books, records, electronic data or other
    documents relevant to the administration of this Act can be found in
    the place.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Private dwelling-place

  [(2)]{.lawlabel} An enforcement officer may not enter a private
  dwelling-place or any part of a place that is designed to be used and
  is being used as a permanent or temporary private dwelling-place
  except

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} with the consent of the occupant of the place; or

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} under the authority of a warrant issued under
    subsection (3).

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrant for inspection of
  dwelling-place

  [(3)]{.lawlabel} Where on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application
  a justice is satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions for entry described in
    subsection (1) exist in relation to a private dwelling-place,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry to the dwelling-place is necessary for any
    purpose relating to the administration of this Act, and

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry to the dwelling-place has been refused or
    there are reasonable grounds for believing that entry will be
    refused,

  the justice may issue a warrant authorizing the enforcement officer
  named in it to conduct an inspection of the dwelling-place subject to
  any conditions that may be specified in the warrant, and authorizing
  any other person named therein to accompany the enforcement officer
  and exercise any power specified in the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Warrants for inspection of
  non-dwellings

  [(4)]{.lawlabel} Where on [ex parte]{.otherLang lang="la"} application
  a justice is satisfied by information on oath that

  - [(a)]{.lawlabel} the conditions for entry described in
    subsection (1) exist in relation to a place other than a private
    dwelling-place,

  - [(b)]{.lawlabel} entry to that place is necessary for any purpose
    relating to the administration of this Act,

  - [(c)]{.lawlabel} entry to that place has been refused, the
    enforcement officer was not able to enter without the use of force
    or the place was abandoned, and

  - [(d)]{.lawlabel} subject to subsection (5), all reasonable attempts
    were made to notify the owner, operator or person in charge of the
    place,

  the justice may issue a warrant authorizing the enforcement officer
  named in it to conduct an inspection of the place, subject to any
  conditions that may be specified in the warrant, and authorizing any
  other person named therein to accompany the enforcement officer and
  exercise any power specified in the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Waiving notice

  [(5)]{.lawlabel} The justice may waive the requirement to give notice
  referred to in subsection (4) where the justice is satisfied that
  attempts to give the notice would be unsuccessful because the owner,
  operator or person in charge is absent from the jurisdiction of the
  justice or that it is not in the public interest to give the notice.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Use of force

  [(6)]{.lawlabel} In executing a warrant issued under subsection (3) or
  (4), an enforcement officer shall not use force unless the use of
  force has been specifically authorized in the warrant.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Stopping and detaining conveyances

  [(7)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act and the regulations, an
  enforcement officer may, at any reasonable time, direct that any
  conveyance be stopped --- or be moved by the route and in the manner
  that the officer may specify, to a place specified by the officer
  where an inspection can be carried out --- and the officer may, for a
  reasonable time, detain any conveyance, platform or other structure.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Moving and detaining shipping
  containers

  [(7.1)]{.lawlabel} For the purposes of this Act and the regulations,
  an enforcement officer may, at any reasonable time, direct that any
  shipping container be moved to a place specified by the officer and
  the officer may, for a reasonable time, detain the container.

- [Marginal note:]{.wb-invisible}Powers in relation to ships, etc.

  [(8)]{.lawlabel} Subject to subsection (2), for the purposes of this
  Act and the regulations, an enforcement officer may, at any reasonable
  time,
